In the next lemma we summarize some simple properties of the set
\ell^{w}_{p}(E)
.

Lemma 4.6

Let
p,q\in[1,\infty]
, and let
(E,\tau)
be a locally convex space. Then:

(i)

\ell^{w}_{p}(E)
is a vector space and every
(x_{n})\in\ell^{w}_{p}(E)
is weakly null in
E
;

(ii)

if
p<q
, then
\ell^{w}_{p}(E)\subsetneq\ell^{w}_{q}(E)
;

(iii)

if
T:E\to L
is an operator into an lcs
L
and
(x_{n})_{n\in\omega}\in\ell_{p}^{w}(E)

(
resp.,
(x_{n})_{n\in\omega}\in\ell_{p}^{w}(E_{\beta})
)
, then
\big(T(x_{n})\big)_{n\in\omega}\in\ell_{p}^{w}(L)

(
resp.,
(x_{n})_{n\in\omega}\in\ell_{p}^{w}(L_{\beta})
)
; in particular,
\ell_{p}^{w}(E_{\beta})\subseteq\ell_{p}^{w}(E)
;

(iv)

if
\mathcal{T}
is a locally convex vector topology on
E
compatible with the topology
\tau
of
E
, then
\ell^{w}_{p}(E)=\ell^{w}_{p}(E,\mathcal{T})
; consequently, also the notions of being a weakly
p
-convergent sequence or a weakly
p
-Cauchy sequence depend only on the duality
(E,E^{\prime})
;

(v)

\ell_{p}^{w}(E^{\prime}_{\beta})\subseteq\ell_{p}^{w}(E^{\prime}_{w^{\ast}})
and the equality holds for semi-reflexive spaces;

(vi)

if
H
is a subspace of
E
, then
\ell^{w}_{p}(H)\subseteq\ell^{w}_{p}(E)
; more precisely,
\ell^{w}_{p}(H)=\ell^{w}_{p}(E)\cap H^{\omega}
.

Proof 18

(i), (iv) and (v) are evident, and (vi) follows from the Hahn–Banach extension theorem.

(ii) The inclusion
\ell^{w}_{p}(E)\subseteq\ell^{w}_{q}(E)
is clear. To show that
\ell^{w}_{p}(E)\not=\ell^{w}_{q}(E)
, fix
p<t<q
and a nonzero element
x\in E
. For every
n\in\omega
, define
x_{n}:=\tfrac{1}{(n+1)^{1/t}}\cdot x
. Then
(x_{n})_{n\in\omega}\in\ell_{q}^{w}(E){\setminus}\ell_{p}^{w}(E)
.

(iii) Let
\eta\in L^{\prime}
. Then
\chi:=\eta\circ T\in E^{\prime}
and
(\langle\eta,T(x_{n})\rangle)_{n\in\omega}=(\langle\chi,x_{n}\rangle)_{n\in\omega}
belongs to
\ell_{p}
(or to
c_{0}
, if
p=\infty
). Thus
\big(T(x_{n})\big)_{n\in\omega}\in\ell_{p}^{w}(L)
. The case when
(x_{n})_{n\in\omega}\in\ell_{p}^{w}(E_{\beta})
follows from the proved one because, by Theorem 8.11.3 of
[
67
]
, the map
T:E_{\beta}\to L_{\beta}
is continuous.
Since the topology
\tau
of
E_{\beta}
is finer than the topology of
E
, we have
E^{\prime}\subseteq(E_{\beta})^{\prime}
. Thus
\ell_{p}^{w}(E_{\beta})\subseteq\ell_{p}^{w}(E)
.∎

Note that the inclusion
\ell_{p}^{w}(E^{\prime}_{\beta})\subseteq\ell_{p}^{w}(E^{\prime}_{w^{\ast}})
can be strict, see Example
5.4
.

For the case
p=1
, the proof of the next lemma is taken from §16.5 of
[
52
]
.

Lemma 4.7

Let
E
be a locally convex space, and let
U\in\mathcal{N}_{0}^{c}(E)
. Then for every
1\leq p<\infty
, the function

\rho_{U}:\ell_{p}^{w}(E)\to\mathbb{R},\quad(x_{n})\mapsto\sup_{\chi\in U^{\circ}}\big\|\big(\langle\chi,x_{n}\rangle\big)_{n}\big\|_{\ell_{p}},

is a seminorm on
\ell_{p}^{w}(E)
, and if
p=\infty
, then the function

\rho_{U}:c_{0}^{w}(E)\to\mathbb{R},\quad(x_{n})\mapsto\sup_{\chi\in U^{\circ}}\big\|\big(\langle\chi,x_{n}\rangle\big)_{n}\big\|_{c_{0}},

is a seminorm on
c_{0}^{w}(E)
.

Proof 19

It suffices to show that the function
\rho_{U}
is well-defined, then the seminorm conditions follow from the corresponding properties of the norm of
\ell_{p}
or
c_{0}
. Fix
(x_{i})\in\ell_{p}^{w}(E)
(or
\in c_{0}^{w}(E)
if
p=\infty
). We distinguish between three cases.

Case 1. Let
p=1
. Set

A_{1}:=\Big\{\sum_{i=0}^{n}\alpha_{i}x_{i}:n\in\omega\mbox{ and }(\alpha_{i})\in B_{\ell_{\infty}}\Big\}.

Then for each
\chi\in E^{\prime}
and
\sum_{i=0}^{n}\alpha_{i}x_{i}\in A_{1}
, we have

\Big|\langle\chi,\sum_{i=0}^{n}\alpha_{i}x_{i}\rangle\Big|\leq\sum_{i=0}^{n}|\alpha_{i}|\cdot|\langle\chi,x_{i}\rangle|\leq\big\|\big(\langle\chi,x_{i}\rangle\big)\big\|_{\ell_{1}}

which means that
A_{1}
is bounded. Choose
\lambda>0
such that
A_{1}\subseteq\lambda U
. Given
\chi\in U^{\circ}
, for every
i\in\omega
choose
\alpha_{i}\in\mathbb{F}
such that
\alpha_{i}\langle\chi,x_{i}\rangle=|\langle\chi,x_{i}\rangle|
. Then
(\alpha_{i})\in B_{\ell_{\infty}}
and

\sum_{i=0}^{n}\big|\langle\chi,x_{i}\rangle\big|=\Big|\langle\chi,\sum_{i=0}^{n}\alpha_{i}x_{i}\rangle\Big|\leq\lambda.

Therefore
\sup_{\chi\in U^{\circ}}\big\|\big(\langle\chi,x_{n}\rangle\big)_{n}\big\|_{\ell_{p}}
is finite, and hence
\rho_{U}
is well-defined.

Case 2. Let
1<p<\infty
. Set

A_{p}:=\Big\{\sum_{i=0}^{n}\alpha_{i}x_{i}:n\in\omega\mbox{ and }(\alpha_{i})\in B_{\ell_{p^{\ast}}}\Big\}\subseteq E.

We claim that
A_{p}
is bounded. Indeed, let
\chi\in E^{\prime}
and
\sum_{i=0}^{n}\alpha_{i}x_{i}\in A_{p}
. Then the Hölder inequality implies

\Big|\langle\chi,\sum_{i=0}^{n}\alpha_{i}x_{i}\rangle\Big|\leq\sum_{i=0}^{n}|\alpha_{i}|\cdot|\langle\chi,x_{i}\rangle|\leq\Big(\sum_{i=0}^{n}|\alpha_{i}|^{p^{\ast}}\Big)^{\tfrac{1}{p^{\ast}}}\cdot\Big(\sum_{i=0}^{n}|\langle\chi,x_{i}\rangle|^{p}\Big)^{\tfrac{1}{p}}\leq\big\|\big(\langle\chi,x_{i}\rangle\big)_{i}\big\|_{\ell_{p}}.

As
\chi
was arbitrary, it follows that
A_{p}
is bounded.
Therefore there is
\lambda>0
such that
A_{p}\subseteq\lambda U
.

To show that
\rho_{U}
is well-defined we prove that

\big\|\big(\langle\chi,x_{i}\rangle\big)_{i}\big\|_{\ell_{p}}\leq\lambda\;\;\mbox{ for every $\chi\in U^{\circ}$}.

(4.2)

To this end, fix an arbitrary
\chi\in U^{\circ}
such that
\big\|\big(\langle\chi,x_{i}\rangle\big)_{i}\big\|_{\ell_{p}}>0
.
Set
C(\chi):=\big\|\big(\langle\chi,x_{i}\rangle\big)_{i}\big\|_{\ell_{p}}^{p/p^{\ast}}>0
. Denote by
I:=\{i\in\omega:\langle\chi,x_{i}\rangle\not=0\}
the “support” of
\chi
on
(x_{i})
. For every
i\in\omega
, define
\alpha_{i}\in\mathbb{F}
by

\alpha_{i}:=0\;\mbox{ if }\;i\not\in I,\;\;\mbox{ and }\;\;\alpha_{i}:=\tfrac{\overline{\langle\chi,x_{i}\rangle}}{|\langle\chi,x_{i}\rangle|}\cdot\tfrac{|\langle\chi,x_{i}\rangle|^{p-1}}{C(\chi)}\;\mbox{ if }\;i\in I.

Then the equality
(p-1)p^{\ast}=p
implies

\sum_{i\in\omega}|\alpha_{i}|^{p^{\ast}}=\sum_{i\in I}\tfrac{|\langle\chi,x_{i}\rangle|^{(p-1)p^{\ast}}}{C(\chi)^{p^{\ast}}}=\tfrac{1}{\big\|\big(\langle\chi,x_{i}\rangle\big)_{i}\big\|_{\ell_{p}}^{p}}\cdot\sum_{i\in I}|\langle\chi,x_{i}\rangle|^{p}=1,

which means that
(\alpha_{i})\in B_{\ell_{p^{\ast}}}
. Therefore, for every
n\in\omega
, the inclusion
A_{p}\subseteq\lambda U
implies

\displaystyle\tfrac{1}{C(\chi)}\sum_{i\leq n}|\langle\chi,x_{i}\rangle|^{p}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i\leq n,\;n\in I}\tfrac{\overline{\langle\chi,x_{i}\rangle}}{|\langle\chi,x_{i}\rangle|}\cdot\tfrac{|\langle\chi,x_{i}\rangle|^{p-1}}{C(\chi)}\cdot\langle\chi,x_{i}\rangle

\displaystyle=\Big|\sum_{i\leq n}\langle\chi,\alpha_{i}x_{i}\rangle\Big|=\Big|\big\langle\chi,\sum_{i\leq n}\alpha_{i}x_{i}\big\rangle\Big|\leq\lambda,

and hence

\sum_{i\leq n}|\langle\chi,x_{i}\rangle|^{p}\leq\lambda\cdot C(\chi)=\lambda\cdot\left(\sum_{i\in\omega}|\langle\chi,x_{i}\rangle|^{p}\right)^{1/p^{\ast}}\Longleftrightarrow\left(\sum_{i\in\omega}|\langle\chi,x_{i}\rangle|^{p}\right)^{1-\tfrac{1}{p^{\ast}}}\leq\lambda.

Thus (
4.2
) is proved.

Case 3. Let
p=\infty
. Since
S=\{x_{i}\}_{i\in\omega}
is a bounded subset of
E
, there is
\lambda>0
such that
S\subseteq\lambda U
. Then for every
i\in\omega
and each
\chi\in U^{\circ}
, we have
|\langle\chi,x_{i}\rangle|=\lambda\cdot|\langle\chi,\tfrac{1}{\lambda}\cdot x_{i}\rangle|\leq\lambda
. Thus
\rho_{U}
is well-defined.∎

As an immediate corollary of Lemma
4.7
we obtain the next result.

Proposition 4.8

Let
p\in[1,\infty]
, and let
E
be a locally convex space. Then the family
\{\rho_{U}:U\in\mathcal{N}_{0}^{c}(E)\}
defines a locally convex vector topology
\mathcal{T}_{p}
on the vector space
\ell_{p}^{w}(E)

(
or on
c_{0}^{w}(E)
if
p=\infty
)
.

Notation 4.9

Following notations of § 19.4 of
[
52
]
, we set
\ell_{p}[E]:=\big(\ell_{p}^{w}(E),\mathcal{T}_{p}\big)
if
1\leq p<\infty
and
c_{0}[E]:=\big(c_{0}^{w}(E),\mathcal{T}_{\infty}\big)
if
p=\infty
.∎

The next easy assertion shows that
E
can be considered as a direct summand of the spaces
\ell_{p}[E]
and
c_{0}[E]
.

Proposition 4.10

For a locally convex space
E
and
p\in[1,\infty]
, the map
I:E\to\ell_{p}[E]

(
or
I:E\to c_{0}[E]
if
p=\infty
)
defined by
x\mapsto(x,0,\dots)
is an embedding onto a direct summand.

Proof 20

We consider only the case
1\leq p<\infty
, the case
p=\infty
is considered analogously. Consider the subspace
{\tilde{E}}:=\{(x_{i})\in\ell_{p}[E]:(x_{i})=(x_{0},0,\dots)\}
. Since the projection
P:\ell_{p}[E]\to\ell_{p}[E]
onto the first coordinate defined by
P(x_{i}):=(x_{0},0\dots)
is the converse to the identity embedding of
{\tilde{E}}
, we see that
{\tilde{E}}
is a direct summand of
\ell_{p}[E]
. It is clear that
I
is a linear isomorphism of
E
onto
{\tilde{E}}
. If
U\in\mathcal{N}_{0}^{c}(E)
, then
I(U)={\tilde{E}}\cap\{(x_{i})\in\ell_{p}[E]:\rho_{U}(x)\leq 1\}
. Therefore
I
is a homeomorphism. ∎

Proposition 19.4.2 of
[
52
]
states that if
E
is complete, then the spaces
\ell_{p}[E]
and
c_{0}[E]
are complete as well. Below we extend this assertion.

Proposition 4.11

Let
p\in[1,\infty]
, and let
E
be a locally convex space. Then
E
is complete
(
resp., quasi-complete, separably quasi-complete, or sequentially complete
)
if and only if so is
\ell_{p}[E]

(
or
c_{0}[E]
if
p=\infty
)
.

Proof 21

The condition is sufficient because, by Proposition
4.10
,
E
is a direct summand of
\ell_{p}[E]
(or of
c_{0}[E]
if
p=\infty
). To prove the necessity, assume that
E
is complete (resp., quasi-complete, separably quasi-complete, or sequentially complete). We consider only the case
p<\infty
since the case
p=\infty
can be proved analogously.
For every
n\in\omega
, let
P_{n}
be the projection of
\ell_{p}[E]
onto the
n
th coordinate. As in the proof of Proposition
4.10
, we obtain that
P_{n}(\ell_{p}[E])
is topologically isomorphic to
E
.

Fix a Cauchy net
\big\{(x_{n,\alpha})_{n\in\omega}\big\}_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}
in
\ell_{p}[E]
(resp., in a closed bounded subset
A
of
\ell_{p}[E]
, a closed bounded and separable subset
A
of
\ell_{p}[E]
, or a Cauchy sequence for
\mathcal{A}=\omega
). For every
n\in\omega
, the projection
\big\{x_{n,\alpha}\big\}_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}
of the net
\big\{(x_{n,\alpha})_{n\in\omega}\big\}_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}
onto the
n
th coordinate is Cauchy in
P_{n}(\ell_{p}[E])
(resp., in the closure
B
of the bounded subset
P_{n}(A)
of
P_{n}(\ell_{p}[E])
, in the closure
B
of the bounded and separable subset
P_{n}(A)
of
P_{n}(\ell_{p}[E])
, or a Cauchy sequence in
P_{n}(\ell_{p}[E])
for
\mathcal{A}=\omega
). Therefore,
\big\{x_{n,\alpha}\big\}_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}
has a limit point
z_{n}
. Set
z:=(z_{n})
.

We claim that
z\in\ell_{p}^{w}(E)
. Indeed, fix an arbitrary
\chi\in E^{\prime}
. Choose
V\in\mathcal{N}_{0}^{c}(E)
such that
\chi\in V^{\circ}
. Then

\sum_{n\in\omega}|\langle\chi,x_{n}\rangle|^{p}\leq 1\;\;\mbox{ if }\;\;\rho_{V}\big((x_{n})_{n\in\omega}\big)\leq 1,

which means that the map
\ell_{p}[E]\to\ell_{p}
,
(x_{n})\mapsto\big(\langle\chi,x_{n}\rangle\big)_{n}
, is a well-defined operator. Therefore
\big\{\big(\langle\chi,x_{n,\alpha}\rangle\big)_{n\in\omega}\big\}_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}
is a Cauchy net (or a Cauchy sequence if
\mathcal{A}=\omega
) in
\ell_{p}
and hence converges to some
(a_{n})\in\ell_{p}
. It is clear that
a_{n}=\langle\chi,z_{n}\rangle
for every
n\in\omega
. Thus
z\in\ell_{p}^{w}(E)
.

Taking into account that
A
is closed, it remains to show that
(x_{n,\alpha})_{n\in\omega}\to z
. To this end, fix
U\in\mathcal{N}_{0}^{c}(E)
and
\delta>0
. Choose
\gamma\in\mathcal{A}
such that

\sum_{n\in\omega}|\langle\chi,x_{n,\alpha}-x_{n,\beta}\rangle|^{p}\leq\delta^{p}\;\;\mbox{ for all }\;\chi\in U^{\circ}\;\mbox{ and }\;\alpha,\beta\geq\gamma.

Passing to the limit by
\beta
in the above inequality, we obtain
\sum_{n\in\omega}|\langle\chi,x_{n,\alpha}-z_{n}\rangle|^{p}\leq\delta^{p}
which means that
\rho_{U}\big((x_{n,\alpha})_{n\in\omega},(z_{n})_{n\in\omega}\big)\leq\delta
for every
\alpha\geq\gamma
. Thus
z
is the limit of
\big\{(x_{n,\alpha})_{n\in\omega}\big\}_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}
in
\ell_{p}[E]
.∎

Recall that an lcs
E
has a
\mathfrak{G}
-base
or an
\omega^{\omega}
-base
if it has a base
\{U_{\alpha}:\alpha\in\omega^{\omega}\}
of neighborhoods at zero such that
U_{\beta}\subseteq U_{\alpha}
whenever
\alpha\leq\beta
for all
\alpha,\beta\in\omega^{\omega}
, where
\alpha=(\alpha(n))_{n\in\omega}\leq\beta=(\beta(n))_{n\in\omega}
if
\alpha(n)\leq\beta(n)
for all
n\in\omega
. Each metrizable or strict
(LF)
space has a
\mathfrak{G}
-base.

Corollary 4.12

Let
p\in[1,\infty]
, and let
E
be a locally convex space. Then:

(i)

E
is a Banach
(
resp., normed, metrizable or Fréchet
)
space if and only if so is
\ell_{p}[E]

(
or
c_{0}[E]
if
p=\infty
)
;

(ii)

E
has a
\mathfrak{G}
-base if and only if
\ell_{p}[E]

(
or
c_{0}[E]
if
p=\infty
)
has a
\mathfrak{G}
-base.

Proof 22

Observe that if
U,V\in\mathcal{N}_{0}^{c}(E)
are such that
U\subseteq V
, then
\rho_{U}\geq\rho_{V}
. Now the corollary immediately follows from Propositions
4.8
,
4.10
and
4.11
.∎

We shall use repeatedly the next observation.

Lemma 4.13

Let
p\in[1,\infty]
, and let
E
be a locally convex space. A sequence
\{\chi_{n}\}_{n\in\omega}
in
E^{\prime}
is weakly
p
-summable in
E^{\prime}_{\beta}

(
or in
E^{\prime}_{w^{\ast}}
)
and finite-dimensional if and only if there are linearly independent elements
\eta_{1},\dots,\eta_{s}\in E^{\prime}
and sequences
(a_{1,n}),\dots,(a_{s,n})\in\ell_{p}

(
or
\in c_{0}
if
p=\infty
)
such that

\chi_{n}=a_{1,n}\eta_{1}+\cdots+a_{s,n}\eta_{s}\;\;\mbox{ for every $n\in\omega$}.

Proof 23

The sufficiency is clear. To prove the necessity, let
\eta_{1},\dots,\eta_{s}\in E^{\prime}
be a basis of
\mathsf{span}\big(\{\chi_{n}\}_{n\in\omega}\big)
. Then for every
n\in\omega
, there is a unique representation
\chi_{n}=a_{1,n}\eta_{1}+\cdots+a_{s,n}\eta_{s}
. Let
\xi_{i}\in E^{\prime\prime}
(resp.,
\in E
) be such that
\langle\xi_{i},\eta_{i}\rangle=1
and
\langle\xi_{i},\eta_{j}\rangle=0
for every
j\not=i
. Since
\{\chi_{n}\}_{n\in\omega}
is weakly
p
-summable, we have
\big(\langle\xi_{i},\chi_{n}\rangle\big)_{n}=(a_{i,n})_{n}\in\ell_{p}
(or
\in c_{0}
if
p=\infty
) for every
i=1,\dots,s
.∎

Let
E
be a locally convex space, and let
L
be a normed space. In the next proposition we consider the topology on
\mathcal{L}(L,E)
whose base of neighborhoods at zero operator is the family

\big\{[B_{L};U]:U\in\mathcal{N}_{0}^{c}(E)\big\},\quad\mbox{ where }\;[B_{L};U]:=\{T\in\mathcal{L}(L,E):T(B_{L})\subseteq U\}.

Proposition 4.14

Let
p\in[1,\infty)
, and let
E
be a locally convex space. Then the map

R_{p}:\mathcal{L}(\ell_{p^{\ast}}^{0},E)\to\ell_{p}^{w}(E)\;\;\mbox{ $($or $R_{1}:\mathcal{L}(c_{0}^{0},E)\to\ell_{1}^{w}(E)$ if $p=1$$)$}

defined by

R_{p}(T):=\big(T(e_{n}^{\ast})\big)_{n\in\omega},

is a linear homeomorphism. If in addition
E
is sequentially complete, then the same map

R_{p}:\mathcal{L}(\ell_{p^{\ast}},E)\to\ell_{p}^{w}(E),\;\;(\mbox{or $R_{1}:\mathcal{L}(c_{0})\to\ell_{1}^{w}(E)$ if $p=1$}),\quad R_{p}(T):=\big(T(e_{n}^{\ast})\big)_{n\in\omega}

is a linear homeomorphism as well.

Proof 24

To show that
R_{p}
is well-defined, fix an arbitrary
T\in\mathcal{L}(\ell_{p^{\ast}}^{0},E)
(or
T\in\mathcal{L}(c_{0}^{0},E)
if
p=1
). Then
R_{p}(T)=\big(T(e_{n}^{\ast})\big)
. Since
T^{\ast}\in\mathcal{L}(E^{\prime}_{\beta},\ell_{p})
, for every
\chi\in E^{\prime}
, we have

\big(\langle\chi,T(e_{n}^{\ast})\rangle\big)_{n\in\omega}=\big(\langle T^{\ast}(\chi),e_{n}^{\ast}\rangle\big)_{n\in\omega}=T^{\ast}(\chi)\in\ell_{p},

(4.3)

which means that
R_{p}(T)\in\ell_{p}^{w}(E)
, as desired. It is clear that
R_{p}
is linear and injective.

To prove that
R_{p}
is also surjective, fix an arbitrary
\big(x_{n}\big)_{n\in\omega}\in\ell_{p}^{w}(E)
. Define a linear map
T:\ell_{p^{\ast}}^{0}\to E
(or
T:c_{0}^{0}\to E
if
p=1
) by
T(e_{n}^{\ast})=x_{n}
for every
n\in\omega
. To prove that
T
is continuous it suffices to show that
T
is bounded. To this end, we prove that the set
Q:=T\big(B_{\ell^{0}_{p^{\ast}}}\big)
(or
Q:=T\big(B_{c^{0}_{0}}\big)
if
p=1
) is a bounded subset of
E
. Fix
\chi\in E^{\prime}
. If
p>1
, the Hölder inequality implies

\big|\langle\chi,T(\sum a_{i}e^{\ast}_{i})\rangle\big|=\big|\sum a_{i}\langle\chi,x_{i}\rangle\big|\leq\big(\sum|a_{i}|^{p^{\ast}}\big)^{1/p^{\ast}}\cdot\big(\sum|\langle\chi,x_{i}\rangle|^{p}\big)^{1/p}\leq\big\|\big(\langle\chi,x_{i}\rangle\big)_{i}\|_{\ell_{p}},

and if
p=1
, we obtain

\big|\langle\chi,T(\sum a_{i}e^{\ast}_{i})\rangle\big|=\big|\sum a_{i}\langle\chi,x_{i}\rangle\big|\leq\max\{|a_{i}|:i\in\omega\}\cdot\sum|\langle\chi,x_{i}\rangle|\leq\big\|\big(\langle\chi,x_{i}\rangle\big)_{i}\|_{\ell_{1}}.

In any case, we obtain that
|\langle\chi,Q\rangle|
is a bounded subset of
\mathbb{R}
. Thus
Q
is bounded, as desired.

Assume that
E
is sequentially complete. Then, by Proposition
3.7
, the operator
T
can be extended to a bounded operator from
\ell_{p^{\ast}}
(or from
c_{0}^{0}
onto
c_{0}
if
p=1
) to
E
. Thus
R_{p}
is a well-defined map from
\mathcal{L}(\ell_{p^{\ast}},E)
onto
\ell_{p}^{w}(E)
(or onto
\ell_{1}^{w}(E)
if
p=1
).

In both cases, by construction, we have
R_{p}(T)=\big(x_{n}\big)_{n\in\omega}
and hence
R_{p}
is surjective. Thus
R_{p}
is a linear isomorphism.

To show that
R_{p}
is also a homeomorphism, let
U\in\mathcal{N}_{0}^{c}(E)
. Denote by
B
the closed unit ball of
\ell_{p^{\ast}}^{0}
(resp.,
\ell_{p^{\ast}}
,
c_{0}^{0}
or
c_{0}
). Then (
4.3
) implies

\displaystyle\rho_{U}\big(R_{p}(T)\big)\leq 1

\displaystyle\Leftrightarrow\sup_{\chi\in U^{\circ}}\big\|\big(\langle\chi,R_{p}(T)\rangle\big)\big\|_{\ell_{p}}\leq 1\Leftrightarrow\sup_{\chi\in U^{\circ}}\big\|\big(\langle\chi,\big(T(e_{n}^{\ast})\big)_{n\in\omega}\rangle\big)\big\|_{\ell_{p}}\leq 1

\displaystyle\Leftrightarrow\sup_{\chi\in U^{\circ}}\|T^{\ast}(\chi)\|_{\ell_{p}}\leq 1\Leftrightarrow\sup_{\chi\in U^{\circ}}\sup_{x\in B}|\langle T^{\ast}(\chi),x\rangle|\leq 1

\displaystyle\Leftrightarrow\sup_{x\in B}\Big(\sup_{\chi\in U^{\circ}}|\langle\chi,T(x)\rangle|\Big)\leq 1\Leftrightarrow T(B)\subseteq U^{\circ}\Leftrightarrow T\in[B;U].

Thus
R_{p}
is a homeomorphism. ∎

Remark 4.15

The condition
p<\infty
in Proposition
4.14
is essential. Indeed, for
p=\infty
, let
E=\ell_{1}
and let
T:\ell_{1}^{0}\to E
be the identity map. Then
R_{\infty}(T)=(e_{n}^{\ast})
does not belong to
c_{0}^{w}(E)
(= the family of weakly null sequences in
E
) by the Schur property.∎

Below we introduce new classes of weak barrelledness conditions which will be studied in the next Section
5
and used actively in what follows.

Definition 4.16

Let
p\in[1,\infty]
. A locally convex space
E
is called

(i)

p
-barrelled if every weakly
p
-summable sequence in
E^{\prime}_{w^{\ast}}
is equicontinuous;

(ii)

p
-quasibarrelled if every weakly
p
-summable sequence in
E^{\prime}_{\beta}
is equicontinuous.∎

Let
p\in[1,\infty]
,
E
be a locally convex space, and let
T:E\to\ell_{p}
(or
T:E\to c_{0}
if
p=\infty
) be an operator. Then for every
x\in E
, we have (recall that
\{e^{\ast}_{n}\}_{n\in\omega}
is the canonical basis of
\ell_{p^{\ast}}
)

T(x)=\big(\langle e_{n}^{\ast},T(x)\rangle\big)_{n\in\omega}=\big(\langle T^{\ast}(e_{n}^{\ast}),x\rangle\big)_{n\in\omega}\in\ell_{p}\;\mbox{ (or $\in c_{0}$ if $p=\infty$)}.

For every
n\in\omega
, set
\chi_{n}:=T^{\ast}(e_{n}^{\ast})
. Therefore
T
defines a sequence
\{\chi_{n}\}
in
E^{\prime}
such that

T(x)=\big(\langle\chi_{n},x\rangle\big)_{n\in\omega}\in\ell_{p}\mbox{ (or $\in c_{0}$ if $p=\infty$) for every $x\in E$}.

Analogously to the map
R_{p}
defined in Lemma
4.6
, for any locally convex vector topology
\mathcal{T}
on
E^{\prime}
finer than
\sigma(E^{\prime},E)
, we can define a map
S_{p}^{\mathcal{T}}:\mathcal{L}(E,\ell_{p})\to\ell_{p}^{w}(E^{\prime},\mathcal{T})
(or
S_{\infty}^{\mathcal{T}}:\mathcal{L}(E,c_{0})\to\ell_{\infty}^{w}(E^{\prime},\mathcal{T})=c_{0}^{w}(E^{\prime},\mathcal{T})
if
p=\infty
) by

S_{p}^{\mathcal{T}}(T):=\big(T^{\ast}(e^{\ast}_{n})\big)_{n\in\omega}.

It is clear that
\ell_{p}^{w}(E^{\prime},\mathcal{T})
can and will be considered as a vector subspace of
\ell_{p}^{w}(E^{\prime}_{w^{\ast}})
. By this reason we shall omit the superscript
\mathcal{T}
and write simply
S_{p}
, and hence we obtain the linear map

S_{p}:\mathcal{L}(E,\ell_{p})\to\ell_{p}^{w}(E^{\prime}_{w^{\ast}})\quad\big(\mbox{or $S_{\infty}:\mathcal{L}(E,c_{0})\to\ell_{\infty}^{w}(E^{\prime}_{w^{\ast}})=c_{0}^{w}(E^{\prime}_{w^{\ast}})$ if $p=\infty$}\big)

defined by
S_{p}(T):=\big(T^{\ast}(e^{\ast}_{n})\big)_{n\in\omega}
.

Proposition 4.17

Let
p\in[1,\infty]
, and let
E
be a locally convex space. Then:

(i)

S_{p}
is a well-defined linear injection whose image contains equicontinuous sequences
(
so that the sequence
\big\{T^{\ast}(e^{\ast}_{n})\big\}_{n\in\omega}
is bounded in
E^{\prime}_{\beta}
)
; hence if
S_{p}
is surjective, then
E
is a
p
-barrelled space;

(ii)

the map
S_{\infty}
is an isomorphism if and only if
E
is
\infty
-barrelled
(=

c_{0}
-barrelled
)
;

(iii)

if
1<p<\infty
, then the image of
S_{p}
is contained in
\ell_{p}^{w}(E^{\prime}_{\beta})
; hence if
S_{p}\big(\mathcal{L}(E,\ell_{p})\big)=\ell_{p}^{w}(E^{\prime}_{\beta})
, then
E
is a
p
-quasibarrelled space.

Proof 25

(i) To show that
S_{p}
is well-defined, let
T\in\mathcal{L}(E,\ell_{p})
(or
T\in\mathcal{L}(E,c_{0})
if
p=\infty
). Then for every
x\in E=\big(E^{\prime}_{w^{\ast}}\big)^{\prime}
, we have

\big(\langle T^{\ast}(e^{\ast}_{n}),x\rangle\big)_{n\in\omega}=\big(\langle e^{\ast}_{n},T(x)\rangle)=T(x)\in\ell_{p}\;(\mbox{or $\in c_{0}$ if $p=\infty$}),

and hence
S_{p}(T)\in\ell_{p}^{w}(E^{\prime}_{w^{\ast}})
. The injectivity of
S_{p}
follows from the fact that
(e^{\ast}_{n})
is a basis of
\ell_{p^{\ast}}
(or
c_{0}
in the case
p=1
).

To show that the image of
S_{p}
contains equicontinuous sequences assume that
p<\infty
(the case
p=\infty
can be considered analogously). Fix
S_{p}(T)=(\chi_{n})\in\ell_{p}^{w}(E^{\prime}_{w^{\ast}})
, where
T\in\mathcal{L}(E,\ell_{p})
and
\chi_{n}=T^{\ast}(e^{\ast}_{n})
for every
n\in\omega
. The continuity of
T
implies that there is
U\in\mathcal{N}_{0}(E)
such that
\|T(x)\|_{\ell_{p}}\leq 1
for every
x\in U
. In particular, for every
n\in\omega
, we have

|\langle\chi_{n},x\rangle|=|\langle T^{\ast}(e^{\ast}_{n}),x\rangle|=|\langle e^{\ast}_{n},T(x)\rangle|\leq 1\;\;\mbox{ for each }\;x\in U.

Therefore
\{\chi_{n}\}_{n\in\omega}\subseteq U^{\circ}
, and hence the sequence
\{\chi_{n}\}_{n\in\omega}
is equicontinuous; it is strongly bounded by Theorem 11.3.5 of
[
67
]
.

Assume now that the map
S_{p}
is onto. Then each sequence
(\chi_{n})\in\ell_{p}^{w}(E^{\prime}_{w^{\ast}})
(or
(\chi_{n})\in c_{0}^{w}(E^{\prime}_{w^{\ast}})
if
p=\infty
) is equicontinuous. By definition this means that
E
is
p
-barrelled.

(ii) By (i) it suffices to prove that the map
S_{\infty}
is surjective if
E
is
\infty
-barrelled. To this end, let
(\chi_{n})\in c_{0}^{w}(E^{\prime}_{w^{\ast}})
. So
(\chi_{n})
is a weak
∗
null-sequence in
E^{\prime}
. Define a map
T:E\to c_{0}
by
T(x):=\big(\langle\chi_{n},x\rangle)
. Clearly,
T
is a well-defined linear map. We show that
T
is continuous. Since
E
is
\infty
-barrelled, the sequence
(\chi_{n})
is equicontinuous and hence there is
U\in\mathcal{N}_{0}(E)
such that
\chi_{n}\in U^{\circ}
for every
x\in U
and each
n\in\omega
. Therefore, for every
x\in U
, we obtain

\|T(x)\|_{c_{0}}=\sup_{n\in\omega}|\langle\chi_{n},x\rangle|\leq 1

and hence
T(U)\subseteq B_{c_{0}}
. Therefore
T
is continuous. Observe that

\langle T^{\ast}(e^{\ast}_{n}),x\rangle=\langle e^{\ast}_{n},T(x)\rangle=\langle\chi_{n},x\rangle\;\;\mbox{ for every $x\in E$ and $n\in\omega$},

which means that
T^{\ast}(e^{\ast}_{n})=\chi_{n}
and hence
S_{\infty}(T)=(\chi_{n})
, as desired.

(iii) To show that the image of
S_{p}
is contained in
\ell_{p}^{w}(E^{\prime}_{\beta})
, let
T\in\mathcal{L}(E,\ell_{p})
. Fix an arbitrary
\eta\in E^{\prime\prime}
. Then
T^{\ast\ast}(\eta)\in(\ell_{p})^{\prime\prime}=\ell_{p}
and hence

\sum_{n\in\omega}\big|\langle\eta,T^{\ast}(e^{\ast}_{n})\rangle\big|^{p}=\sum_{n\in\omega}\big|\langle T^{\ast\ast}(\eta),e^{\ast}_{n}\rangle\big|^{p}=\big\|T^{\ast\ast}(\eta)\big\|_{\ell_{p}}^{p},

therefore
S_{p}(T)\in\ell_{p}^{w}(E^{\prime}_{\beta})
.

If the image of
S_{p}
is the whole space
\ell_{p}^{w}(E^{\prime}_{\beta})
, then, by (i), each sequence
(\chi_{n})\in\ell_{p}^{w}(E^{\prime}_{\beta})
is equicontinuous. By definition this means that
E
is
p
-quasibarrelled.∎

One can naturally find some sufficient conditions on a locally convex space
E
for which the map
S_{p}:\mathcal{L}(E,\ell_{p})\to\ell_{p}^{w}(E^{\prime}_{w^{\ast}})
is surjective. It is not surprising that barrelled spaces satisfy this condition. We prove this assertion in Proposition
4.19
.

Lemma 4.18

Let
p\in[1,\infty]
, and let
E
be a locally convex space. If
\{\chi_{n}\}_{n\in\omega}
is a weakly
p
-summable sequence in
E^{\prime}_{w^{\ast}}
, then the linear map
T:E\to\ell_{p}

(
or
T:E\to c_{0}
if
p=\infty
)
defined by
T(x):=(\langle\chi_{n},x\rangle)
has closed graph.

Proof 26

Assume that a net
\big\{\big(x_{i},T(x_{i})\big)\big\}
converges to a point
\big(x,(y_{n})\big)
in
E\times\ell_{p}
(or
E\times c_{0}
if
p=\infty
). Since the coordinate projections are continuous, it follows that
x_{i}\to x
and
\langle\chi_{n},x_{i}\rangle\to y_{n}
for every
n\in\omega
. Since all
\chi_{n}
are continuous and
x_{i}\to x
, we obtain
\langle\chi_{n},x_{i}\rangle\to\langle\chi_{n},x\rangle
and hence
y_{n}=\langle\chi_{n},x\rangle
for every
n\in\omega
. Therefore
\big(x,(y_{n})\big)=\big(x,T(x)\big)
. Thus the graph of
T
is closed.∎

Proposition 4.19

Let
p\in[1,\infty]
, and let
E
be a barrelled locally convex space. Then for every weakly
p
-summable sequence
\{\chi_{n}\}_{n\in\omega}
in
E^{\prime}_{w^{\ast}}
, the linear map
T:E\to\ell_{p}

(
or
T:E\to c_{0}
if
p=\infty
)
defined by
T(x):=(\langle\chi_{n},x\rangle)
is continuous. Consequently, the linear map
S_{p}
is a bijection.

Proof 27

By Lemma
4.18
, the linear map
T
has closed graph. Therefore, by the Closed Graph Theorem
[
67
, 14.3.4]
,
T
is continuous. Thus
S_{p}
is surjective and hence, by (i) of Proposition
4.17
,
S_{p}
is bijective.∎

Corollary 4.20

Let
E
be a barrelled locally convex space, and let
\{\chi_{n}\}_{n\in\omega}\subseteq E^{\prime}_{w^{\ast}}
be a weakly
1
-summable sequence. Then for every
t=(t_{n})\in\ell_{\infty}
, the map
\eta_{t}:E\to\mathbb{F}
defined by
\langle\eta_{t},x\rangle:=\sum_{n\in\omega}t_{n}\cdot\langle\chi_{n},x\rangle
is continuous.

Proof 28

By Proposition
4.19
, the map
T:E\to\ell_{1}
defined by
T(x)=\big(\langle\chi_{n},x\rangle\big)
is continuous. It remains to note that
\langle\eta_{t},x\rangle=\langle t,T(x)\rangle
.∎

Instead of to say “weakly
p
-summable sequence in
E^{\prime}_{w^{\ast}}
” as in Lemma
4.18
and Proposition
4.19
, we introduce a more convenient notation. For Banach spaces this notion is introduced in
[
31
]
.

Definition 4.21

Let
p\in[1,\infty]
, and let
E
be a separated topological vector space.
A sequence
\{\chi_{n}\}_{n\in\omega}
in
E^{\prime}
is called

\bullet

weak
∗

p
-summable if it is weakly
p
-summable in
E^{\prime}_{w^{\ast}}
, i.e., if for every
x\in E
, it follows that
(\langle\chi_{n},x\rangle)_{n\in\omega}\in\ell_{p}
if
p<\infty
, or
(\langle\chi_{n},x\rangle)_{n\in\omega}\in c_{0}
if
p=\infty
;

\bullet

weak
∗

p
-convergent to
\chi\in E^{\prime}
if
\{\chi_{n}-\chi\}_{n\in\omega}
is weakly
p
-summable in
E^{\prime}_{w^{\ast}}
;

\bullet

weak
∗

p
-Cauchy if it is weakly
p
-Cauchy in
E^{\prime}_{w^{\ast}}
, i.e., if for each pair of strictly increasing sequences
(k_{n}),(j_{n})\subseteq\omega
, the sequence
\{\chi_{k_{n}}-\chi_{j_{n}}\}_{n\in\omega}
is weak
∗

p
-summable;

Note that
\ell_{p}^{w}(E^{\prime}_{w^{\ast}})
(or
c_{0}^{w}(E^{\prime}_{w^{\ast}})
if
p=\infty
) denotes the family of all weak
∗

p
-summable sequences in
E^{\prime}
. For simplicity of notations we shall write
\ell_{p}^{w^{\ast}}(E^{\prime}):=\ell_{p}^{w}(E^{\prime}_{w^{\ast}})
and
c_{0}^{w^{\ast}}(E^{\prime}):=c_{0}^{w}(E^{\prime}_{w^{\ast}})
. Definitions
4.2
and
4.21
immediately imply the next observation.

Proposition 4.22

Let
E
be a locally convex space. Then every weakly
p
-summable sequence in
E^{\prime}_{\beta}
is weak
∗

p
-summable. If in addition
E
is semi-reflexive, then the converse is also true.

The condition of being a semi-reflexive space in Proposition
4.22
is essential as Example
5.4
shows.

If
1\leq p<q\leq\infty
, we have the following relationships between the notions introduced in Definition
4.16
:

\textstyle{{\begin{subarray}{c}\mbox{weak${}^{\ast}$}\\
\mbox{$1$-summable}\end{subarray}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\begin{subarray}{c}\mbox{weak${}^{\ast}$}\\
\mbox{$p$-summable}\end{subarray}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\begin{subarray}{c}\mbox{weak${}^{\ast}$}\\
\mbox{$q$-summable}\end{subarray}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\begin{subarray}{c}\mbox{weak${}^{\ast}$ $\infty$-summable}\;\\
\mbox{(= weak${}^{\ast}$ null)}\end{subarray}}}

(4.4)

The case when sequences from
\ell_{p}^{w}(E^{\prime}_{w^{\ast}})
are equicontinuous is of independent interest.

Definition 4.23

Let
p\in[1,\infty]
, and let
E
be a locally convex space. Denote by
\mathcal{E}\ell_{p}^{w}(E^{\prime}_{w^{\ast}})
or
\mathcal{E}c_{0}^{w}(E^{\prime}_{w^{\ast}})
the family of all equicontinuous sequences from
\ell_{p}^{w}(E^{\prime}_{w^{\ast}})
or
c_{0}^{w}(E^{\prime}_{w^{\ast}})
.∎

We shall use the next simple assertion.

Proposition 4.24

Let
p\in[1,\infty]
, and let
E
be a locally convex space such that
E=E_{w}
. Then every equicontinuous weak
∗

p
-summable sequence
\{\chi_{n}\}_{n\in\omega}
in
E^{\prime}
is finite-dimensional.

Proof 29

Since
\{\chi_{n}\}
is equicontinuous and
E
carries its weak topology, there is a finite subset
F
of
E^{\prime}
such that
\{\chi_{n}\}_{n\in\omega}\subseteq\big(F^{\circ}\big)^{\circ}
. Then
\{\chi_{n}\}_{n\in\omega}\subseteq\mathsf{span}(F)
and hence
\{\chi_{n}\}_{n\in\omega}
is finite-dimensional.∎

The next lemma describes weakly
p
-summable sequences and weak
∗

p
-summable sequences in direct products and direct sums and their (strong) duals.

Lemma 4.25

Let
p\in[1,\infty]
, and let
\{E_{i}\}_{i\in I}
be a non-empty family of locally convex spaces. Then:

(i)

a sequence
\{\chi_{n}\}_{n\in\omega}\subseteq\big(\prod_{i\in I}E_{i}\big)^{\prime}_{\beta}
is weak
∗

p
-summable
(
resp., weakly
p
-summable
)
if and only if its support
F:=\mathrm{supp}\{\chi_{n}\}
is finite and the sequence
\{\chi_{n}(i)\}_{n\in\omega}
is weak
∗

p
-summable
(
resp., weakly
p
-summable
)
in
(E_{i})^{\prime}_{\beta}
for every
i\in F
;

(ii)

a sequence
\{x_{n}\}_{n\in\omega}\subseteq\prod_{i\in I}E_{i}
is weakly
p
-summable if and only if the sequence
\{x_{n}(i)\}_{n\in\omega}
is weakly
p
-summable in
E_{i}
for every
i\in I
;

(iii)

a sequence
\{\chi_{n}\}_{n\in\omega}\subseteq\big(\bigoplus_{i\in I}E_{i}\big)^{\prime}_{\beta}
is weak
∗

p
-summable
(
resp., weakly
p
-summable
)
if and only if the sequence
\{\chi_{n}(i)\}_{n\in\omega}
is weak
∗

p
-summable
(
resp., weakly
p
-summable
)
in
(E_{i})^{\prime}_{\beta}
for every
i\in F
;

(iv)

a sequence
\{x_{n}\}_{n\in\omega}\subseteq\bigoplus_{i\in I}E_{i}
is weakly
p
-summable if and only if its support
F:=\mathrm{supp}\{x_{n}\}
is finite and the sequence
\{x_{n}(i)\}_{n\in\omega}
is weakly
p
-summable in
E_{i}
for every
i\in F
.

Proof 30

(i) To prove the necessity we suppose for a contradiction that the support
F
is infinite. Then there is a sequence
(n_{k})
in
\omega
such that

\mathrm{supp}(\chi_{n_{k+1}}){\setminus}\bigcup_{i\leq k}\mathrm{supp}(\chi_{n_{i}})\not=\emptyset.

Therefore there is a one-to-one sequence
(j_{k})
in
I
such that

j_{0}\in\mathrm{supp}(\chi_{n_{0}})\;\;\mbox{ and }\;\;j_{k+1}\in\mathrm{supp}(\chi_{n_{k+1}}){\setminus}\bigcup_{i\leq k}\mathrm{supp}(\chi_{n_{i}}).

(4.5)

For every
k\in\omega
, by induction on
k
, choose
x_{j_{k}}\in E_{j_{k}}
such that

\big\langle\chi_{n_{k+1}}(j_{k+1}),x_{j_{k+1}}\big\rangle>k+1+\sum_{s\leq k}\big|\big\langle\chi_{n_{k+1}}(j_{s}),x_{j_{s}}\big\rangle\big|.

(4.6)

Define
\mathbf{x}=(x_{i})\in\prod_{i\in I}E_{i}
by
x_{i}=x_{j_{k}}
if
i=j_{k}
for some
k\in\omega
, and
x_{i}=0
otherwise. Then, for every
k\in\omega
, (
4.5
) and (
4.6
) imply
\langle\chi_{n_{k+1}},\mathbf{x}\rangle>k+1
and hence the sequence
\{\chi_{n}\}
is not weak
∗
(resp., weakly)
p
-summable, a contradiction. Thus
F
is finite.

If
j\in F
and
\mathbf{x}=(x_{i})\in\prod_{i\in I}E_{i}
(resp.,
\mathbf{x}=(x_{i})\in\big(\prod_{i\in I}E_{i}\big)^{\prime\prime}_{\beta}=\prod_{i\in I}(E_{i})^{\prime\prime}_{\beta}
, see Proposition
2.5
) is such that only the
j
th coordinate is non-zero, then the definition of weak
∗
(resp., weakly)
p
-summable sequences implies that the sequence
\{\chi_{n}(j)\}_{n\in\omega}
is weak
∗
(resp., weakly)
p
-summable in
(E_{j})^{\prime}_{\beta}
.

The sufficiency is trivial.

(ii) and (iii) follow from Proposition
2.5
.

(iv) The assertion is trivial if
I
is finite. If
I
is infinite, the assertion holds true by the case of finite
I
and the well known fact that every bounded subset of
\bigoplus_{i\in I}E_{i}
has finite support. ∎

5
p
-(quasi)barrelled locally convex spaces

Let
p\in[1,\infty]
. In the previous section we introduced new weak barrelledness conditions of being
p
-(quasi)barrelled spaces. Recall that an lcs
E
is
p
-(quasi)barrelled
if every weakly
p
-summable sequence in
E^{\prime}_{w^{\ast}}
(resp.,
E^{\prime}_{\beta}
) is equicontinuous. Since these spaces naturally appear in the study of geometrical properties of locally convex spaces like
V
-,
V^{\ast}
- or Gelfand–Phillips type properties, in this section we prove some general results and provide several examples which show the relationships between the weak barrelledness conditions introduced in Definitions
2.9
and
4.16
. We start from the following lemma which immediately follows from the corresponding definitions and (ii) of Lemma
4.6
.

Lemma 5.1

Let
E
be a locally convex space.

(i)

If
E
is
\ell_{\infty}
-
(
quasi
)
barrelled, then it is
\infty
-
(
quasi
)
barrelled.

(ii)

If
E
is
\infty
-quasibarrelled, then it is
c_{0}
-quasibarrelled; the converse is true if
E^{\prime}_{\beta}
has the Schur property.

In other words, the class of
\infty
-quasibarrelled spaces lies between the class of
c_{0}
-barrelled spaces and the class of
c_{0}
-quasibarrelled spaces, and Examples
5.4
and
5.5
below show that this disposition is strict.
It is convenient to describe the relationships between the weak barrelledness conditions introduced in Definitions
2.9
and
4.16
in a diagram which immediately follows from definitions and Lemmas
4.6
(ii) and
5.1
.
If
1\leq p<q\leq\infty
, then we have

barrelled
\textstyle{{\begin{subarray}{c}\mbox{$\infty$-barrelled}\\
\mbox{(= $c_{0}$-barrelled)}\end{subarray}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
q
-barrelled
p
-barrelled
1
-barrelled
\textstyle{{\begin{subarray}{c}\mbox{quasi-}\\
\mbox{barrelled}\end{subarray}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\begin{subarray}{c}\mbox{$\infty$-quasi-}\\
\mbox{barrelled}\end{subarray}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\begin{subarray}{c}\mbox{$q$-quasi-}\\
\mbox{barrelled}\end{subarray}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\begin{subarray}{c}\mbox{$p$-quasi-}\\
\mbox{barrelled}\end{subarray}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\begin{subarray}{c}\mbox{$1$-quasi-}\\
\mbox{barrelled}\end{subarray}}}
\textstyle{{\begin{subarray}{c}\mbox{$c_{0}$-quasi-}\\
\mbox{barrelled}\end{subarray}}}

(5.1)

In the next assertions and examples we clarify Diagram
5.1
.

Proposition 5.2

Let
X
be a sequentially complete barrelled space with the Glicksberg property, and let
E=\big(X^{\prime},\mu(X^{\prime},X)\big)
. Then:

(i)

X_{w}=(E^{\prime})_{w^{\ast}}
is sequentially complete;

(ii)

E
is a
c_{0}
-barrelled space;

(iii)

if moreover
X
is von Neumann complete, then
E
is
\ell_{\infty}
-quasibarrelled if and only if every bounded subset of
X
is relatively compact.

Proof 31

First we note that the barrelledness of
X
implies that
E^{\prime}_{\beta}
is the space
X
since the polar of a bounded subset of
E
is a barrel, see Theorem 8.8.4 of
[
67
]
.

(i) Let
S=\{x_{n}\}_{n\in\omega}
be a weakly Cauchy sequence in
X
. Since
X
has the Glicksberg property it is a Schur space and hence
S
is a Cauchy sequence in
X
. As
X
is sequentially complete,
S
converges to a point
x\in X
. Thus
x_{n}\to x
also in the weak topology.

(ii) Let
S=\{x_{n}\}_{n\in\omega}
be a weak
∗
null sequence in
E^{\prime}=X
. Then
S
is a weakly null sequence in the space
X
. By the Schur property,
x_{n}\to 0
in
X
. Since
X
is sequentially (hence also locally) complete, the closed absolutely convex hull
K:=\overline{\mathrm{acx}}(S)
of
S
is a compact subset of
X
. Therefore, by the Mackey–Arens theorem,
K^{\circ}
is a neighborhood of zero in
E
. Whence
K^{\circ\circ}=K
and hence also
S
are equicontinuous. Thus
E
is
c_{0}
-barrelled.

(iii) Assume that
E
is an
\ell_{\infty}
-quasibarrelled space. Suppose for a contradiction that there is a bounded subset
B
of
X
which is not relatively compact. Since
X
is von Neumann complete,
B
is not precompact. Therefore there are a sequence
S=\{x_{n}\}_{n\in\omega}
in
B
and
U\in\mathcal{N}_{0}(X)
such that
x_{n}-x_{m}\not\in U
for all distinct
n,m\in\omega
. Then
S
is also bounded and non-precompact in
E^{\prime}_{\beta}=X
. As
E
is
\ell_{\infty}
-quasibarrelled,
S
is equicontinuous and hence there is
V\in\mathcal{N}_{0}(E)
such that
S\subseteq V^{\circ}
. Since, by the Alaoglu theorem,
V^{\circ}
is weakly compact in
X
, the Glicksberg property of
X
implies that
V^{\circ}
is a compact subset of
X
. Therefore the sequence
S
is precompact in
X
. But this contradicts the choice of
S
.

Conversely, assume that every bounded subset of
X
is relatively compact. To show that
E
is an
\ell_{\infty}
-quasibarrelled space, we prove that every countable bounded subset
B
of
E^{\prime}_{\beta}=X
is equicontinuous. Observe that
B^{\circ\circ}
is bounded and closed in
X
, and hence, by assumption,
B^{\circ\circ}
is an absolutely convex (weakly) compact subset of
X
. By the Mackey–Arens theorem, we obtain that
B^{\circ}=B^{\circ\circ\circ}
is a neighborhood of zero in
E
. So
B^{\circ\circ}
and hence also
B
are equicontinuous. Thus
E
is
\ell_{\infty}
-quasibarrelled. ∎

Recall that a locally convex space
E
is called
semi-Montel
if every bounded subset of
E
is relatively compact, and
E
is a
Montel space
if it is a barrelled semi-Montel space. Clearly, every semi-Montel space has the Glicksberg property.

Corollary 5.3

Let
X
be a strict
(LF)
-space. Then
X
is a Montel space if and only if it has the Schur property and the space
\big(X^{\prime},\mu(X^{\prime},X)\big)
is
\ell_{\infty}
-quasibarrelled.

Proof 32

Any strict
(LF)
-space is complete and barrelled by Theorem 4.5.7 and Proposition 11.3.1 of
[
52
]
. By Theorem 1.2 of
[
35
]
,
X
has the Glicksberg property if and only if it has the Schur property. Now Proposition
5.2
applies. ∎

In particular, Corollary
5.3
gives a new characterization of Fréchet–Montel spaces which are studied in Section 11.6 of
[
52
]
.

The next example shows that there are even metrizable (hence quasibarrelled) locally convex spaces which are not
p
-barrelled for every
p\in[1,\infty]
. On the other hand, this example complements (iv) of Proposition
4.17
by showing that the condition “
1<p<\infty
” is not necessary for the inclusion
S_{p}\big(\mathcal{L}(E,\ell_{p})\big)\subseteq\ell_{p}^{w}(E^{\prime}_{\beta})
.

Example 5.4

Let
p\in[1,\infty]
, and let
E=(c_{0})_{p}
. Then:

(i)

E
is metrizable
(
hence quasibarrelled
)
but not
p
-barrelled;

(ii)

each operator
T\in\mathcal{L}(E,\ell_{p})
or
T\in\mathcal{L}(E,c_{0})
is finite-dimensional;

(iii)

S_{p}\big(\mathcal{L}(E,\ell_{p})\big)=\ell_{p}^{w}(E^{\prime}_{\beta})

(
or
S_{\infty}\big(\mathcal{L}(E,c_{0})\big)=c_{0}^{w}(E^{\prime}_{\beta})
if
p=\infty
)
;

(iv)

\ell_{p}^{w}(E^{\prime}_{\beta})\subsetneq\ell_{p}^{w}(E^{\prime}_{w^{\ast}})

(
or
c_{0}^{w}(E^{\prime}_{\beta})\subsetneq c_{0}^{w}(E^{\prime}_{w^{\ast}})
if
p=\infty
)
.

Proof 33

First we prove the following claim which is used in what follows.

Claim 1.
E^{\prime}_{\beta}=\varphi
, and hence
E^{\prime\prime}=\mathbb{F}^{\omega}
. Indeed, since
E
is dense in the product
\mathbb{F}^{\omega}
, then
E^{\prime}=\varphi
algebraically. To show that the equality holds also topologically, let
B=\prod_{n\in\omega}a_{n}\mathbb{D}
(where all
a_{n}>0
) be a bounded subset of
\mathbb{F}^{\omega}
, so that
B^{\circ}
is a standard neighborhood of zero in
\varphi
. Fix an arbitrary
\chi\in E^{\prime}
, so
\chi
has finite support. Since
E\cap B
is dense in
B
and is bounded in
E
, we obtain that
\chi\in B^{\circ}
if and only if
\chi\in(E\cap B)^{\circ}
. Now it is clear that
E^{\prime}_{\beta}=\varphi
. The claim is proved.

(i) Since every equicontinuous subset of
E^{\prime}
is strongly bounded by Theorem 11.3.5 of
[
67
]
, Claim 1 implies that the equicontinuous subsets of
E^{\prime}
are finite-dimensional. Therefore to prove that
E
is not
p
-barrelled, it suffices to show that the infinite-dimensional sequence
S=\big\{\tfrac{e_{n}^{\ast}}{(n+1)^{2}}\big\}_{n\in\omega}
, where
\{e_{n}^{\ast}\big\}_{n\in\omega}
is the standard coordinate basis of
E^{\prime}=\varphi
, is weakly
1
-summable in
E^{\prime}_{w^{\ast}}
. To this end, fix an arbitrary
\mathbf{x}=(x_{n})\in E
. Since
x_{n}\to 0
it follows that the series

\sum_{n\in\omega}\Big|\Big\langle\tfrac{e_{n}^{\ast}}{(n+1)^{2}},\mathbf{x}\Big\rangle\Big|=\sum_{n\in\omega}\tfrac{|x_{n}|}{(n+1)^{2}}

converges. Thus
S
is weakly
1
-summable in
E^{\prime}_{w^{\ast}}
, as desired.

(ii) Let
T\in\mathcal{L}(E,\ell_{p})
(or
T\in\mathcal{L}(E,c_{0})
if
p=\infty
). Take a standard neighborhood
U=E\cap(a\mathbb{D})^{n}\times\mathbb{F}^{\omega{\setminus}n}
(
a>0
) of zero in
E
such that
T(U)\subseteq B_{\ell_{p}}
(or
T(U)\subseteq B_{c_{0}}
if
p=\infty
). Then
E\cap\big(\{0\}^{n}\times\mathbb{F}^{\omega{\setminus}n}\big)
is contained in the kernel of
T
. Thus
T
is finite-dimensional.

(iii) First we describe the space
\ell_{p}^{w}(E^{\prime}_{\beta})
(or
c_{0}^{w}(E^{\prime}_{\beta})
if
p=\infty
). By Claim 1,
E^{\prime}_{\beta}=\varphi
and
E^{\prime\prime}=\mathbb{F}^{\omega}
. Since any sequence in
\ell_{p}^{w}(E^{\prime}_{\beta})
(or in
c_{0}^{w}(E^{\prime}_{\beta})
if
p=\infty
) is strongly bounded and hence finite-dimensional, Lemma
4.13
implies that
(\chi_{n})_{n\in\omega}
belongs to
\ell_{p}^{w}(E^{\prime}_{\beta})
if and only if there is
s\in\omega
such that

\chi_{n}=a_{0,n}e^{\ast}_{0}+\cdots+a_{s,n}e^{\ast}_{s}\quad(n\in\omega)

(5.2)

for some scalars
a_{i,j}\in\mathbb{F}
such that

(a_{i,n})_{n\in\omega}\in\ell_{p}\;\;(\mbox{or $\in c_{0}$ if $p=\infty$})\;\;\mbox{ for all }\;0\leq i\leq s.

(5.3)

Let
T\in\mathcal{L}(E,\ell_{p})
(or
T\in\mathcal{L}(E,c_{0})
if
p=\infty
). Then, by (ii),
T
is finite-dimensional. Therefore there are some
\mu_{1},\dots,\mu_{m}\in E^{\prime}
and
\nu_{1},\dots,\nu_{m}\in\ell_{p}
(or
\in c_{0}
if
p=\infty
) such that
T(x)=\sum_{i=1}^{m}\langle\mu_{i},x\rangle\nu_{i}
. Then for every
x\in E
and
n\in\omega
, we have

\langle T^{\ast}(e_{n}^{\ast}),x\rangle=\sum_{i=1}^{m}\langle\mu_{i},x\rangle\cdot\langle e_{n}^{\ast},\nu_{i}\rangle=\Big\langle\sum_{i=1}^{m}\langle e_{n}^{\ast},\nu_{i}\rangle\mu_{i},x\Big\rangle

and hence
T^{\ast}(e_{n}^{\ast})=\sum_{i=1}^{m}\langle e_{n}^{\ast},\nu_{i}\rangle\mu_{i}
. Thus
\{T^{\ast}(e_{n}^{\ast})\}_{n\in\omega}
is contained in the finite-dimensional subspace
\mathsf{span}\{\mu_{1},\dots,\mu_{m}\}
of
E^{\prime}_{\beta}=\varphi
. If

\mu_{i}=d_{0,i}e^{\ast}_{0}+\cdots+d_{s,i}e^{\ast}_{s}\;\;\mbox{ for }\;\;1\leq i\leq m,

then for every
n\in\omega
, we obtain

T^{\ast}(e_{n}^{\ast})=\sum_{i=1}^{m}\langle e_{n}^{\ast},\nu_{i}\rangle\Big(\sum_{j=0}^{s}d_{j,i}e_{j}^{\ast}\Big)=\sum_{j=0}^{s}\Big(\sum_{i=1}^{m}\langle e_{n}^{\ast},\nu_{i}\rangle d_{j,i}\Big)\cdot e_{j}^{\ast}.

Set
a_{j,n}:=\sum_{i=1}^{m}\langle e_{n}^{\ast},\nu_{i}\rangle d_{j,i}
and
\chi_{n}:=\sum_{j=0}^{s}a_{j,n}e_{j}^{\ast}
. Since
\nu_{i}\in\ell_{p}
(or
\in c_{0}
if
p=\infty
), it follows that
(a_{j,n})_{n}\in\ell_{p}
(or
\in c_{0}
if
p=\infty
) for every
0\leq j\leq s
. Therefore, by (
5.2
) and (
5.3
),
(\chi_{n})\in\ell_{p}^{w}(E^{\prime}_{\beta})
(or
\in c_{0}^{w}(E^{\prime}_{\beta})
if
p=\infty
) and hence
S_{p}\big(\mathcal{L}(E,\ell_{p})\big)\subseteq\ell_{p}^{w}(E^{\prime}_{\beta})
(or
S_{\infty}\big(\mathcal{L}(E,c_{0})\big)\subseteq c_{0}^{w}(E^{\prime}_{\beta})
).

To prove the inverse inclusion
\ell_{p}^{w}(E^{\prime}_{\beta})\subseteq S_{p}\big(\mathcal{L}(E,\ell_{p})\big)
(or
c_{0}^{w}(E^{\prime}_{\beta})\subseteq S_{\infty}\big(\mathcal{L}(E,c_{0})\big)
), let
(\chi_{n})\in\ell_{p}^{w}(E^{\prime}_{\beta})
. Then we can assume that
(\chi_{n})
has a decomposition (
5.2
) such that (
5.3
) is satisfied. Define
T:E\to\ell_{p}
(or
T:E\to c_{0}
if
p=\infty
) by

T(x):=\big(\langle\chi_{n},x\rangle\big)_{n\in\omega}\quad(x\in E).

To show that
T
is continuous, for
0\leq i\leq s
, we set
\nu_{i}:=(a_{i,n})\in\ell_{p}
(or
\in c_{0}
if
p=\infty
). Then, for
x=(x_{n})\in E
, we obtain

T(x)=\big(\langle\chi_{n},x\rangle\big)_{n}=\big(a_{0,n}x_{0}+\cdots+a_{s,n}x_{s}\big)_{n}=\sum_{i=0}^{s}x_{i}\nu_{i}=\sum_{i=0}^{s}\langle e^{\ast}_{i},x\rangle\nu_{i}.

Therefore
T
is finite-dimensional and hence continuous. Since
\langle T^{\ast}(e^{\ast}_{n}),x\rangle=\langle e^{\ast}_{n},T(x)\rangle=\langle\chi_{n},x\rangle
we obtain
S_{p}(T)=(\chi_{n})
. As
(\chi_{n})
was arbitrary, this proves the inverse inclusion.

(iv) To prove this clause it suffices to show that the weakly
1
-summable sequence
(\chi_{n})_{n}
in
E^{\prime}_{w^{\ast}}
constructed in (i) does not belong to
\ell_{p}^{w}(E^{\prime}_{\beta})
. To this end, let
\eta=(1,2,\dots)\in E^{\prime\prime}=\mathbb{F}^{\omega}
. Since the series
\sum_{n\in\omega}|\langle\eta,\chi_{n}\rangle|=\sum_{n\in\omega}\tfrac{1}{n+1}
diverges, we obtain
(\chi_{n})\not\in\ell_{p}^{w}(E^{\prime}_{\beta})
(or
\not\in c_{0}^{w}(E^{\prime}_{\beta})
if
p=\infty
).∎

The next examples shows that there exist
c_{0}
-quasibarrelled spaces which are not
1
-quasibarrelled clarifying the relationships in Diagram
5.1
.

Example 5.5

There is a
c_{0}
-quasibarrelled space which is not
1
-quasibarrelled.

Proof 34

For every
n\in\omega
, let
x_{n}=\tfrac{1}{n+1}
and
x_{\infty}=0
. Let
\mathbf{s}=\{x_{n}\}_{n\in\omega}\cup\{x_{\infty}\}
be the convergent sequence endowed with the topology induced from
\mathbb{R}
, and let
E=L(\mathbf{s})
be the free lcs over
\mathbf{s}
. Then, by Theorem 1.2 of
[
34
]
, the space
E
is
c_{0}
-quasibarrelled. To show that
E
is not
1
-quasibarrelled, we construct a weakly
1
-summable sequence
\{f_{n}\}
in the strong dual
E^{\prime}_{\beta}
of
E
which is not equicontinuous. Note that
E^{\prime}_{\beta}
is the Banach space
C(\mathbf{s})
by Proposition 3.4 of
[
34
]
. For every
n\in\omega
, define
f_{n}\in C(\mathbf{s})
by

f_{n}(x)=1\;\mbox{ if }x=x_{n}\;\mbox{ and }\;f_{n}(x)=0\;\mbox{ otherwise}.

Now, let
\mu\in C(\mathbf{s})^{\prime}
and hence
\mu=\sum_{n\leq\omega}a_{n}\delta_{x_{n}}
, where
\|\mu\|=\sum_{n\leq\omega}|a_{n}|<\infty
. Then
\sum_{n\leq\omega}|\langle\mu,f_{n}\rangle|=\sum_{n\in\omega}|a_{n}|<\infty
. Thus
\{f_{n}\}
is weakly
1
-summable.

Let us show that
\{f_{n}\}
is not equicontinuous which means that
E
is not
1
-quasibarrelled. Indeed, suppose for a contradiction that
\{f_{n}\}
is equicontinuous. Then, by Proposition 2.3 of
[
34
]
, the sequence
\{f_{n}\}
is equicontinuous as a subset of the function space
C(\mathbf{s})
. Since
f_{n}(x_{\infty})=0
for every
n\in\omega
and
x_{n}\to x_{\infty}
, it is easy to see that
\{f_{n}\}
is not equicontinuous at
0
, a contradiction.∎

The aforementioned results motivate the problem of finding classes of locally convex spaces
E
such that
E
is
c_{0}
-
(
quasi
)
barrelled if and only if it is a
p
-
(
quasi
)
barrelled space for some (every)
p\in[1,\infty]
. Below we show that the class of Mackey spaces satisfies this condition for
p
-barrelledness. Let us recall that Theorem 12.1.4 of
[
52
]
states that an lcs
E
is
c_{0}
-(quasi)barrelled if and only if the space
\big(E^{\prime},\sigma(E^{\prime},E)\big)

\big(
resp.,
\big(E^{\prime},\beta(E^{\prime},E)\big)
\big)
is locally complete. Analogously we have the following assertion.

Theorem 5.6

Let
p\in[1,\infty]
, and let
E
be a locally convex space.
If
E
is a
p
-barrelled space, then
\big(E^{\prime},\sigma(E^{\prime},E)\big)
is locally complete; the converse is true if
E
is a Mackey space.

Proof 35

By Theorem 10.2.4 of
[
52
]
, to show that the space
E^{\prime}_{w^{\ast}}:=\big(E^{\prime},\sigma(E^{\prime},E)\big)
is locally complete it suffices to prove that for every weak
∗

p
-summable sequence
S=\{\chi_{n}\}_{n\in\omega}
in
E^{\prime}_{\beta}
, the set
\overline{\mathrm{acx}}(S)
is
\sigma(E^{\prime},E)
-compact. Since
E
is a
p
-barrelled space, the sequence
S
and hence also
\overline{\mathrm{acx}}(S)
are equicontinuous. It follows that
\overline{\mathrm{acx}}(S)^{\circ}
is a neighborhood of zero in
E
. By the Alaoglu theorem,
\overline{\mathrm{acx}}(S)^{\circ\circ}=\overline{\mathrm{acx}}(S)
is weak
∗
compact. Thus
\big(E^{\prime},\sigma(E^{\prime},E)\big)
is locally complete.

Let now
E
be a Mackey space, and assume that
\big(E^{\prime},\sigma(E^{\prime},E)\big)
is locally complete. To show that
E
is a
p
-barrelled space, let
S=\{\chi_{n}\}_{n\in\omega}
be a weak
∗

p
-summable sequence in
E^{\prime}
. Since
E^{\prime}_{w^{\ast}}
is locally complete, Theorem 10.2.4 of
[
52
]
implies that
K:=\overline{\mathrm{acx}}(S)
is compact in
E^{\prime}_{w^{\ast}}
. Since
E
is Mackey, the Mackey–Arens theorem implies that
K^{\circ}
is a neighborhood of zero in
E
. Therefore
K^{\circ\circ}=K
and hence also
S
are equicontinuous. Thus
E
is a
p
-barrelled space.∎

Corollary 5.7

A Mackey space
E
is
c_{0}
-barrelled if and only if it is
p
-barrelled for some
(
every
)

p\in[1,\infty]
.

Proof 36

If
E
is
c_{0}
-barrelled, then clearly
E
is
p
-barrelled for all
p\in[1,\infty]
. Conversely, if
E
is
p
-barrelled for some
p\in[1,\infty]
, then, by Theorem
5.6
,
\big(E^{\prime},\sigma(E^{\prime},E)\big)
is locally complete. Thus
E
is
c_{0}
-barrelled by Theorem 12.1.4 of
[
52
]
.∎

It is well known that the class of (quasi)barrelled spaces is closed under taking products, direct sums, inductive limits, Hausdorff quotients and a completion of a barrelled space is barrelled, see Proposition 11.3.1 of
[
52
]
. Below we consider analogous properties for
p
-(quasi)barrelled spaces.

Proposition 5.8

Let
p\in[1,\infty]
.

(i)

Every Hausdorff quotient space of a
p
-
(
quasi
)
barrelled space is
p
-
(
quasi
)
barrelled. Consequently, complemented subspaces of

p
-
(
quasi
)
barrelled spaces are
p
-
(
quasi
)
barrelled.

(ii)

The Tychonoff product of a family of locally convex spaces is
p
-
(
quasi
)
barrelled if and only if each of its factor is a
p
-
(
quasi
)
barrelled space.

(iii)

Countable locally convex direct sums of
p
-
(
quasi
)
barrelled spaces are
p
-
(
quasi
)
barrelled spaces.

(iv)

Let
H
be a dense subspace of an lcs
E
. If
H
is
p
-barrelled then so is
E
.

(v)

Let
H
be a dense, large subspace of an lcs
E
. If
H
is
p
-quasibarrelled then so is
E
.

Proof 37

(i) Let
H
be a closed subspace of a
p
-(quasi)barrelled space
E
, and let
q:E\to E/H
be the quotient map. To show that
E/H
is
p
-(quasi)barrelled, let
S=\{\chi_{i}\}_{i\in\omega}
be a weak
∗
(resp., weakly)
p
-summable sequence
(E/H)^{\prime}_{\beta}
. Since the adjoint map
q^{\ast}
is weak
∗
(resp., strongly) continuous, (iii) of Lemma
4.6
implies that
\{q^{\ast}(\chi_{i})\}_{i\in\omega}
is a weak
∗
(resp., weakly)
p
-summable sequence in
E^{\prime}_{\beta}
. As
E
is
p
-(quasi)barrelled,
\{q^{\ast}(\chi_{i})\}_{i\in\omega}
is equicontinuous and hence there is
U\in\mathcal{N}_{0}(E)
such that
\{q^{\ast}(\chi_{i})\}_{i\in\omega}\subseteq U^{\circ}
. Since
q
is a quotient map, the image
q(U)
of
U
is a neighborhood of zero in
E/H
. Then for every
q(x)\in q(U)
with
x\in U
and each
i\in\omega
, we have

|\langle\chi_{i},q(x)\rangle|=|\langle q^{\ast}(\chi_{i}),x\rangle|\leq 1

which means that
S\subseteq q(U)^{\circ}
and hence the sequence
S
is equicontinuous. Thus
E/H
is a
p
-(quasi)barrelled space.

(ii) The necessity follows from (i).
To prove the sufficiency, assume that
E=\prod_{i\in I}E_{i}
is the product of a non-empty family
\{E_{i}\}_{i\in I}
of
p
-(quasi)barrelled spaces, and let
S=\{\chi_{n}\}_{n\in\omega}
be a weak
∗
(resp., weakly)
p
-summable sequence in
E^{\prime}_{\beta}
. Then, by (i) of Lemma
4.25
,
S
has finite support
F\subseteq I
and for every
i\in F
, the sequence
S_{i}:=\{\chi_{n}(i)\}_{n\in\omega}
is weak
∗
(resp., weakly)
p
-summable in
(E_{i})^{\prime}_{\beta}
. Since
E_{i}
is
p
-(quasi)barrelled,
S_{i}
is equicontinuous and hence
S_{i}\subseteq U_{i}^{\circ}
for some
U_{i}\in\mathcal{N}_{0}(E_{i})
. Set
U:=\prod_{i\in F}\tfrac{1}{|F|}U_{i}\times\prod_{i\in I{\setminus}F}E_{i}
. Then
U
is a neighborhood of zero in
E
such that for every
x=(x_{i})\in U
and each
n\in\omega
, we have

|\langle\chi_{n},x\rangle|=\Big|\sum_{i\in F}\langle\chi_{n}(i),x_{i}\rangle\Big|\leq\sum_{i\in F}|\langle\chi_{n}(i),x_{i}\rangle|\leq 1.

Therefore
S\subseteq U^{\circ}
is equicontinuous. Thus
E
is a
p
-(quasi)barrelled space.

(iii) Let
E=\bigoplus_{n\in\omega}E_{n}
be the direct sum of a sequence
\{E_{n}\}_{n\in\omega}
of
p
-(quasi)barrelled spaces. To show that
E
is
p
-(quasi)barrelled, fix a weak
∗
(resp., weakly)
p
-summable sequence
S=\{\chi_{i}\}_{i\in\omega}
in
E^{\prime}_{\beta}
. Then, by (i) of Lemma
4.25
, the sequence
\{\chi_{i}(n)\}_{i\in\omega}
is weak
∗
(resp., weakly)
p
-summable in
(E_{n})^{\prime}_{\beta}
for every
n\in\omega
. For every
n\in\omega
, the
p
-(quasi)barrelledness of
E_{n}
implies that
\{\chi_{i}(n)\}_{n\in\omega}
is equicontinuous and therefore there is
U_{n}\in\mathcal{N}_{0}(E_{n})
such that
\{\chi_{i}(n)\}_{n\in\omega}\subseteq\tfrac{1}{2^{n+1}}U_{n}^{\circ}
. Set
U:=E\cap\prod_{n\in\omega}U_{n}
. Then
U
is a neighborhood of zero in
E
. For every
x=(x_{n})\in U
and each
i\in\omega
, we have

|\langle\chi_{i},x\rangle|=\Big|\sum_{n\in\omega}\langle\chi_{i}(n),x_{n}\rangle\Big|\leq\sum_{n\in\omega}|\langle\chi_{i}(n),x_{n}\rangle|\leq\sum_{n\in\omega}\tfrac{1}{2^{n+1}}=1.

Therefore
S\subseteq U^{\circ}
. Thus
S
is equicontinuous and hence
E
is
p
-(quasi)barrelled.

(iv), (v): Recall that
E^{\prime}=H^{\prime}
and, in the case (v), Proposition
2.4
implies that
E^{\prime}_{\beta}=H^{\prime}_{\beta}
. Now, fix a weak
∗
(resp., weakly)
p
-summable sequence
S=\{\chi_{i}\}_{i\in\omega}
in
E^{\prime}_{\beta}
. Then
S
is weak
∗
(resp., weakly)
p
-summable also in
H^{\prime}_{\beta}
. Since
E
is
p
-barrelled (resp.,
p
-quasibarrelled),
S
is equicontinuous and hence there is
U\in\mathcal{N}_{0}(H)
such that
S\subseteq U^{\circ}
. Since the closure
\overline{U}
of
U
in
E
is a neighborhood of zero in
E
and
S\subseteq\overline{U}^{\,\circ}
, we obtain that
S
is equicontinuous in
E^{\prime}_{\beta}
. Thus
E
is
p
-(quasi)barrelled.∎

Since the strict inductive limit of a sequence
\{E_{n}\}_{n\in\omega}
of locally convex spaces is a quotient space of
\bigoplus_{n\in\omega}E_{n}
, Proposition
5.8
implies

Corollary 5.9

Strict inductive limits of sequences of
p
-
(
quasi
)
barrelled spaces are
p
-
(
quasi
)
barrelled.

Remark 5.10

(i) A dense subspace of a barreled space can be not
p
-barrelled. Indeed, let
X
be a Tychonoff space containing infinite functionally bounded subsets, and let
H=C_{p}(X)
and
E=B_{1}(X)
. Then
E
is barrelled by Theorem 1.1 of
[
4
]
. By the Buchwalter–Schmets theorem, the dense subspace
H
of
E
is not barrelled. Note also that, by Proposition 2.6 of
[
40
]
,
C_{p}(X)
is barrelled if and only if it is
c_{0}
-barrelled. Since
C_{p}(X)
is always quasibarrelled (hence Mackey), Corollary
5.7
implies that
H
is not
p
-barrelled for all
p\in[1,\infty]
.

(ii) Closed subspaces of
p
-barrelled spaces can be not
p
-barrelled. Indeed, let
H=C_{p}(X)
from (i). By Theorem 4.7 of
[
5
]
,
H
is topologically isomorphic to a closed subspace of even a barrelled space
E
.∎

6
p
-Schur property for locally convex spaces

Analogously to the
p
-Schur property for Banach spaces we define.

Definition 6.1

Let
p\in[1,\infty]
. A separated tvs
E
is said to have a
p
-Schur property or is a
p
-Schur space if every weakly
p
-summable sequence is a null-sequence.∎

In particular,
E
has the Schur property if and only if it is an
\infty
-Schur space.

For further references we select the next three simple lemmas. The first one is an immediate corollary of definitions and the Hahn–Banach extension theorem.

Lemma 6.2

Let
p\in[1,\infty]
, and let
E
be a locally convex space with the
p
-Schur property.

(i)

If
1\leq q\leq p
, then
E
has the
q
-Schur property.

(ii)

If
H
is a linear subspace of
E
, then
H
is also a
p
-Schur space.

Lemma 6.3

Let
p\in[1,\infty]
, and let
\{E_{i}\}_{i\in I}
be a non-empty family of locally convex spaces. Then the following assertions are equivalent:

(i)

\prod_{i\in I}E_{i}
is a
p
-Schur space;

(ii)

\bigoplus_{i\in I}E_{i}
is a
p
-Schur space;

(iii)

E_{i}
is a
p
-Schur space for each
i\in I
.

Proof 38

(i)
\Rightarrow
(ii) Let
S=\{x_{n}=(x_{n,i})_{i\in I}\}_{n\in\omega}
be a weakly
p
-summable sequence in the direct sum
E:=\bigoplus_{i\in I}E_{i}
. Then, by (iv) of Lemma
4.25
, the sequence
\{x_{n,i}\}_{n\in\omega}
is weakly
p
-summable in
E_{i}
for every
i\in I
and the support
F
of the sequence
S
is finite. Therefore, by (ii) of Lemma
6.2
and (i),
x_{n}\to 0
in
\prod_{i\in F}E_{i}
, and hence
x_{n}\to 0
in
E
.

The implication (ii)
\Rightarrow
(iii) follows from (ii) of Lemma
6.2
.

(iii)
\Rightarrow
(i) Let
\{x_{n}=(x_{n,i})_{i\in I}\}_{n\in\omega}
be a weakly
p
-summable sequence in the product
E:=\prod_{i\in I}E_{i}
. Then, by (ii) of Lemma
4.25
, the sequence
\{x_{n,i}\}_{n\in\omega}
is weakly
p
-summable in
E_{i}
for every
i\in I
. By the
p
-Schur property of
E_{i}
, we obtain that
x_{n,i}\to 0
in
E_{i}
. Thus
x_{n}\to 0
in
E
.∎

Lemma 6.4

Let
p\in[1,\infty]
, and let
E=\underrightarrow{\mbox{ s-$\mathsf{ind}$}}_{n}\,E_{n}
be the strict inductive limit of an increasing sequence
\{E_{n}\}_{n\in\omega}
of locally convex spaces. Then
E
has the
p
-Schur property if and only if all spaces
E_{n}
are
p
-Schur spaces.

Proof 39

The necessity follows from the fact that all
E_{n}
are subspaces of
E
and (ii) of Lemma
6.2
. The sufficiency follows from the fact that
E
is regular (= any bounded subset of
E
sits in some
E_{n}
) and (vi) of Lemma
4.6
.∎

The next proposition shows that the property of being a
p
-Schur space depends on
p
and will be used for constructing other counterexamples.
Recall (see Example 2.6 of
[
21
]
) that the Banach space
\ell_{p}
does not have the
p^{\ast}
-Schur property, where
\tfrac{1}{p}+\tfrac{1}{p^{\ast}}=1
. In (ii) of the next proposition we generalize this result.

Proposition 6.5

Let
1<p<\infty
, and let
q\in[1,\infty]
. Then:

(i)

if
q<p^{\ast}
, then
\ell_{p}
has the
q
-Schur property;

(ii)

if
q\geq p^{\ast}
, then
\ell_{p}^{0}
does not have the
q
-Schur property;

Proof 40

(i) To show that
\ell_{p}
has the
q
-Schur property, let
\{x_{n}\}_{n\in\omega}
be a weakly
q
-summable sequence in
\ell_{p}
, and suppose for a contradiction that
\|x_{n}\|_{\ell_{p}}\not\to 0
. Then, passing to a subsequence if needed, we can assume that there is
C>1
such that

\tfrac{1}{C}\leq\|x_{n}\|_{\ell_{p}}\leq C\quad\mbox{ for every }\;n\in\omega.

(6.1)

We claim that
\{x_{n}\}_{n\in\omega}
does not contain a subsequence which is Cauchy in the norm topology. Indeed, otherwise, we could find a subsequence
\{x_{n_{k}}\}_{k\in\omega}
of
\{x_{n}\}_{n\in\omega}
which converges in the norm to some
x\in\ell_{p}
. Therefore
x_{n_{k}}\to x
in the weak topology, and hence
x=0
because
\{x_{n}\}_{n\in\omega}
is weakly null. But then
\|x_{n_{k}}\|_{\ell_{p}}\to 0
which contradicts (
6.1
).

Since
\{x_{n}\}_{n\in\omega}
is weakly null, (
6.1
) and Proposition 2.1.3 of
[
1
]
(see also Theorem 1.3.2 of
[
1
]
) imply that there are a basic subsequence
\{x_{n_{k}}\}_{k\in\omega}
of
\{x_{n}\}_{n\in\omega}
and a linear topological isomorphism
R:L:=\overline{\mathsf{span}}\big(\{x_{n_{k}}\}_{k\in\omega}\big)\to\ell_{p}
such that

R(x_{n_{k}})=a_{k}e_{k}\quad\mbox{ for every }\;k\in\omega,\mbox{ where $a_{k}=\|x_{n_{k}}\|_{\ell_{p}}$},

and the subspace
L
is complemented in
\ell_{p}
. It follows from (
6.1
) that the map
Q:\ell_{p}\to\ell_{p}
defined by
Q(\alpha_{k})=(a_{k}\alpha_{k})
is a topological linear isomorphism. Therefore, replacing
R
by
Q^{-1}\circ R
, without loss of generality we can assume that
a_{k}=1
for every
k\in\omega
, that is

R(x_{n_{k}})=e_{k}\quad\mbox{ for every }\;k\in\omega.

(6.2)

Since
q<p^{\ast}
, there exists an element
\chi=(y_{n})\in\ell_{p^{\ast}}{\setminus}\ell_{q}
. As
L
is a subspace of
\ell_{p}
we can consider
R^{\ast}(\chi)\in L^{\prime}
as an element of
(\ell_{p})^{\prime}
. Then, by (
6.2
), we have

\sum_{k\in\omega}\big|\langle R^{\ast}(\chi),x_{n_{k}}\rangle\big|^{q}=\sum_{k\in\omega}\big|\langle\chi,e_{k}\rangle\big|^{q}=\sum_{k\in\omega}|y_{n}|^{q}=\infty

which means that the sequence
\{x_{n_{k}}\}_{k\in\omega}
and hence also
\{x_{n}\}_{n\in\omega}
are not weakly
q
-summable, a contradiction. Thus
\ell_{p}
has the
q
-Schur property.

(iii) To show that
\ell_{p}^{0}
does not have the
q
-Schur property we consider the standard unit basis
\{e_{n}\}_{n\in\omega}
of
\ell_{p}^{0}
. Then, by Example
4.4
,
\{e_{n}\}_{n\in\omega}
is weakly
q
-summable. But since
\|e_{n}\|_{\ell_{p}}=1
for all
n\in\omega
, the space
\ell_{p}^{0}
does not have the
q
-Schur property.∎

Lemma
6.2
(ii) and Proposition
6.5
immediately imply

Corollary 6.6

Let
1<p<\infty
, and let
q\geq p^{\ast}
. If a locally convex space
E
contains an isomorphic copy of
\ell_{p}^{0}
, then
E
does not have the
q
-Schur property.

7
(V^{\ast})
type subsets of locally convex spaces

We start from the following generalization of the notion of a
p
-
(V^{\ast})
set in a Banach space.

Definition 7.1

Let
p,q\in[1,\infty]
. A non-empty subset
A
of a separated topological vector space
E
is called a
(p,q)
-
(V^{\ast})
set (resp., a
(p,q)
-
(EV^{\ast})
set) if

\Big(\sup_{a\in A}|\langle\chi_{n},a\rangle|\Big)\in\ell_{q}\;\mbox{ if $q<\infty$, }\;\mbox{ or }\;\;\Big(\sup_{a\in A}|\langle\chi_{n},a\rangle|\Big)\in c_{0}\;\mbox{ if $q=\infty$},

for every (resp., equicontinuous) weakly
p
-summable sequence
\{\chi_{n}\}_{n\in\omega}
in
E^{\prime}_{\beta}
.
(p,\infty)
-
(V^{\ast})
sets and
(1,\infty)
-
(V^{\ast})
sets will be called simply
p
-
(V^{\ast})
sets and
(V^{\ast})
sets, respectively. Analogously,
(p,\infty)
-
(EV^{\ast})
sets and
(1,\infty)
-
(EV^{\ast})
sets will be called
p
-
(EV^{\ast})
sets and
(EV^{\ast})
sets, respectively. ∎

In the next lemma we summarize some of basic properties of
(p,q)
-
(V^{\ast})
sets.

Lemma 7.2

Let
p,q\in[1,\infty]
, and let
(E,\tau)
be a locally convex space. Then:

(i)

every
(p,q)
-
(V^{\ast})
set in
E
is a
(p,q)
-
(EV^{\ast})
set, the converse is true if
E
is
p
-quasibarrelled;

(ii)

every
(p,q)
-
(EV^{\ast})
set in
E
is bounded;

(iii)

the family of all
(p,q)
-
(V^{\ast})

(
resp.,
(p,q)
-
(EV^{\ast})
)
sets in
E
is closed under taking subsets, finite unions, finite sums, and closed absolutely convex hulls;

(iv)

if
T
is an operator from
E
into an lcs
L
and
A
is a subset of
E
, then
T(A)
is a
(p,q)
-
(V^{\ast})

(
resp.,
(p,q)
-
(EV^{\ast})
)
set if so is
A
;

(v)

a subset
A
of
E
is a
(p,q)
-
(V^{\ast})

(
resp.,
(p,q)
-
(EV^{\ast})
)
set if and only if every countable subset of
A
is a
(p,q)
-
(V^{\ast})

(
resp.,
(p,q)
-
(EV^{\ast})
)
set;

(vi)

if
p^{\prime},q^{\prime}\in[1,\infty]
are such that
p^{\prime}\leq p
and
q\leq q^{\prime}
, then every
(p,q)
-
(V^{\ast})

(
resp.,
(p,q)
-
(EV^{\ast})
)
set
A
in
E
is also a
(p^{\prime},q^{\prime})
-
(V^{\ast})

(
resp.,
(p^{\prime},q^{\prime})
-
(EV^{\ast})
)
set; in particular, any
(p,q)
-
(V^{\ast})

(
resp.,
(p,q)
-
(EV^{\ast})
)
is a
(V^{\ast})

(
resp.,
(EV^{\ast})
)
set;

(vii)

the property of being a
(p,q)
-
(V^{\ast})
set depends only on the duality
(E,E^{\prime})
, i.e., if
\mathcal{T}
is a locally convex vector topology on
E
compatible with the topology
\tau
of
E
, then
(E,\mathcal{T})
and
(E,\tau)
have the same
(p,q)
-
(V^{\ast})
sets;

(viii)

if
H
is a subspace of
E
, then
(p,q)
-
(V^{\ast})
sets of
H
are also
(p,q)
-
(V^{\ast})

(
resp.,
(p,q)
-
(EV^{\ast})
)
sets in
E
.

Proof 41

(i) and (iii) are clear, and (viii) follows from (iv) applied to the identity operator
\mathsf{id}_{H}:H\to E
.

(ii) Let
A
be a
(p,q)
-
(EV^{\ast})
set and suppose for a contradiction that it is unbounded. Then there is
\chi\in E^{\prime}
such that for every
n\in\omega
, there exists
a_{n}\in A
for which
|\langle\chi,a_{n}\rangle|>(n+1)^{3}
. For every
n\in\omega
, set
\chi_{n}:=\tfrac{1}{(n+1)^{2}}\cdot\chi
. Then
\{\chi_{n}\}_{n\in\omega}
is an equicontinuous weakly
p
-summable sequence in
E^{\prime}_{\beta}
. However, since

\sup_{a\in A}|\langle\chi_{n},a\rangle|\geq|\langle\chi_{n},a_{n}\rangle|>n+1\to\infty

it follows that
A
is not a
(p,q)
-
(EV^{\ast})
set, a contradiction. Thus
A
is bounded.

(iv) Observe that the adjoint operator
T^{\ast}:L^{\prime}_{\beta}\to E^{\prime}_{\beta}
is continuous. Fix a (resp., equicontinuous) weakly
p
-summable sequence
\{\chi_{n}\}_{n\in\omega}
in
L^{\prime}_{\beta}
. Then, by Lemma
4.6
(iii), the sequence
\{T^{\ast}(\chi_{n})\}_{n\in\omega}
is weakly
p
-summable in
E^{\prime}_{\beta}
, and if
\{\chi_{n}\}_{n\in\omega}
is equicontinuous then so is
\{T^{\ast}(\chi_{n})\}_{n\in\omega}
. Therefore

\Big(\sup_{a\in A}|\langle\chi_{n},T(a)\rangle|\Big)=\Big(\sup_{a\in A}|\langle T^{\ast}(\chi_{n}),a\rangle|\Big)\in\ell_{q}\;\mbox{ (or }\;\in c_{0}\;\mbox{ if $q=\infty$}),

which means that
T(A)
is a
(p,q)
-
(V^{\ast})
(resp.,
(p,q)
-
(EV^{\ast})
) set in
L
.

(v) The necessity follows from (iii). To prove the sufficiency suppose for a contradiction that
A
is not a
(p,q)
-
(V^{\ast})
(resp.,
(p,q)
-
(EV^{\ast})
) set in
E
. Then there is a (resp., equicontinuous) weakly
p
-summable sequence
\{\chi_{n}\}_{n\in\omega}
in
E^{\prime}_{\beta}
such that

\Big(\sup_{a\in A}|\langle\chi_{n},a\rangle|\Big)\not\in\ell_{q}\;\mbox{ if $q<\infty$, }\;\mbox{ or }\;\;\Big(\sup_{a\in A}|\langle\chi_{n},a\rangle|\Big)\not\in c_{0}\;\mbox{ if $q=\infty$}.

Assume that
q<\infty
(the case
q=\infty
can be considered analogously). For every
n\in\omega
, choose
a_{n}\in A
such that
|\langle\chi_{n},a_{n}\rangle|\geq\tfrac{1}{2}\cdot\sup_{a\in A}|\langle\chi_{n},a\rangle|
. Then

\sum_{n\in\omega}|\langle\chi_{n},a_{n}\rangle|^{q}\geq\tfrac{1}{2^{q}}\sum_{n\in\omega}\big(\sup_{a\in A}|\langle\chi_{n},a\rangle|\big)^{q}=\infty.

Thus the countable subset
\{a_{n}\}_{n\in\omega}
of
A
is not a
(p,q)
-
(V^{\ast})
(resp.,
(p,q)
-
(EV^{\ast})
) set in
E
, a contradiction.

(vi) Take any (resp., equicontinuous) weakly
p^{\prime}
-summable sequence
\{\chi_{n}\}_{n\in\omega}
in
E^{\prime}_{\beta}
. Since
p^{\prime}\leq p
,
\{\chi_{n}\}
is also (resp., equicontinuous) weakly
p
-summable and hence
\big(\sup_{a\in A}|\langle\chi_{n},a\rangle|\big)\in\ell_{q}
(or
\in c_{0}
if
q=\infty
). It remains to note that
\ell_{q}\subseteq\ell_{q^{\prime}}
because
q\leq q^{\prime}
.

(vii) Since
\tau
and
\mathcal{T}
are compatible,
\mathsf{Bo}(E,\tau)=\mathsf{Bo}(E,\mathcal{T})
and hence
(E,\tau)^{\prime}_{\beta}=(E,\mathcal{T})^{\prime}_{\beta}
. Now the assertion follows from the definition of
(p,q)
-
(V^{\ast})
sets.∎

Notation 7.3

The family of all
(p,q)
-
(V^{\ast})
sets (resp.
p
-
(V^{\ast})
sets,
(p,q)
-
(EV^{\ast})
sets,
(V^{\ast})
sets etc.) of an lcs
E
is denoted by
\mathsf{V}^{\ast}_{(p,q)}(E)
(resp.
\mathsf{V}^{\ast}_{p}(E)
,
\mathsf{EV}^{\ast}_{(p,q)}(E)
,
\mathsf{V}^{\ast}(E)
etc.).∎

Below we characterize
p
-
(V^{\ast})
sets in Tychonoff products and locally convex direct sums.

Proposition 7.4

Let
p\in[1,\infty]
, and let
\{E_{i}\}_{i\in I}
be a non-empty family of locally convex spaces. Then:

(i)

a subset
K
of
E=\prod_{i\in I}E_{i}
is a
(p,q)
-
(V^{\ast})
set
(
resp., a
(p,q)
-
(EV^{\ast})
set
)
if and only if so are all its coordinate projections;

(ii)

a subset
K
of
E=\bigoplus_{i\in I}E_{i}
is a
(p,q)
-
(V^{\ast})
set
(
resp., a
(p,q)
-
(EV^{\ast})
set
)
if and only if so are all its coordinate projections and the support of
K
is finite.

Proof 42

The necessity follows from (iv) of Lemma
7.2
since
E_{i}
is a direct summand of
E
and, for the case (ii), from the well known fact that any bounded subset of a locally convex direct sum has finite support.

To prove the sufficiency, let
K
be a subset of
E
such that each projection
K_{i}
of
K
is a
(p,q)
-
(V^{\ast})
set
(
resp., a
(p,q)
-
(EV^{\ast})
set
)
in
E_{i}
, and, for the case (ii),
K_{i}=\{0\}
for all but finitely many indices
i\in I
. We distinguish between the cases (i) and (ii).

(i) Take an arbitrary (resp., equicontinuous) weakly
p
-summable sequence
\{\chi_{n}\}_{n\in\omega}
in
E^{\prime}_{\beta}
, where
\chi_{n}=(\chi_{i,n})_{i\in I}
. Since
\{\chi_{n}\}
is a bounded subset of the direct sum
E^{\prime}_{\beta}
(see Proposition
2.5
), it has finite support, i.e., for some finite set
F\subseteq I
it follows that
\chi_{i,n}=0
for every
n\in\omega
and each
i\in I{\setminus}F
. Observe also that if
\{\chi_{n}\}
is equicontinuous, then for every
i\in F
, the sequence
\{\chi_{i,n}\}_{n\in\omega}
is also equicontinuous as a projection of
\{\chi_{n}\}
onto the
i
th coordinate. Then

\sup_{x\in K}|\langle\chi_{n},x\rangle|=\sup_{x\in K}\big|\sum_{i\in F}\langle\chi_{i,n},x(i)\rangle\big|\leq\sum_{i\in F}\sup_{x(i)\in K_{i}}|\langle\chi_{i,n},x(i)\rangle|.

(7.1)

Since all
K_{i}
are
(p,q)
-
(V^{\ast})
sets (resp.,
(p,q)
-
(EV^{\ast})
sets) and
\{\chi_{i,n}\}_{n\in\omega}
is weakly
p
-summable by (i) of Lemma
4.25
, we have
\big(\sup_{x(i)\in K_{i}}|\langle\chi_{i,n},x(i)\rangle|\big)\in\ell_{q}
(or
\in c_{0}
if
q=\infty
). Therefore, by (
7.1
), also
\big(\sup_{x\in K}|\langle\chi_{n},x\rangle|\big)\in\ell_{q}
(or
\in c_{0}
if
q=\infty
). Thus
K
is a
(p,q)
-
(V^{\ast})
set (resp., a
(p,q)
-
(EV^{\ast})
set) in
E^{\prime}
.

(ii) Let
F\subseteq I
be the finite support of
K
. Take an arbitrary (resp., equicontinuous) weakly
p
-summable sequence
\{\chi_{n}\}_{n\in\omega}
in
E^{\prime}_{\beta}
, where
\chi_{n}=(\chi_{i,n})_{i\in I}
with
\chi_{i,n}\in E^{\prime}_{i}
. If in addition
\{\chi_{n}\}
is equicontinuous, then as above, for every
i\in F
, the sequence
\{\chi_{i,n}\}_{n\in\omega}
is also equicontinuous in
E^{\prime}_{i}
. Then

\sup_{x\in K}|\langle\chi_{n},x\rangle|=\sup_{x\in K}\big|\sum_{i\in F}\langle\chi_{i,n},x(i)\rangle\big|\leq\sum_{i\in F}\sup_{x(i)\in K_{i}}|\langle\chi_{i,n},x(i)\rangle|.

(7.2)

Since all
K_{i}
are
(p,q)
-
(V^{\ast})
sets (resp.,
(p,q)
-
(EV^{\ast})
sets) and
\{\chi_{i,n}\}_{n\in\omega}
is (resp., equicontinuous) weakly
p
-summable by (iii) of Lemma
4.25
, we have
\big(\sup_{x(i)\in K_{i}}|\langle\chi_{i,n},x(i)\rangle|\big)\in\ell_{q}
(or
\in c_{0}
if
q=\infty
). Therefore, by (
7.2
),
\big(\sup_{x\in K}|\langle\chi_{n},x\rangle|\big)\in\ell_{q}
(or
\in c_{0}
if
q=\infty
) as well. Thus
K
is a
(p,q)
-
(V^{\ast})
set (resp., a
(p,q)
-
(EV^{\ast})
set) in
E^{\prime}
.∎

Although the class
\mathsf{EV}^{\ast}_{(p,q)}(E)
of
(p,q)
-
(EV^{\ast})
sets is a bornology by (iii) of Lemma
7.2
, for the case
q<p
this class contains only the unique element
\{0\}
as the next assertion shows.

Proposition 7.5

Let
E
be a locally convex space.

(i)

If
1\leq q<p\leq\infty
, then
\mathsf{V}_{(p,q)}^{\ast}(E)=\mathsf{EV}_{(p,q)}^{\ast}(E)=\{0\}
.

(ii)

If
1\leq p\leq q\leq\infty
, then
\mathsf{V}_{(p,q)}^{\ast}(E)
contains all finite subsets of
E
.

Proof 43

(i) By (i) of Lemma
7.2
, we have
\mathsf{V}_{(p,q)}^{\ast}(E)\subseteq\mathsf{EV}_{(p,q)}^{\ast}(E)
. So it suffices to prove that every
(p,q)
-
(EV^{\ast})
set is
\{0\}
. Suppose for a contradiction and some
A\in\mathsf{EV}_{(p,q)}^{\ast}(E)
contains a non-zero element
a
. Taking into account (iii) of Lemma
7.2
we can assume that
A=\mathbb{D}\cdot a
. Since
E
is locally convex, there is a closed subspace
\tilde{E}
of
E
such that
E=\mathsf{span}(a)\oplus{\tilde{E}}
. Fix an arbitrary
\chi\in E^{\prime}
such that
\chi\in{\tilde{E}}^{\perp}
and
\langle\chi,a\rangle=1
. Take positive numbers
t,s
such that
q<t<s<p
. For every
n\in\omega
, let
\chi_{n}:=\tfrac{\chi}{(n+1)^{1/t}}
. It is clear that the sequence
\{\chi_{n}\}_{n\in\omega}
is equicontinuous. For every
\eta\in E^{\prime\prime}
, we have

\sum_{n\in\omega}|\langle\eta,\chi_{n}\rangle|^{s}=|\langle\eta,\chi\rangle|^{s}\cdot\sum_{n\in\omega}\tfrac{1}{(n+1)^{s/t}}<\infty

and hence the sequence
\{\chi_{n}\}_{n\in\omega}
is weakly
p
-summable in
E^{\prime}_{\beta}
. On the other hand, since

\sum_{n\in\omega}\Big(\sup_{x\in A}|\langle\chi_{n},x\rangle|\Big)^{q}=\sum_{n\in\omega}|\langle\chi_{n},a\rangle|^{q}=\sum_{n\in\omega}\tfrac{1}{(n+1)^{q/t}}=\infty

we obtain that
A
is not a
(p,q)
-
(EV^{\ast})
set, a contradiction.

(ii) By (iii) of Lemma
7.2
it suffices to show that
A=\{x\}
is a
(p,q)
-
(V^{\ast})
set for every
x\in E
. Let
\{\chi_{n}\}_{n\in\omega}
be a weakly
p
-summable sequence in
E^{\prime}_{\beta}
. Then
\big(\langle\chi_{n},x\rangle\big)\in\ell_{p}
(or
\in c_{0}
if
p=\infty
). Since
p\leq q
it follows that
\big(\sup_{x\in A}|\langle\chi_{n},x\rangle|\big)\in\ell_{q}
(or
\in c_{0}
if
q=\infty
). Thus
A
is a
(p,q)
-
(V^{\ast})
set.∎

It is natural to find some classes of subsets which are
(p,q)
-
(V^{\ast})
sets. Below, under additional assumption on an lcs
E
, we show that any precompact subset
A
of
E
is
p
-
(V^{\ast})
sets.

Proposition 7.6

Let
p\in[1,\infty]
, and let
E
be a locally convex space.

(i)

Every precompact subset
A
of
E
is a
p
-
(EV^{\ast})
set.

(ii)

If
E
is
p
-quasibarrelled, then every precompact subset
A
of
E
is a
p
-
(V^{\ast})
set.

Proof 44

Let
S=\{\chi_{n}\}_{n\in\omega}
be a (resp., equicontinuous) weakly
p
-summable sequence in
E^{\prime}_{\beta}
. If
E
is
p
-quasibarrelled, then
S
is equicontinuous. Therefore in both cases we can assume that
S
is equicontinuous. Since
S
is weakly
p
-summable, it is a weak
∗
null-sequence. Hence, by Proposition 3.9.8 of
[
51
]
, the weak
∗
topology
\sigma(E^{\prime},E)
and the topology
\tau_{pc}
of uniform convergence on precompact subsets of
E
coincide on
S
. Therefore
\chi_{n}\to 0
also in
\tau_{pc}
. As
A
is precompact, we obtain
\sup_{x\in A}|\langle\chi_{n},x\rangle|\to 0
. Thus
A
is a
p
-
(EV^{\ast})
set (resp., a
p
-
(V^{\ast})
set).∎

Setting
p=\infty
in (ii) of Proposition
7.6
we obtain

Corollary 7.7

If
E
is a
\infty
-quasibarrelled space, then every precompact subset of
E
is a
(V^{\ast})
set.

It is natural to consider the next problem:
Characterize locally convex spaces
E
for which every bounded set is a
(p,q)
-
(V^{\ast})
set, i.e.
\mathsf{Bo}(E)=\mathsf{V}_{(p,q)}^{\ast}(E)
.
Of course, by Proposition
7.5
, the problem has sense only in the case
p\leq q
. For the case
q=\infty
, a complete solution of this problem is obtained in the next assertion, see also Corollary
14.2
below.

Theorem 7.8

Let
p\in[1,\infty]
, and let
E
be a locally convex space. Then
\mathsf{Bo}(E)=\mathsf{V}_{p}^{\ast}(E)
if and only if
E^{\prime}_{\beta}
has the
p
-Schur property.

Proof 45

Assume that
\mathsf{Bo}(E)=\mathsf{V}_{p}^{\ast}(E)
. To show that
E^{\prime}_{\beta}
has the
p
-Schur property, fix an arbitrary weakly
p
-summable sequence
\{\chi_{n}\}_{n\in\omega}
in
E^{\prime}_{\beta}
. To show that
\chi_{n}\to 0
in
E^{\prime}_{\beta}
, let
B\in\mathsf{Bo}(E)
. Then
B
is a
p
-
(V^{\ast})
set in
E
, and hence

\lim_{n\to\infty}\sup_{b\in B}\big|\langle\chi_{n},b\rangle\big|=0

and therefore
\chi_{n}\in B^{\circ}
for all sufficiently large
n\in\omega
. Since
B
was arbitrary this means that
\chi_{n}\to 0
in
E^{\prime}_{\beta}
, as desired.

Conversely, assume that
E^{\prime}_{\beta}
has the
p
-Schur property. Let
B\in\mathsf{Bo}(E)
. To show that
B
is a
p
-
(V^{\ast})
set in
E
, take any weakly
p
-summable sequence
\{\chi_{n}\}_{n\in\omega}
in
E^{\prime}_{\beta}
. Since
E^{\prime}_{\beta}
has the
p
-Schur property, we obtain that for every
\varepsilon>0
, there is
N_{\varepsilon}\in\omega
such that
\chi_{n}\in\varepsilon B^{\circ}
for all
n\geq N_{\varepsilon}
. Therefore
\sup_{b\in B}\big|\langle\chi_{n},b\rangle\big|\leq\varepsilon
for all
n\geq N_{\varepsilon}
.
As
\varepsilon
was arbitrary we obtain that
\sup_{b\in B}\big|\langle\chi_{n},b\rangle\big|\to 0
. Thus
B
is a
p
-
(V^{\ast})
set.∎

To obtain the equalities
\mathsf{V}_{(p,q)}^{\ast}(E)=\mathsf{EV}_{(p,q)}^{\ast}(E)=\mathsf{Bo}(E)
for some (in particular, function) spaces we need the next result.
Recall that an lcs
E
is called
feral
if every bounded subset of
E
is finite-dimensional.

Proposition 7.9

A locally convex space
E
carries its weak topology and is quasibarrelled if and only if
E^{\prime}_{\beta}
is feral.

Proof 46

Assume that
E
carries its weak topology and is quasibarrelled. If
B
is a bounded subset of
E^{\prime}_{\beta}
, then
B
is equicontinuous. Since
E
carries its weak topology, there is a finite subset
F
of
E^{\prime}
such that
B\subseteq(F^{\circ})^{\circ}
and hence
B
is contained in the finite-dimensional subspace
\mathsf{span}(F)
of
E^{\prime}
.

Conversely, assume that
E^{\prime}_{\beta}
is feral. Then every bounded subset of
E^{\prime}_{\beta}
, being finite-dimensional, is trivially equicontinuous. Therefore
E
is quasibarrelled. In particular,
E
is a subspace of
E^{\prime\prime}=(E^{\prime}_{\beta})^{\prime}_{\beta}
. But since all bounded subsets of
E^{\prime}_{\beta}
are finite-dimensional it follows that
E^{\prime\prime}
carries its weak
∗
topology. Thus
E
carries its weak topology.∎

Proposition 7.10

Let
1\leq p\leq q\leq\infty
, and let
E
be a locally convex space whose strong dual is feral. Then
\mathsf{V}_{(p,q)}^{\ast}(E)=\mathsf{EV}_{(p,q)}^{\ast}(E)=\mathsf{Bo}(E)
.

Proof 47

Since any weakly
p
-summable sequence
\{\chi_{n}\}_{n\in\omega}
in
E^{\prime}_{\beta}
is weakly bounded, the ferality of
E^{\prime}_{\beta}
implies that
\{\chi_{n}\}
is finite-dimensional. Then, by Lemma
4.13
, there are linearly independent elements
\eta_{1},\dots,\eta_{s}\in E^{\prime}
and sequences
(a_{1,n}),\dots,(a_{s,n})\in\ell_{p}
(or
\in c_{0}
if
p=\infty
) such that

\chi_{n}=a_{1,n}\eta_{1}+\cdots+a_{s,n}\eta_{s}\;\;\mbox{ for every $n\in\omega$}.

Now, let
A
be a bounded subset of
E
. Then

\sup_{a\in A}|\langle\chi_{n},a\rangle|\leq\sum_{i=1}^{s}|a_{i,n}|\cdot\sup_{a\in A}|\langle\eta_{i},a\rangle|

and hence the inequality
p\leq q
implies
\big(\sup_{a\in A}|\langle\chi_{n},a\rangle|\big)_{n}\in\ell_{q}
(or
\in c_{0}
if
q=\infty
). Therefore
A
is a
(p,q)
-
(V^{\ast})
set, and hence
\mathsf{Bo}(E)\subseteq\mathsf{V}_{(p,q)}^{\ast}(E)
. As, by Lemma
7.2
, the inclusions
\mathsf{V}_{(p,q)}^{\ast}(E)\subseteq\mathsf{EV}_{(p,q)}^{\ast}(E)\subseteq\mathsf{Bo}(E)
are satisfied for every locally convex space we obtain
\mathsf{V}_{(p,q)}^{\ast}(E)=\mathsf{EV}_{(p,q)}^{\ast}(E)=\mathsf{Bo}(E)
.∎

Since the space
C_{p}(X)
carries its weak topology and is quasibarrelled for every Tychonoff space
X
, Propositions
7.9
and
7.10
immediately imply the next assertion.

Corollary 7.11

Let
1\leq p\leq q\leq\infty
,
X
be a Tychonoff space, and let
E
be a linear subspace of
\mathbb{F}^{X}
containing
C_{p}(X)
. Then
\mathsf{V}_{(p,q)}^{\ast}(E)=\mathsf{EV}_{(p,q)}^{\ast}(E)=\mathsf{Bo}(E)
.

It is well known (see
[
27
, p. 477]
) that every
(V^{\ast})
