subset of a Banach space is weakly sequentially precompact. However this result is not true for general locally convex spaces.

Example 7.12

The space
\mathbb{R}^{\mathfrak{c}}
contains a uniformly bounded sequence
S=\{f_{n}\}_{n\in\omega}
which is a
(p,q)
-
(V^{\ast})
set for all
1\leq p\leq q\leq\infty
but is not (weakly) sequentially precompact.

Proof 48

Consider the compact space
3^{\omega}
with the discrete topology, so
\mathbb{R}^{3^{\omega}}=\mathbb{R}^{\mathfrak{c}}
. Let
S=\{f_{n}\}_{n\in\omega}
be the sequence defined in Lemma
2.2
, i.e.,
f_{n}
is the projection of
3^{\omega}
onto the
n
th coordinate. It follows from Corollary
7.11
that
S
is
(p,q)
-
(V^{\ast})
subset of
\mathbb{R}^{\mathfrak{c}}
for all
1\leq p\leq q\leq\infty
. To show that
S
is not sequentially precompact, suppose the converse and for some infinite subset
I
of
\omega
, the sequence
\{f_{n}\}_{n\in I}
is Cauchy in
\mathbb{R}^{\mathfrak{c}}
. Choose an arbitrary infinite subset
J
of
I
such that
I{\setminus}J
is infinite. Definite
h\in 3^{\omega}
by

h(n):=\left\{\begin{aligned} 1,&\;\mbox{ if }n\in J,\\
0,&\;\mbox{ if }n\in\omega{\setminus}J,\end{aligned}\right.

and let
e_{h}:\mathbb{R}^{\mathfrak{c}}\to\mathbb{R}
be the evaluation functional at
h
, i.e.,
\langle e_{h},f\rangle:=f(h)
for every function
f\in\mathbb{R}^{\mathfrak{c}}
. By assumption,
\{f_{n}\}_{n\in I}
is Cauchy and hence there is a finite subset
F
of
I
such that

|\langle e_{h},f_{i}\rangle-\langle e_{h},f_{j}\rangle|<\tfrac{1}{4}\;\;\mbox{ for all }\;i,j\in I{\setminus}F.

However, if
i\in I{\setminus}(F\cup J)
and
j\in J{\setminus}F
we obtain

\langle e_{h},f_{i}\rangle=f_{i}(h)=0\;\mbox{ and }\;\langle e_{h},f_{j}\rangle=f_{j}(h)=1

and hence
|\langle e_{h},f_{i}\rangle-\langle e_{h},f_{j}\rangle|=1>\tfrac{1}{4}
, a contradiction.∎

Theorem 7.13

Let
1\leq p\leq q\leq\infty
. For a locally convex space
E
the following assertions are equivalent:

(i)

every
(p,q)
-
(V^{\ast})
subset
(
resp.,
(p,q)
-
(EV^{\ast})
-subset
)
of
E
is precompact;

(ii)

each operator
T:L\to E
from an lcs
L
to
E
which transforms bounded subsets of
L
to
(p,q)
-
(V^{\ast})
subsets
(
resp.,
(p,q)
-
(EV^{\ast})
-subset
)
of
E
, transforms bounded subsets of
L
to precompact subsets of
E
;

(iii)

as in
(ii)
with a normed space
L
.

If in addition
E
is locally complete, then
(i)–(iii)
are equivalent to

(iv)

as in
(ii)
with a Banach space
L
.

Proof 49

(i)
\Rightarrow
(ii) Let
T:L\to E
be an operator which transforms bounded subsets of an lcs
L
to
(p,q)
-
(V^{\ast})
(resp.,
(p,q)
-
(EV^{\ast})
) subsets of
E
. Let
A
be a bounded subset of
L
. Then
T(A)
is a
(p,q)
-
(V^{\ast})
(resp.,
(p,q)
-
(EV^{\ast})
) subset of
E
, and hence, by (i),
T(A)
is precompact. Thus
T
transforms bounded subsets of
L
to precompact subsets of
E
.

(ii)
\Rightarrow
(iii) and (ii)
\Rightarrow
(iv) are trivial.

(iii)
\Rightarrow
(i) and (iv)
\Rightarrow
(i): Fix a
(p,q)
-
(V^{\ast})
(resp.,
(p,q)
-
(EV^{\ast})
) subset
A
of
E
. By Lemma
7.2
, without loss of generality we can assume that
A=A^{\circ\circ}
. Consider the normed space
E_{A}
(if
E
is locally complete, then
E_{A}
is a Banach space), and recall that the closed unit ball
B
of
E_{A}
is exactly
A
. By Proposition
3.6
, the identity inclusion
T:E_{A}\to E
is continuous and the set
T(B)=A
is a
(p,q)
-
(V^{\ast})
set. Since any bounded subset of
E_{A}
is contained in some
aB
,
a>0
, Lemma
7.2
implies that
T
transforms bounded subsets of the normed (resp., Banach) space
E_{B}
to
(p,q)
-
(V^{\ast})
(resp.,
(p,q)
-
(EV^{\ast})
) subsets of
E
. Therefore, by (iii) and (iv), the set
A=T(B)
is precompact.∎

8
(V)
type subsets of locally convex spaces

Below we generalize the notion of a
p
-
(V)
subset of a Banach space given in Definition
1.4
.

Definition 8.1

Let
p,q\in[1,\infty]
, and let
E
be a separated topological vector space. A non-empty subset
B
of
E^{\prime}
is called a
(p,q)
-
(V)
set if

\Big(\sup_{\chi\in B}|\langle\chi,x_{n}\rangle|\Big)\in\ell_{q}\;\mbox{ if $q<\infty$, }\;\mbox{ or }\;\;\Big(\sup_{\chi\in B}|\langle\chi,x_{n}\rangle|\Big)\in c_{0}\;\mbox{ if $q=\infty$},

for every weakly
p
-summable sequence
\{x_{n}\}_{n\in\omega}
in
E
.
(p,\infty)
-
(V)
sets and
(1,\infty)
-
(V)
sets will be called simply
p
-
(V)
sets and
(V)
sets, respectively. If in addition the set
B
is equicontinuous, it is called a
(p,q)
-
(EV)
set, a
p
-
(EV)
set or a
(EV)
set, respectively.∎

Lemma 8.2

Let
p,q\in[1,\infty]
, and let
(E,\tau)
be a locally convex space. Then:

(i)

each
(p,q)
-
(EV)
set in
E^{\prime}
is a strongly bounded
(p,q)
-
(V)
set; if in addition
E
is barrelled, then every
(p,q)
-
(V)
set is a
(p,q)
-
(EV)
set;

(ii)

every
(p,q)
-
(V)
set in
E^{\prime}
is weak
∗
bounded;

(iii)

the family of all
(p,q)
-
(V)

(
resp.,
(p,q)
-
(EV)
)
sets in
E^{\prime}
is closed under taking subsets, finite unions, finite sums, and closed absolutely convex hulls in
E^{\prime}_{w^{\ast}}
;

(iv)

if
T:E\to L
is an operator to an lcs
L
and
B
is a
(p,q)
-
(V)

(
resp.,
(p,q)
-
(EV)
)
set in
L^{\prime}_{\beta}
, then
T^{\ast}(B)
is a
(p,q)
-
(V)

(
resp.,
(p,q)
-
(EV)
)
set in
E^{\prime}_{\beta}
;

(v)

a subset
B
of
E^{\prime}
is a
(p,q)
-
(V)
set if and only if every countable subset of
B
is a
(p,q)
-
(V)
set;

(vi)

if
p^{\prime},q^{\prime}\in[1,\infty]
are such that
p^{\prime}\leq p
and
q\leq q^{\prime}
, then every
(p,q)
-
(V)

(
resp.,
(p,q)
-
(EV)
)
set in
E^{\prime}
is also a
(p^{\prime},q^{\prime})
-
(V)

(
resp.,
(p^{\prime},q^{\prime})
-
(EV)
)
set; in particular, any
(p,q)
-
(V)

(
resp.,
(p,q)
-
(EV)
)
is a
(V)

(
resp.,
(EV)
)
set;

(vii)

the property of being a
(p,q)
-
(V)
set depends only on the duality
(E,E^{\prime})
, i.e., if
\mathcal{T}
is a locally convex vector topology on
E
compatible with the topology
\tau
of
E
, then the
(p,q)
-
(V)
sets of
(E,\mathcal{T})^{\prime}
are exactly the
(p,q)
-
(V)
sets of
E^{\prime}
;

(viii)

every
(p,q)
-
(V)
set in
E^{\prime}
is a
(p,q)
-
(V^{\ast})
set in
E^{\prime}_{w^{\ast}}
; the converse is true if
E
is barrelled;

(ix)

if
H
is a dense subspace of
E
, then every
(p,q)
-
(V)

(
resp.,
(p,q)
-
(EV)
)
set in
E^{\prime}
is a
(p,q)
-
(V)

(
resp.,
(p,q)
-
(EV)
)
set in
H^{\prime}
;

(x)

if
E
is additionally quasibarrelled, then every
(p,q)
-
(V^{\ast})
set in
E^{\prime}_{\beta}
is a
(p,q)
-
(V)
set.

Proof 50

(i) and (iii) are clear, and (ix) follows from (iv) applied to the identity inclusion
\mathsf{id}_{H}:H\to E
and the algebraic equality
H^{\prime}=E^{\prime}
.

(ii) Let
B
be a
(p,q)
-
(V)
set in
E^{\prime}
and suppose for a contradiction that it is not weak
∗
bounded. Then there is
x\in E
such that for every
n\in\omega
, there exists
b_{n}\in B
for which
|\langle b_{n},x\rangle|>(n+1)^{3}
. For every
n\in\omega
, set
x_{n}:=\tfrac{1}{(n+1)^{2}}\cdot x
. Then
\{x_{n}\}_{n\in\omega}
is a weakly
p
-summable sequence in
E
. However, since

\sup_{b\in B}|\langle b,x_{n}\rangle|\geq|\langle b_{n},x_{n}\rangle|>n+1\to\infty

it follows that
B
is not a
(p,q)
-
(V)
set, a contradiction.

(iv) First we note that the adjoint operator
T^{\ast}:L^{\prime}_{\beta}\to E^{\prime}_{\beta}
is continuous, and hence if
B\subseteq L^{\prime}
is equicontinuous then so is its image
T^{\ast}(B)
. Therefore it suffices to consider only the case when
B
is a
(p,q)
-
(V)
set in
L^{\prime}
. Fix a weakly
p
-summable sequence
\{x_{n}\}_{n\in\omega}
in
E
. Then, by Lemma
4.6
(iii), the sequence
\{T(x_{n})\}
is weakly
p
-summable in
L
. Therefore

\Big(\sup_{\chi\in B}|\langle T^{\ast}(\chi),x_{n}\rangle|\Big)=\Big(\sup_{\chi\in B}|\langle\chi,T(x_{n})\rangle|\Big)\in\ell_{q}\;\mbox{ (or }\;\in c_{0}\;\mbox{ if $q=\infty$}),

which means that
T^{\ast}(B)
is a
(p,q)
-
(V)
set in
E^{\prime}
.

(v) The necessity follows from (iii). To prove the sufficiency suppose for a contradiction that
B
is not a
(p,q)
-
(V)
set in
E
. Then there is a weakly
p
-summable sequence
\{x_{n}\}_{n\in\omega}
in
E
such that

\Big(\sup_{\chi\in B}|\langle\chi,x_{n}\rangle|\Big)\not\in\ell_{q}\;\mbox{ if $q<\infty$, }\;\mbox{ or }\;\;\Big(\sup_{\chi\in B}|\langle\chi,x_{n}\rangle|\Big)\not\in c_{0}\;\mbox{ if $q=\infty$}.

Assume that
q<\infty
(the case
q=\infty
can be considered analogously). For every
n\in\omega
, choose
\chi_{n}\in B
such that
|\langle\chi_{n},x_{n}\rangle|\geq\tfrac{1}{2}\cdot\sup_{a\in A}|\langle\chi_{n},a\rangle|
. Then

\sum_{n\in\omega}|\langle\chi_{n},x_{n}\rangle|^{q}\geq\tfrac{1}{2^{q}}\sum_{n\in\omega}\big(\sup_{\chi\in B}|\langle\chi,x_{n}\rangle|\big)^{q}=\infty.

Thus the countable subset
\{\chi_{n}\}_{n\in\omega}
of
B
is not a
(p,q)
-
(V)
set in
E^{\prime}
, a contradiction.

(vi) Take any weakly
p^{\prime}
-summable sequence
\{x_{n}\}_{n\in\omega}
in
E
. Since
p^{\prime}\leq p
,
\{x_{n}\}
is also weakly
p
-summable and hence
\big(\sup_{\chi\in B}|\langle\chi,x_{n}\rangle|\big)\in\ell_{q}
(or
\in c_{0}
if
q=\infty
). It remains to note that
\ell_{q}\subseteq\ell_{q^{\prime}}
because
q\leq q^{\prime}
.

(vii) immediately follows from (iv) of Lemma
4.6
.

(viii) Let
B
be a
(p,q)
-
(V)
set in
E^{\prime}
. Then for every weakly
p
-summable sequence
\{x_{n}\}_{n\in\omega}
in
E
we have
\big(\sup_{\chi\in B}|\langle\chi,x_{n}\rangle|\big)\in\ell_{q}
(or
\in c_{0}
if
q=\infty
). Observe that
\big(E^{\prime}_{w^{\ast}}\big)^{\prime}_{\beta}=E_{\beta}
. By (ii) of Lemma
4.6
, we have
\ell_{p}^{w}(E_{\beta})\subseteq\ell_{p}^{w}(E)
. Therefore also for every weakly
p
-summable sequence
(y_{n})\in\ell_{p}^{w}(E_{\beta})
we have
\big(\sup_{\chi\in B}|\langle\chi,y_{n}\rangle|\big)\in\ell_{q}
(or
\in c_{0}
if
q=\infty
). Thus
B
is a
(p,q)
-
(V^{\ast})
set in
E^{\prime}_{w^{\ast}}
. The last assertion follows from the equality
E_{\beta}=E
if
E
is barrelled.

(x) Let
B
be a
(p,q)
-
(V^{\ast})
set in
E^{\prime}_{\beta}
. To show that
B
is also a
(p,q)
-
(V)
set, let
\{x_{n}\}_{n\in\omega}
be a weakly
p
-summable sequence in
E
. Since
E
is quasibarrelled, the canonical map
J_{E}:E\to E^{\prime\prime}=(E^{\prime}_{\beta})^{\prime}_{\beta}
is continuous. Therefore the sequence
\{J_{E}(x_{n})\}_{n\in\omega}
is weakly
p
-summable in
E^{\prime\prime}
by Lemma
4.6
(iii). Since
B
is a
(p,q)
-
(V^{\ast})
set in
E^{\prime}_{\beta}
and
\sup_{\chi\in B}|\langle J_{E}(x_{n}),\chi\rangle|=\sup_{\chi\in B}|\langle\chi,x_{n}\rangle|
, it follows that
\big(\sup_{\chi\in B}|\langle\chi,x_{n}\rangle|\big)\in\ell_{q}
(or
\in c_{0}
if
q=\infty
). Thus
B
is a
(p,q)
-
(V)
set in
E^{\prime}
.∎

Notation 8.3

The family of all
(p,q)
-
(V)
sets (resp.
p
-
(V)
sets,
(p,q)
-
(EV)
sets,
(V)
sets etc.) in
E^{\prime}
of an lcs
E
is denoted by
\mathsf{V}_{(p,q)}(E^{\prime})
(resp.
\mathsf{V}_{p}(E^{\prime})
,
\mathsf{EV}_{(p,q)}(E^{\prime})
,
\mathsf{V}(E^{\prime})
etc.).∎

Now we consider
(p,q)
-
(V)
sets in topological products and direct sums.

Proposition 8.4

Let
p,q\in[1,\infty]
, and let
\{E_{i}\}_{i\in I}
be a non-empty family of locally convex spaces. Then:

(i)

a subset
K
of
\big(\prod_{i\in I}E_{i}\big)^{\prime}
is a
(p,q)
-
(V)
set
(
resp., a
(p,q)
-
(EV)
set
)
if and only if so are all its coordinate projections and the support of
K
is finite;

(ii)

a subset
K
of
\big(\bigoplus_{i\in I}E_{i}\big)^{\prime}
is a
(p,q)
-
(V)
set if and only if so are all its coordinate projections; if
I
is countable, then the same is true for
(p,q)
-
(EV)
sets.

Proof 51

To prove the necessity in both cases (i) and (ii), let
E=\prod_{i\in I}E_{i}
or
E=\bigoplus_{i\in I}E_{i}
.
Fix
j\in I
, and let
K_{j}
be the projection of
K
onto the
j
th coordinate. To show that
K_{j}
is a
(p,q)
-
(V)
set (resp., a
(p,q)
-
(EV)
set) in
(E_{j})^{\prime}
, let
T_{j}:E_{j}\to E
be the identity embedding. Then, by (iv) of Lemma
8.2
,
T_{j}^{\ast}(K)=K_{j}
is a
(p,q)
-
(V)
set (resp., a
(p,q)
-
(EV)
set) in
(E_{j})^{\prime}
, as desired.

In the case (i) we need to check also that the support of
K
is finite. Suppose for a contradiction that
K_{i}\not=\{0_{i}\}
for infinitely many indices
i
. Then we can choose a sequence
\{\chi_{n}\}_{n\in\omega}
in
K
such that

\mathrm{supp}(\chi_{0})\not=\emptyset\;\mbox{ and }\;\mathrm{supp}(\chi_{n+1}){\setminus}\bigcup_{j\leq n}\mathrm{supp}(\chi_{j})\not=\emptyset\mbox{ for all }n\in\omega,

and hence we can choose a sequence
\{i_{n}\}_{n\in\omega}
in
I
such that

i_{0}\in\mathrm{supp}(\chi_{0})\;\mbox{ and }\;i_{n+1}\in\mathrm{supp}(\chi_{n+1}){\setminus}\bigcup_{j\leq n}\mathrm{supp}(\chi_{j})\;\mbox{ for all $n\in\omega$.}

(8.1)

By induction on
n
, for every
n\in\omega
, choose
y_{i_{n}}\in E_{i_{n}}
such that

|\langle\chi_{n}(i_{n}),y_{i_{n}}\rangle|\geq 1+\sum_{k<n}|\langle\chi_{n}(i_{k}),y_{i_{k}}\rangle|

(8.2)

and define
x_{n}=(x_{i,n})\in E
by
x_{i,n}=y_{i_{n}}
if
i=i_{n}
, and
x_{i,n}=0
otherwise. Since every
\chi\in E^{\prime}
has finite support, we obtain that
\langle\chi,x_{n}\rangle=0
for all but finitely many
n\in\omega
. Therefore the sequence
\{x_{n}\}_{n\in\omega}
is weakly
p
-summable in
E
. As, by (
8.1
) and (
8.2
),

\sup_{\chi\in K}|\langle\chi,x_{n}\rangle|\geq|\langle\chi_{n},x_{n}\rangle|\geq|\langle\chi_{n}(i_{n}),y_{i_{n}}\rangle|-\sum_{k<n}|\langle\chi_{n}(i_{k}),y_{i_{k}}\rangle|\geq 1\not\to 0

it follows that
K
is not a
(p,q)
-
(V)
set, a contradiction. Thus the support of
K
is finite, as desired.

To prove the sufficiency, let
K
be a subset of
E^{\prime}
such that each projection
K_{i}
of
K
is a
(p,q)
-
(V)
set (resp., a
(p,q)
-
(EV)
set) in
E^{\prime}_{i}
, and, for the case (i),
K_{i}=\{0\}
for all but finitely many indices
i\in I
. We distinguish between the cases (i) and (ii).

(i) Let
F\subseteq I
be the finite support of
K
. Take an arbitrary weakly
p
-summable sequence
\{x_{n}\}_{n\in\omega}
in
E
, where
x_{n}=(x_{i,n})_{i\in I}
. Observe that for every
i\in I
, the sequence
\{x_{i,n}\}_{n\in\omega}
is weakly
p
-summable in
E_{i}
. Then

\sup_{\chi\in K}|\langle\chi,x_{n}\rangle|=\sup_{\chi\in K}\big|\sum_{i\in F}\langle\chi(i),x_{i,n}\rangle\big|\leq\sum_{i\in F}\sup_{\chi(i)\in K_{i}}|\langle\chi(i),x_{i,n}\rangle|

and hence
\big(\sup_{\chi\in K}|\langle\chi,x_{n}\rangle|\big)\in\ell_{q}
(or
\in c_{0}
if
q=\infty
). Thus
K
is a
(p,q)
-
(V)
set in
E^{\prime}
.

If all
K_{i}
are equicontinuous, then also
K
is equicontinuous since
F
is finite. Therefore
K
is a
(p,q)
-
(EV)
set in
E^{\prime}
.

(ii) Take an arbitrary weakly
p
-summable sequence
\{x_{n}\}_{n\in\omega}
in
E
, where
x_{n}=(x_{i,n})_{i\in I}
. Since
\{x_{n}\}_{n\in\omega}
is a bounded subset of
E
, it has finite support, i.e., for some finite set
F\subseteq I
it follows that
x_{i,n}=0
for all
n\in\omega
and each
i\in I{\setminus}F
. Observe also that for every
i\in I
, the sequence
\{x_{i,n}\}_{n\in\omega}
is weakly
p
-summable. Then

\sup_{\chi\in K}|\langle\chi,x_{n}\rangle|=\sup_{\chi\in K}\big|\sum_{i\in F}\langle\chi(i),x_{i,n}\rangle\big|\leq\sum_{i\in F}\sup_{\chi(i)\in K_{i}}|\langle\chi(i),x_{i,n}\rangle|

and hence
\big(\sup_{\chi\in K}|\langle\chi,x_{n}\rangle|\big)\in\ell_{q}
(or
\in c_{0}
if
q=\infty
). Thus
K
is a
(p,q)
-
(V)
set in
E^{\prime}
.

Assume that
I=\omega
is countable, and all
K_{i}
are equicontinuous, so that
K_{i}\subseteq U_{i}^{\circ}
for some
U_{i}\in\mathcal{N}_{0}(E_{i})
. Set
U:=\bigoplus_{i\in\omega}\tfrac{1}{2^{i+1}}U_{i}
. Then
U\in\mathcal{N}_{0}(E)
and for every
\chi=(\chi_{i})\in K
and each
x=(x_{i})\in U
, we have

|\langle\chi,x\rangle|=\Big|\sum_{i\in\mathrm{supp}(x)}\langle\chi_{i},x_{i}\rangle\Big|\leq\sum_{i\in\omega}\tfrac{1}{2^{i+1}}=1.

Therefore
K\subseteq U^{\circ}
and hence
K
is equicontinuous. Thus
K
is a
(p,q)
-
(EV)
set in
E^{\prime}
. ∎

Analogously to
(p,q)
-
(V)
sets we define

Definition 8.5

Let
p,q\in[1,\infty]
, and let
E
be a separated tvs. A subset
B
of
E^{\prime}
is called a weak
∗

(p,q)
-
(V)
set if it is a
(p,q)
-
(V^{\ast})
set in
E^{\prime}_{w^{\ast}}
, i.e.,

\Big(\sup_{\chi\in B}|\langle\chi,x_{n}\rangle|\Big)\in\ell_{q}\;\mbox{ if $q<\infty$, }\;\mbox{ or }\;\;\Big(\sup_{\chi\in B}|\langle\chi,x_{n}\rangle|\Big)\in c_{0}\;\mbox{ if $q=\infty$},

for every weakly
p
-summable sequence
\{x_{n}\}_{n\in\omega}
in
\big(E^{\prime}_{w^{\ast}}\big)^{\prime}_{\beta}=E_{\beta}
. Weak
∗

(p,\infty)
-
(V)
sets and weak
∗

(\infty,\infty)
-
(V)
sets will be called simply weak
∗

p
-
(V)
sets and weak
∗

(V)
sets, respectively. If in addition the set
B
is equicontinuous, it is called a weak
∗

(p,q)
-
(EV)
set, a weak
∗

p
-
(EV)
set or a weak
∗

(EV)
set, respectively.∎

In the next lemma, for further references, we summarize the main properties of weak
∗

(p,q)
-
(V)
sets and weak
∗

(p,q)
-
(EV)
sets.

Lemma 8.6

Let
p,q\in[1,\infty]
, and let
(E,\tau)
be a locally convex space. Then:

(i)

each weak
∗

(p,q)
-
(EV)
set is a weak
∗

(p,q)
-
(V)
set, the converse is true if
E
is barrelled;

(ii)

every weak
∗

(p,q)
-
(V)
set in
E^{\prime}
is weak
∗
bounded;

(iii)

the family of all weak
∗

(p,q)
-
(V)

(
resp., weak
∗

(p,q)
-
(EV)
)
sets in
E^{\prime}
is closed under taking subsets, finite unions, finite sums, and closed absolutely convex hulls in
E^{\prime}_{w^{\ast}}
;

(iv)

if
T:E\to L
is an operator to an lcs
L
and
B
is a weak
∗

(p,q)
-
(V)

(
resp., weak
∗

(p,q)
-
(EV)
)
set in
L^{\prime}
, then
T^{\ast}(B)
is a weak
∗

(p,q)
-
(V)
set
(
resp., weak
∗

(p,q)
-
(EV)
)
set in
E^{\prime}
;

(v)

a subset
B
of
E^{\prime}
is a weak
∗

(p,q)
-
(V)
set if and only if every countable subset of
B
is a weak
∗

(p,q)
-
(V)
set in
E^{\prime}
;

(vi)

if
p^{\prime},q^{\prime}\in[1,\infty]
are such that
p^{\prime}\leq p
and
q\leq q^{\prime}
, then every weak
∗

(p,q)
-
(V)

(
resp., weak
∗

(p,q)
-
(EV)
)
set in
E^{\prime}
is also a
(p^{\prime},q^{\prime})
-
(V)

(
resp., weak
∗

(p^{\prime},q^{\prime})
-
(EV)
)
set; in particular, any weak
∗

(p,q)
-
(V)

(
resp., weak
∗

(p,q)
-
(EV)
)
set is a weak
∗

1
-
(V)

(
resp., weak
∗

1
-
(EV)
)
set;

(vii)

the property of being a weak
∗

(p,q)
-
(V)
set depends only on the duality
(E,E^{\prime})
, i.e., if
\mathcal{T}
is a locally convex vector topology on
E
compatible with the topology
\tau
of
E
, then the weak
∗

(p,q)
-
(V)
sets of
(E,\mathcal{T})^{\prime}
are exactly the weak
∗

(p,q)
-
(V)
sets of
E^{\prime}
;

(viii)

every
(p,q)
-
(V)
set in
E^{\prime}
is a weak
∗

(p,q)
-
(V)
set; the converse assertion is true if
E
is a barrelled space;

(ix)

if
H
is a dense subspace of
E
, then every weak
∗

(p,q)
-
(V)
set in
E^{\prime}
is a weak
∗

(p,q)
-
(V)
set in
H^{\prime}
.

Proof 52

(i) is clear and the converse assertion follows from (ii) and the barrelledness of
E
. The clause (ii) follows from (ii) of Lemma
7.2
, and (iii) follows from (iii) of Lemma
7.2
and the easy fact that subsets, finite unions, and closed absolutely convex hulls of equicontinuous subsets of
E^{\prime}
are equicontinuous. Since the adjoint map
T^{\ast}
is weak
∗
-weak
∗
continuous (Theorem 8.10.5 of
[
67
]
), (iv) follows from (iv) of Lemma
7.2
. The clauses (v)-(vii) follow from (v)-(vii) of Lemma
7.2
. The clause (ix) follows from (iv) applied to the identity inclusion
T=\mathsf{id}_{H}:H\to E
.

(viii) Let
B\subseteq E^{\prime}
be a
(p,q)
-
(V)
set, and let
\{x_{n}\}_{n\in\omega}
be a weakly
p
-summable sequence in
\big(E^{\prime}_{w^{\ast}}\big)^{\prime}_{\beta}=E_{\beta}
. Since the strong topology
\beta(E,E^{\prime})
is finer than the original topology of
E
, it follows that
\{x_{n}\}_{n\in\omega}
is a weakly
p
-summable sequence in
E
. Therefore
\big(\sup_{\chi\in B}|\langle\chi,x_{n}\rangle|\big)\in\ell_{q}
if
q<\infty
, or
\big(\sup_{\chi\in B}|\langle\chi,x_{n}\rangle|\big)\in c_{0}
if
q=\infty
. This means that
B
is a weak
∗

(p,q)
-
(V)
set in
E^{\prime}
. The converse assertion is true for barrelled spaces because
E_{\beta}=E
.∎

Notation 8.7

The family of all weak
∗

(p,q)
-
(V)
sets (resp. weak
∗

p
-
(V)
sets, weak
∗

(p,q)
-
(EV)
sets, weak
∗

(V)
sets etc.) in
E^{\prime}
of an lcs
E
is denoted by
\mathsf{V}_{(p,q)}^{\ast}(E^{\prime}_{w^{\ast}})
(resp.
\mathsf{V}_{p}^{\ast}(E^{\prime}_{w^{\ast}})
,
\mathsf{EV}_{(p,q)}^{\ast}(E^{\prime}_{w^{\ast}})
,
\mathsf{V}^{\ast}(E^{\prime}_{w^{\ast}})
etc.).∎

The condition of being a barrelled space in (viii) of Lemma
8.6
is essential as the following example shows, in which the space
\varphi
endowed with the pointwise topology induced from
\mathbb{F}^{\omega}
is denoted by
\varphi_{p}
.

Example 8.8

Let
1\leq p\leq q\leq\infty
, and let
E=\varphi_{p}
. Then:

(i)

E^{\prime}_{w^{\ast}}=E
and
E_{\beta}=\varphi
, in particular,
E
is not barrelled;

(ii)

each weakly
p
-summable sequence in
E_{\beta}
is finite-dimensional, so
\ell^{w}_{p}(E_{\beta})\subsetneq\ell^{w}_{p}(E)
;

(iii)

every weak
∗
bounded subset of
E^{\prime}
is a weak
∗

(p,q)
-
(V)
set, so
\mathsf{Bo}(E^{\prime}_{w^{\ast}})=\mathsf{V}_{(p,q)}^{\ast}(E^{\prime}_{w^{\ast}})
;

(iv)

every
(p,q)
-
(V)
set in
E^{\prime}
is finite-dimensional, so
\mathsf{V}_{(p,q)}(E)\subsetneq\mathsf{V}_{(p,q)}^{\ast}(E^{\prime}_{w^{\ast}})
.

Proof 53

(i) Since
E
is dense in
\mathbb{F}^{\omega}
, we have
E^{\prime}=\varphi
algebraically. Thus
E^{\prime}_{w^{\ast}}=E
.

It is clear that any pointwise bounded subset of
E^{\prime}
is weak
∗
bounded. Then the polars

\Big(\varphi\cap\prod_{i\in\omega}a_{n}\mathbb{D}\Big)^{\circ}\;\;\mbox{ where all }\;\;a_{n}>0,

form a base at zero in
E_{\beta}
. But these sets form also a base at zero in
\varphi
. Thus
E_{\beta}=\varphi
.

Since
E_{\beta}=\varphi\not=E
, the space
E
is not barrelled.

(ii) follows from (i) since any bounded subset (in particular, each weakly
p
-summable sequence) of
\varphi
is finite-dimensional.
Since
\{e_{n}\}_{n\in\omega}
is a weakly
p
-summable sequence in
E
and is infinite-dimensional, we have
\ell^{w}_{p}(E_{\beta})\subsetneq\ell^{w}_{p}(E)
.

(iii) Let
B
be a weak
∗
bounded subset of
E^{\prime}
. Let
S=\{x_{n}\}_{n\in\omega}
be a weakly
p
-summable sequence in
E_{\beta}
. By (ii),
S
is finite-dimensional. Therefore there are
s\in\omega
and scalars
a_{0,n},\dots,a_{s,n}
such that

x_{n}=a_{0,n}e_{0}+\cdots+a_{s,n}e_{s}\;\;\mbox{ for every }\;n\in\omega.

Since
S
is weakly
p
-summable it follows that
(a_{0,n}),\dots,(a_{s,n})\in\ell_{p}
(or
\in c_{0}
if
p=\infty
). Then

\sup_{\chi\in B}|\langle\chi,x_{n}\rangle|\leq\sum_{i=0}^{s}|a_{i,n}|\cdot\sup_{\chi\in B}|\langle\chi,e_{i}\rangle|

and hence the inequality
p\leq q
implies
\big(\sup_{\chi\in B}|\langle\chi_{n},a\rangle|\big)_{n}\in\ell_{q}
(or
\in c_{0}
if
q=\infty
). Therefore
B
is weak
∗

(p,q)
-
(V)
set, and hence
\mathsf{Bo}(E^{\prime}_{w^{\ast}})=\mathsf{V}_{(p,q)}^{\ast}(E^{\prime}_{w^{\ast}})
.

(iv) Suppose for a contradiction that there is an infinite-dimensional
(p,q)
-
(V)
set
B
in
E^{\prime}
. Then we can find a sequence
\{\chi_{n}\}_{n\in\omega}
in
B
such that

m_{n+1}:=\max\big\{\mathrm{supp}(\chi_{n+1})\big\}>\max\Big\{\bigcup_{i\leq n}\mathrm{supp}(\chi_{i})\Big\}.

For every
n\in\omega
, choose
a_{n}\in\mathbb{F}
such that

a_{n}\cdot\chi_{n}(m_{n})>n
and set
x_{n}=a_{n}\cdot e_{m_{n}}\in E
. It is clear that the sequence
\{x_{n}\}_{n\in\omega}
is weakly
p
-summable in
E
. However, since

\sup_{\chi\in B}|\langle\chi,x_{n}\rangle|\geq|\langle\chi_{n},x_{n}\rangle|>n\not\to 0

it follows that
B
is not a
(p,q)
-
(V)
set, a contradiction.

Finally, since every
(p,q)
-
(V)
set in
E^{\prime}
is a weak
∗

(p,q)
-
(V)
set by (viii) of Lemma
8.6
, the strict inclusion
\mathsf{V}_{(p,q)}(E)\subsetneq\mathsf{V}_{(p,q)}^{\ast}(E^{\prime}_{w^{\ast}})
follows from (iii).∎

In Proposition
7.5
we showed that
\mathsf{V}_{(p,q)}^{\ast}(E)=\mathsf{EV}_{(p,q)}^{\ast}(E)=\{0\}
for all
1\leq q<p\leq\infty
. An analogous result holds also for
V
-type sets.

Proposition 8.9

Let
E
be a locally convex space.

(i)

If
1\leq q<p\leq\infty
, then
\mathsf{V}_{(p,q)}(E^{\prime})=\mathsf{EV}_{(p,q)}(E^{\prime})=\mathsf{V}_{(p,q)}(E^{\prime}_{w^{\ast}})=\mathsf{EV}_{(p,q)}(E^{\prime}_{w^{\ast}})=\{0\}
.

(ii)

If
1\leq p\leq q\leq\infty
, then every finite subset of
E^{\prime}
belongs to
\mathsf{V}_{(p,q)}(E^{\prime})\cap\mathsf{EV}_{(p,q)}(E^{\prime})\cap\mathsf{V}_{(p,q)}(E^{\prime}_{w^{\ast}})\cap\mathsf{EV}_{(p,q)}(E^{\prime}_{w^{\ast}})
.

Proof 54

(i) Taking into account (i) of Lemma
8.2
and (i) and (viii) of Lemma
8.6
, it suffices to prove that every weak
∗

(p,q)
-
(V)
set contains only the zero. Suppose for a contradiction and some
B\in\mathsf{V}_{(p,q)}(E^{\prime}_{w^{\ast}})
contains a non-zero element
b
. Taking into account (iii) of Lemma
8.6
we can assume that
B=\mathbb{D}\cdot b
. Since
E^{\prime}_{w^{\ast}}
is locally convex, there is a closed subspace
H
of
E^{\prime}_{w^{\ast}}
such that
E^{\prime}_{w^{\ast}}=\mathsf{span}(b)\oplus H
. Fix an arbitrary
z\in E_{\beta}
such that
x\in H^{\perp}
and
\langle b,z\rangle=1
. Take positive numbers
t,s
such that
q<t<s<p
. For every
n\in\omega
, let
x_{n}:=\tfrac{z}{(n+1)^{1/t}}
. For every
\eta\in(E_{\beta})^{\prime}
, we have

\sum_{n\in\omega}|\langle\eta,x_{n}\rangle|^{s}=|\langle\eta,z\rangle|^{s}\cdot\sum_{n\in\omega}\tfrac{1}{(n+1)^{s/t}}<\infty

and hence the sequence
\{x_{n}\}_{n\in\omega}
is weakly
p
-summable in
E_{\beta}
. On the other hand, since

\sum_{n\in\omega}\Big(\sup_{\chi\in B}|\langle\chi,x_{n}\rangle|\Big)^{q}=\sum_{n\in\omega}|\langle b,x_{n}\rangle|^{q}=\sum_{n\in\omega}\tfrac{1}{(n+1)^{q/t}}=\infty

we obtain that
B
is not a weak
∗

(p,q)
-
(V)
set, a contradiction.

(ii) Taking into account (i) and (iii) of Lemma
8.2
and (i) and (viii) of Lemma
8.6
, it suffices to prove that the set
B=\{\chi\}
is a
(p,q)
-
(EV)
set for every
\chi\in E^{\prime}
. Evidently,
B
is equicontinuous. Fix a weakly
p
-summable sequence
\{x_{n}\}_{n\in\omega}
in
E
. Then
\big(\langle\chi,x_{n}\rangle\big)\in\ell_{p}
(or
\in c_{0}
if
p=\infty
). Since
p\leq q
it follows that
\big(\sup_{\chi\in B}|\langle\chi,x_{n}\rangle|\big)\in\ell_{q}
(or
\in c_{0}
if
q=\infty
). Thus
B
is a
(p,q)
-
(EV)
set.∎

9
V
types of weak barrelledness conditions

We start from the following general approach to weak barrelledness conditions in locally convex spaces.

Definition 9.1

Let
E
be a locally convex space, and let
\mathcal{B}
be a bornology of weak
∗
bounded subsets of
E^{\prime}
such that
\bigcup\mathcal{B}=E^{\prime}
. Then the space
E
is said to be

\bullet

\mathcal{B}
-
(
quasi
)
barrelled if every (resp., strongly bounded) set
B
in
E^{\prime}_{\beta}
such that
B\in\mathcal{B}
is equicontinuous;

\bullet

\aleph_{0}
-
\mathcal{B}
-(quasi)barrelled if every (resp., strongly bounded) set
B
in
E^{\prime}_{\beta}
such that
B\in\mathcal{B}
and which is the countable union of equicontinuous subsets is also equicontinuous;

\bullet

\ell_{\infty}
-
\mathcal{B}
-
(
quasi
)
barrelled if every (resp., strongly bounded) sequence
B
in
E^{\prime}_{\beta}
such that
B\in\mathcal{B}
is equicontinuous;

\bullet

c_{0}
-
\mathcal{B}
-
(
quasi
)
barrelled if every weak
∗
(resp., strongly) null-sequence
B
in
E^{\prime}_{\beta}
such that
B\in\mathcal{B}
is equicontinuous.∎

If
\mathcal{B}
is the family
\mathsf{Bo}(E^{\prime}_{w^{\ast}})
of all weak
∗
bounded subsets of
E^{\prime}
, we obtain the classical weak barrelledness conditions from Definition
2.9
; in this case the letter
\mathcal{B}
will be omitted. By Lemma
8.2
and Proposition
8.9
, we know that the family
\mathsf{V}_{(p,q)}(E^{\prime})
is a bornology such that
\bigcup\mathsf{V}_{(p,q)}(E^{\prime})=E^{\prime}
if and only if
1\leq p\leq q\leq\infty
. In this case
\mathsf{V}_{(p,q)}(E^{\prime})
is also saturated and defines weak barrelledness conditions. As usual, if
q=\infty
we shall omit the second subscript and called
V_{(p,\infty)}
-
(
quasi
)
barrelled spaces by
V_{p}
-
(
quasi
)
barrelled spaces etc. Analogously,
V_{(\infty,\infty)}
-
(
quasi
)
barrelled spaces will be called
V
-
(
quasi
)
barrelled etc. Observe that
E
is
V_{(p,q)}
-barrelled if and only if
\mathsf{V}_{(p,q)}(E^{\prime})=\mathsf{EV}_{(p,q)}(E^{\prime})
.

The next proposition shows the relationships between the classical weak barrelledness conditions from Definition
2.9
and the introduced ones in Definition
9.1
for the family
\mathcal{B}=\mathsf{V}_{(p,q)}(E^{\prime})
, it is immediately follows from the inclusion
\mathsf{V}_{(p,q)}(E^{\prime})\subseteq\mathsf{Bo}(E^{\prime}_{w^{\ast}})
.

Proposition 9.2

Let
1\leq p\leq q\leq\infty
, and let
E
be a locally convex space. Then:

(i)

If
E
is
(
quasi
)
barrelled, then it is
V_{(p,q)}
-
(
quasi
)
barrelled.

(ii)

If
E
is
\aleph_{0}
-
(
quasi
)
barrelled, then it is
\aleph_{0}
-
V_{(p,q)}
-
(
quasi
)
barrelled.

(iii)

If
E
is
\ell_{\infty}
-
(
quasi
)
barrelled, then it is
V_{(p,q)}
-
(
quasi
)
barrelled.

(iv)

If
E
is
c_{0}
-
(
quasi
)
barrelled, then it is
c_{0}
-
V_{(p,q)}
-
(
quasi
)
barrelled.

It is naturally to define classes of locally convex spaces between the class of
\ell_{\infty}
-quasibarrelled spaces and the class of
c_{0}
-quasibarrelled spaces, and between the class of
\ell_{\infty}
-
V_{(p,q)}
-quasibarrelled and the class of
c_{0}
-
V_{(p,q)}
-quasibarrelled spaces.

Definition 9.3

Let
1\leq p\leq q\leq\infty
, and let
E
be a locally convex space. Then
E
is called strictly
c_{0}
-quasibarrelled (resp., strictly
c_{0}
-
V_{(p,q)}
-quasibarrelled) if every weakly null sequence in the strong dual
E^{\prime}_{\beta}
(which is a
V_{(p,q)}
-set, respectively) is equicontinuous.∎

Clearly, strictly
c_{0}
-quasibarrelled spaces are exactly
\infty
-quasibarrelled spaces.

One can naturally ask whether the implications in Proposition
9.2
are not reversible. In Corollary
9.5
below we show that there are
V_{(p,q)}
-barrelled spaces which are not
c_{0}
-barrelled.

Theorem 9.4

Let
1\leq p\leq q\leq\infty
,
X
be a Tychonoff space, and let
E
be a linear subspace of the product
\mathbb{F}^{X}
containing
C_{p}(X)
. Then every
(p,q)
-
(V)
set in
E^{\prime}
is finite-dimensional. Consequently,
E
is
V_{(p,q)}
-barrelled.

Proof 55

First we prove the following claim.

Claim 1. A sequence
\{f_{n}\}_{n\in\omega}
in
C_{p}(X)
is weakly
p
-summable if and only if
\big(f_{n}(x)\big)\in\ell_{p}
(or
\in c_{0}
if
p=\infty
) for every
x\in X
.
Indeed, if
\{f_{n}\}_{n\in\omega}
is weakly
p
-summable, then
\big(\langle\delta_{x},f_{n}\rangle\big)\in\ell_{p}
(or
\in c_{0}
if
p=\infty
) for every
x\in X
, where
\delta_{x}
denotes the Dirac measure at
x
defined by
\langle\delta_{x},f\rangle=f(x)
. This proves the necessity. The sufficiency follows from the fact that every
\chi\in C_{p}(X)^{\prime}
is a finite linear combination of Dirac measures
\delta_{x}
. The claim is proved.

By (vi) of Lemma
8.2
, every
(p,q)
-
(V)
set is a
(V)
set. Therefore it suffices to prove that all
(V)
sets are finite-dimensional. So, let
B
be a
(V)
set in
E^{\prime}
(recall that
E^{\prime}=L(X)
algebraically). Then, for every weakly
1
-summable sequence
\{f_{n}\}
in
E
we have

\lim_{n\to\infty}\sup_{\chi\in B}|\langle\chi,f_{n}\rangle|=0.

(9.1)

To show that
B
is finite-dimensional it suffices to prove that the support
\mathrm{supp}(B)
of
B
is finite.

Suppose for a contradiction that
\mathrm{supp}(B)
is infinite. Then there is a sequence
\{\chi_{n}\}_{n\in\omega}
in
B
such that
\chi_{0}\not=0
and

\mathrm{supp}(\chi_{n+1}){\setminus}\bigcup_{i\leq n}\mathrm{supp}(\chi_{n})\not=\emptyset.

For every
n\in\omega
, choose a point
x_{n}\in\mathrm{supp}(\chi_{n})
such that
x_{n+1}\not\in\bigcup_{i\leq n}\mathrm{supp}(\chi_{n})
.
By Lemma 11.7.1 of
[
52
]
and passing to a subsequence if needed, one can easily find a sequence
\{U_{n}\}_{n\in\omega}
of open sets in
X
such that

U_{n}\cap\mathrm{supp}(\chi_{n})=\{x_{n}\},\;U_{n+1}\cap\bigcup_{i\leq n}\mathrm{supp}(\chi_{n})=\emptyset\;\mbox{ and }U_{n}\cap U_{m}=\emptyset

(9.2)

for all distinct
n,m\in\omega
. For every
n\in\omega
, choose a function
f_{n}\in C_{p}(X)\subseteq E
such that

f_{n}(X{\setminus}U_{n})=\{0\}\;\;\mbox{ and }\;\;f_{n}(x_{n})=\tfrac{1}{|\chi_{n}(x_{n})|}.

(9.3)

Since
\{x:f_{n}(x)\cdot f_{m}(x)\not=0\}\subseteq U_{n}\cap U_{m}=\emptyset
for all distinct
n,m\in\omega
, it follows that the series
\sum_{n\in\omega}|f_{n}(x)|
has at most one nonzero summand and therefore, by Claim 1, the sequence
\{f_{n}\}
is weakly
1
-summable in
E
. On the other hand, (
9.2
) and (
9.3
) imply

\sup_{\chi\in B}|\langle\chi,f_{n}\rangle|\geq|\langle\chi_{n},f_{n}\rangle|=\big|\chi_{n}(x_{n})\cdot f_{n}(x_{n})\big|=1\not\to 0,

which contradicts the choice of
B
. Thus
\mathrm{supp}(B)
is finite.∎

Corollary 9.5

Let
1\leq p\leq q\leq\infty
, and let
\alpha
be a countable ordinal. Then for every Tychonoff space
X
, the function spaces
C_{p}(X)
and
B_{\alpha}(X)
are
V_{(p,q)}
-barrelled. Consequently, if
X
contains an infinite functionally bounded subset, then the space
C_{p}(X)
is
V_{(p,q)}
-barrelled but not
c_{0}
-barrelled.

Proof 56

If
X
contains an infinite functionally bounded subset, then, by the Buchwalter–Schmets theorem, the space
C_{p}(X)
is not barrelled, and hence, by Proposition 2.6 in
[
40
]
, it is not
c_{0}
-barrelled.∎

Below we consider some categorical properties of
V_{(p,q)}
-(quasi)barrelled spaces.

Proposition 9.6

Let
1\leq p\leq q\leq\infty
. Let us say that a locally convex space
E
is
\mathcal{A}
-
(
quasi
)
barrelled if it is either a
V_{(p,q)}
-
(
quasi
)
barrelled space, an
\aleph_{0}
-
V_{(p,q)}
-
(
quasi
)
barrelled space, an
\ell_{\infty}
-
V_{(p,q)}
-
(
quasi
)
barrelled space, or a
c_{0}
-
V_{(p,q)}
-
(
quasi
)
barrelled space.

(i)

Every Hausdorff quotient space of an
\mathcal{A}
-
(
quasi
)
barrelled space is
\mathcal{A}
-
(
quasi
)
barrelled. Consequently, complemented subspaces of
\mathcal{A}
-
(
quasi
)
barrelled spaces are
\mathcal{A}
-
(
quasi
)
barrelled.

(ii)

The Tychonoff product of a family of locally convex spaces is
\mathcal{A}
-
(
quasi
)
barrelled if and only if each of its factor is an
\mathcal{A}
-
(
quasi
)
barrelled space.

(iii)

A countable locally convex direct sum is
\mathcal{A}
-
(
quasi
)
barrelled if and only if all its summands are
\mathcal{A}
-
(
quasi
)
barrelled spaces.

(iv)

Let
H
be a dense subspace of an lcs
E
. If
H
is
\mathcal{A}
-
(
quasi
)
barrelled then so is
E
.

Proof 57

We consider only the
V_{(p,q)}
-(quasi)barrelled case. Other cases can be considered analogously taking into account the next well known facts:

(1)

continuous linear images of equicontinuous sets are equicontinuous,

(2)

if
q:E\to E/H
is a quotient map, then
q^{\ast}:(E/H)^{\prime}_{\beta}\to E^{\prime}_{\beta}
is injective;

(3)

if
T:E\to L
is an operator, then
T^{\ast}
is weak
∗
and strongly continuous (Theorems 8.10.5 and 8.11.3 of
[
67
]
).

(i) Let
H
be a closed subspace of a
V_{(p,q)}
-(quasi)barrelled space
E
, and let
q:E\to E/H
be the quotient map. To show that
E/H
is
V_{(p,q)}
-(quasi)barrelled, let
B\in\mathsf{V}_{(p,q)}\big((E/H)^{\prime}\big)
be a (resp., strongly bounded) set in
(E/H)^{\prime}_{\beta}
. By (iv) of Lemma
8.2
,
q^{\ast}(B)
is a
(p,q)
-
(V)
set in
E^{\prime}_{\beta}
. If in addition
B
is bounded in
(E/H)^{\prime}_{\beta}
, then the strong continuity of
q^{\ast}
implies that also
q^{\ast}(B)
is bounded in
E^{\prime}_{\beta}
. As
E
is
V_{(p,q)}
-(quasi)barrelled,
q^{\ast}(B)
is equicontinuous and hence there is
U\in\mathcal{N}_{0}(E)
such that
q^{\ast}(B)\subseteq U^{\circ}
. Since
q
is a quotient map, the image
q(U)
is a neighborhood of zero in
E/H
. Then for every
q(x)\in q(U)
and each
b\in B
, we obtain
|\langle b,q(x)\rangle|=|\langle q^{\ast}(b),x\rangle|\leq 1
. Therefore
B
is equicontinuous. Thus
E/H
is a
V_{(p,q)}
-(quasi)barrelled space.

(ii) Let
E=\prod_{i\in I}E_{i}
. If
E
is a
V_{(p,q)}
-(quasi)barrelled space then so are all factors
E_{i}
by the clause (i). Conversely, assume that all spaces
E_{i}
are
V_{(p,q)}
-(quasi)barrelled. Let
B\in\mathsf{V}_{(p,q)}(E^{\prime})
. Then, by Proposition
8.4
,
B
has finite support
F\subseteq I
and for every
i\in I
, the projection
B_{i}
of
B
onto
E^{\prime}_{i}
is a
(p,q)
-
(V)
set. As
E_{i}
is
V_{(p,q)}
-(quasi)barrelled, the set
B_{i}
is equicontinuous. Since
B\subseteq\prod_{i\in F}B_{i}
it follows that also
B
is equicontinuous. Thus
E
is a
V_{(p,q)}
-(quasi)barrelled space.

(iii) Let
E=\bigoplus_{n\in\omega}E_{n}
be the direct sum a sequence
\{E_{n}\}_{n\in\omega}
of locally convex spaces. If
E
is
V_{(p,q)}
-(quasi)barrelled then, by (i), so are all its summands
E_{n}
. Conversely, assume that all spaces
E_{n}
are
V_{(p,q)}
-(quasi)barrelled. Let
B\in\mathsf{V}_{(p,q)}(E^{\prime})
. Then, by Proposition
8.4
, for every
n\in\omega
, the projection
B_{n}
of
B
onto
E^{\prime}_{n}
is a
(p,q)
-
(V)
set. As
E_{n}
is
V_{(p,q)}
-(quasi)barrelled, the set
B_{n}
is equicontinuous and therefore there is
U_{n}\in\mathcal{N}_{0}(E_{n})
such that
B_{n}\subseteq\tfrac{1}{2^{n+1}}U_{n}^{\circ}
. Set
U:=E\cap\prod_{n\in\omega}U_{n}
. Then
U
is a neighborhood of zero in
E
and
B\subseteq\prod_{n\in\omega}B_{n}\subseteq U^{\circ}
. Thus
B
is equicontinuous and hence
E
is a
V_{(p,q)}
-(quasi)barrelled space.

(iv) Recall that
E^{\prime}=H^{\prime}
, and let
\mathsf{id}_{H}:H\to E
be the identity map. Let
B\in\mathsf{V}_{(p,q)}(E^{\prime})
. Then, by (ix) of Lemma
8.2
,
B=\mathsf{id}_{H}^{\ast}(B)
is a
(p,q)
-
(V)
set in
H^{\prime}
(and it is bounded in
H^{\prime}_{\beta}
if so is
B
in
E^{\prime}_{\beta}
). Since
H
is
V_{(p,q)}
-(quasi)barrelled,
B
is equicontinuous. Take
U\in\mathcal{N}_{0}(H)
such that
B\subseteq U^{\circ}
. Then
\overline{U}^{\,E}
is a neighborhood of zero in
E
such that
B\subseteq\big(\overline{U}^{\,E}\big)^{\circ}
. Hence
B
is equicontinuous. Thus
E
is a
V_{(p,q)}
-(quasi)barrelled space.∎

Corollary 9.7

In the notation of Proposition
9.6
, if
E=\underrightarrow{\mbox{ s-$\mathsf{ind}$}}_{n}\,E_{n}
is the strict inductive limit of a sequence
\{E_{n}\}_{n\in\omega}
of
\mathcal{A}
-
(
quasi
)
barrelled spaces, then also
E
is
\mathcal{A}
-
(
quasi
)
barrelled.

Proof 58

Recall that
E
is a quotient space of
\bigoplus_{n\in\omega}E_{n}
, and Proposition
9.6
applies.∎

10
V
type properties for locally convex spaces

Below we introduce natural generalizations of the property
V_{p}
for Banach spaces. Proposition
8.9
shows that there is a sense to consider only the case
1\leq p\leq q\leq\infty
.

Definition 10.1

Let
1\leq p\leq q\leq\infty
. A locally convex space
E
is said to have

\bullet

a property
V_{(p,q)}
(a property
EV_{(p,q)}
) if every
(p,q)
-
(V)
(resp.,
(p,q)
-
(EV)
) set in
E^{\prime}_{\beta}
is relatively weakly compact;

\bullet

a property
sV_{(p,q)}
(a property
sEV_{(p,q)}
) if every
(p,q)
-
(V)
(resp.,
(p,q)
-
(EV)
) set in
E^{\prime}_{\beta}
is relatively weakly sequentially compact;

\bullet

a weak property
sV_{(p,q)}
or property
wsV_{(p,q)}
(resp., weak property
sEV_{(p,q)}
or property
wsEV_{(p,q)}
) if each
(p,q)
-
(V)
-subset (resp.,
(p,q)
-
(EV)
-subset) of
E^{\prime}_{\beta}
is weakly sequentially precompact.

In the cases
(p,\infty)
and
(1,\infty)
we shall say that
E
has the property
V_{p}
,
EV_{p}
,
sV_{p}
,
sEV_{p}
,
wsV_{p}
,
wsEV_{p}
or the property
V
,
EV
,
sV
,
sEV
,
wsV
,
wsEV
, respectively.∎

The following lemma summarizes some relationships between
V_{(p,q)}
type properties.

Lemma 10.2

Let
1\leq p\leq q\leq\infty
, and let
(E,\tau)
be a locally convex space.

(i)

If
E
has the property
V_{(p,q)}

(
resp.,
sV_{(p,q)}
)
, then
E
has the property
EV_{(p,q)}

(
resp.,
sEV_{(p,q)}
)
. The converse is true if
E
is
V_{(p,q)}
-barrelled.

(ii)

If
p\leq p^{\prime}\leq q^{\prime}\leq q
and
E
has the property
V_{(p,q)}

(
resp.,
sV_{(p,q)}
,
EV_{(p,q)}
or
sEV_{(p,q)}
)
, then
E
has the property
V_{(p^{\prime},q^{\prime})}

(
resp.,
sV_{(p^{\prime},q^{\prime})}
,
EV_{(p^{\prime},q^{\prime})}
or
sEV_{(p^{\prime},q^{\prime})}
)
.

(iii)

If
E
is
V_{(p,q)}
-barrelled and
E^{\prime}_{\beta}
is weakly angelic (for example,
E^{\prime}_{\beta}
is a strict
(LF)
space), then
E
has the property
V_{(p,q)}
if and only if it has the property
sV_{(p,q)}
if and only if it has the property
EV_{(p,q)}
if and only if it has the property
sEV_{(p,q)}
.

(iv)

If
E
has the property
sV_{(p,q)}

(
resp.,
sEV_{(p,q)}
)
, then
E
has the property
wsV_{(p,q)}

(
resp.,
wsEV_{(p,q)}
)
; the converse assertion is true if
E^{\prime}_{\beta}
is weakly sequentially complete.

(v)

If
\mathcal{T}
is a locally convex vector topology on
E
compatible with
\tau
, then the spaces
(E,\mathcal{T})
and
(E,\tau)
have the property
V_{(p,q)}

(
resp.,
sV_{(p,q)}
)
simultaneously.

Proof 59

(i) Since, by (i) of Lemma
8.2
, every
(p,q)
-
(EV)
set is also a
(p,q)
-
(V)
, it is clear that the property
V_{(p,q)}
(resp.,
sV_{(p,q)}
) is stronger than the the property
EV_{(p,q)}

(
resp.,
sEV_{(p,q)}
). Assume that
E
is a
V_{(p,q)}
-barrelled space. Then, by Proposition
9.2
,
\mathsf{V}_{(p,q)}(E)=\mathsf{EV}_{(p,q)}(E)
. Thus
E
has the property
V_{(p,q)}
(resp.,
sV_{(p,q)}
) if and only if it has the property
EV_{(p,q)}

(
resp.,
sEV_{(p,q)}
).

(ii) follows from (vi) of Lemma
8.2
which states that every
(p^{\prime},q^{\prime})
-
(V)
(resp.,
(p^{\prime},q^{\prime})
-
(EV)
) set is also a
(p,q)
-
(V)
(resp.,
(p,q)
-
(EV)
) set.

(iii) follows from the equality
\mathsf{V}_{(p,q)}(E)=\mathsf{EV}_{(p,q)}(E)
(since
E
is
V_{(p,q)}
-barrelled) and the well known fact that in angelic spaces the property of being a relatively compact set is equivalent to the property of being a relatively sequentially compact set. It remains to note that any strict
(LF)
space is weakly angelic by Lemma
2.8
.

(iv) follows from the corresponding definitions and Lemma
3.5
.

(v) follows from (vii) of Lemma
8.2
and the equality
(E,\mathcal{T})^{\prime}_{\beta}=E^{\prime}_{\beta}
.∎

Example 10.3

Let
1<p<\infty
, and let
E=\ell_{p}
. Then every
(p^{\ast},p^{\ast})
-
(V)
subset of
E^{\prime}_{\beta}
is relatively compact and hence
E
has the property
V_{(p^{\ast},p^{\ast})}
. Consequently, every
(p,p)
-
(V^{\ast})
subset of
E
is relatively compact and hence
E
has the property
V_{(p,p)}^{\ast}
.

Proof 60

Let
B
be a
(p^{\ast},p^{\ast})
-
(V)
subset of
E^{\prime}_{\beta}=\ell_{p^{\ast}}
. By Example
4.4
, the standard unit basis
\{e_{n}\}_{n\in\omega}
of
E
is weakly
p^{\ast}
-summable. Therefore the series
\sum_{n\in\omega}\big(\sup_{\chi\in B}|\langle\chi,e_{n}\rangle|\big)^{p^{\ast}}
converges and hence

\sup\Big\{\sum_{n=m}^{\infty}|a_{n}|^{p^{\ast}}:\chi=(a_{n})\in B\Big\}\leq\sum_{n=m}^{\infty}\Big(\sup_{\chi=(a_{n})\in B}|a_{n}|\Big)^{p^{\ast}}=\sum_{n=m}^{\infty}\big(\sup_{\chi\in B}|\langle\chi,e_{n}\rangle|\big)^{p^{\ast}}\to 0

as
m\to\infty
. Thus, by Proposition
2.10
,
B
is relatively compact. The second assertion follows from the reflexivity of
E
.∎

By Corollary 2 of
[
71
]
, the finite product of Banach spaces with the property
V
has the property
V
. Below we generalize this result.

Proposition 10.4

Let
1\leq p\leq q\leq\infty
, and let
\{E_{i}\}_{i\in I}
be a non-empty family of locally convex spaces. Then:

(i)

E=\prod_{i\in I}E_{i}
has the property
V_{(p,q)}

(
resp.,
EV_{(p,q)}
,
sV_{(p,q)}
,
sEV_{(p,q)}
,
wsV_{(p,q)}
or
wsEV_{(p,q)}
)
if and only if all factors
E_{i}
have the same property;

(ii)

E=\bigoplus_{i\in I}E_{i}
has the property
V_{(p,q)}
if and only if all
E_{i}
have the property
V_{(p,q)}
;

(iii)

if
I
is countable, then
E=\bigoplus_{i\in I}E_{i}
has the property
sV_{(p,q)}

(
resp.,
sEV_{(p,q)}
,
wsV_{(p,q)}
or
wsEV_{(p,q)}
)
if and only if all summands
E_{i}
have the same property.

Proof 61

To prove the necessity, let
E
have the property
V_{(p,q)}
(resp., the property
EV_{(p,q)}
,
sV_{(p,q)}
,
sEV_{(p,q)}
,
wsV_{(p,q)}
or
wsEV_{(p,q)}
). Fix
j\in I
, and let
K_{j}
be a
(p,q)
-
(V)
(resp.,
(p,q)
-
(EV)
) set in
(E_{j})^{\prime}
. Since, by Proposition
2.5
,
(E_{j})^{\prime}_{\beta}
is a direct summand of
E^{\prime}_{\beta}
, to show that
K_{j}
is relatively weakly compact (resp., relatively weakly sequentially compact or weakly sequentially precompact) in
(E_{j})^{\prime}_{\beta}
it suffices to show that the set
K:=K_{j}\times\prod_{i\in I{\setminus}\{j\}}\{0_{i}\}
is relatively weakly compact (resp., relatively weakly sequentially compact or weakly sequentially precompact) in
E^{\prime}_{\beta}
. In all cases (i)–(iii), by Proposition
8.4
,
K
is a
(p,q)
-
(V)
(resp.,
(p,q)
-
(EV)
) set in
E^{\prime}
. Since
E
has the property
V_{(p,q)}
(resp., the property
EV_{(p,q)}
,
sV_{(p,q)}
,
sEV_{(p,q)}
,
wsV_{(p,q)}
or
wsEV_{(p,q)}
), it follows that
K
is relatively weakly compact (resp., relatively weakly sequentially compact or weakly sequentially precompact) in
E^{\prime}_{\beta}
, as desired.

To prove the sufficiency, assume that all spaces
E_{i}
have the property
V_{(p,q)}
(resp., the property
EV_{(p,q)}
,
sV_{(p,q)}
,
sEV_{(p,q)}
,
wsV_{(p,q)}
or
wsEV_{(p,q)}
), and let
K
be a
(p,q)
-
(V)
(resp.,
(p,q)
-
(EV)
) set in
E^{\prime}_{\beta}
.
Taking into account Lemmas
2.1
and
2.7
and that the weak topology of a product is the product of weak topologies (see Theorem 8.8.5 of
[
52
]
), to show that
K
is relatively weakly compact (resp., relatively weakly sequentially compact or weakly sequentially precompact) it suffices to prove that

(1)

for every
i\in I
, the projection
K_{i}
of
K
onto the
i
th coordinate is relatively weakly compact (resp., weakly sequentially compact or weakly sequentially precompact) in
(E_{i})^{\prime}_{\beta}
, and

(2)

in the case (i) when
E
is the direct product,
K_{i}=\{0\}
for all but finitely many indices
i\in I
.

But both conditions (1) and (2) are satisfied by Lemma
2.7
and Proposition
8.4
.∎

Generalizing Corollary 1 of
[
71
]
, it is proved in Corollary 2.5 of
[
61
]
that a quotient space of a Banach space with the property
V_{p}
has the property
V_{p}
, as well. The following proposition generalizes this result.

Proposition 10.5

Let
1\leq p\leq q\leq\infty
, and let
H
be a closed subspace of a locally convex space
E
such that the quotient map
q:E\to E/H
is almost bounded-covering. If
E
has the property
V_{(p,q)}

(
resp.,
EV_{(p,q)}
,
sV_{(p,q)}
,
sEV_{(p,q)}
,
wsV_{(p,q)}
or
wsEV_{(p,q)}
)
, then also its quotient space
E/H
has the same property.

Proof 62

Recall that, by Theorem 8.12.1 of
[
67
]
, the image
q^{\ast}\big[(E/H)^{\prime}\big]
of the adjoint map
q^{\ast}
is the annihilator
H^{\perp}
of
H
.
Hence
q^{\ast}\big[(E/H)^{\prime}\big]
is weakly closed in
E^{\prime}_{\beta}
. Since
q
is almost bounded-covering, Proposition
2.3
implies that
q^{\ast}
is an embedding of
(E/H)^{\prime}_{\beta}
onto the weakly closed subspace
H^{\perp}
of
E^{\prime}_{\beta}
. Now, let
B
be a
(p,q)
-
(V)
(resp.,
(p,q)
-
(EV)
) set in
(E/H)^{\prime}_{\beta}
. Then, by (iv) of Lemma
8.2
,
q^{\ast}(B)
is a
(p,q)
-
(V)
(resp.,
(p,q)
-
(EV)
) set in
E^{\prime}_{\beta}
. Therefore, by the property
V_{(p,q)}
(resp.,
EV_{(p,q)}
,
sV_{(p,q)}
,
sEV_{(p,q)}
,
wsV_{(p,q)}
or
wsEV_{(p,q)}
) of
E
, the set
q^{\ast}(B)
is a relatively weakly compact (resp., relatively weakly sequentially compact or weakly sequentially precompact) subset of
H^{\perp}
. As
q^{\ast}
is a homeomorphism onto
H^{\perp}
it follows that the set
B
is relatively weakly compact (resp., relatively weakly sequentially compact or weakly sequentially precompact) in
(E/H)^{\prime}_{\beta}
. Thus
E/H
has the property
V_{(p,q)}
(resp.,
EV_{(p,q)}
,
sV_{(p,q)}
,
sEV_{(p,q)}
,
wsV_{(p,q)}
or
wsEV_{(p,q)}
).∎

Corollary 10.6

Let
1\leq p\leq q\leq\infty
, and let
E=\underrightarrow{\mbox{ s-$\mathsf{ind}$}}_{n}\,E_{n}
be the strict inductive limit of a sequence
\{(E_{n},\tau_{n})\}_{n\in\omega}
of locally convex spaces such that
E_{n}
is a closed proper subspace of
E_{n+1}
for every
n\in\omega
. If all spaces
E_{n}
have the property
V_{(p,q)}

(
resp.,
EV_{(p,q)}
,
sV_{(p,q)}
,
sEV_{(p,q)}
,
wsV_{(p,q)}
or
wsEV_{(p,q)}
)
, then also
E
has the same property.

Proof 63

First we note that
E=H/L
for some closed subspace
L
of the direct locally convex sum
H=\bigoplus_{n\in\omega}E_{n}
. By Proposition
10.4
,
H
has the property
V_{(p,q)}
(resp.,
EV_{(p,q)}
,
sV_{(p,q)}
,
sEV_{(p,q)}
,
wsV_{(p,q)}
or
wsEV_{(p,q)}
). By Theorem 4.5.5 of
[
52
]
, any bounded set of
E
is contained in some
E_{n}
and is bounded in
E_{n}
. Therefore, the quotient map
q:H\to H/L=E
is bounded-covering and hence, by Proposition
10.5
,
E
has the property
V_{(p,q)}
(resp.,
EV_{(p,q)}
,
sV_{(p,q)}
,
sEV_{(p,q)}
,
wsV_{(p,q)}
or
wsEV_{(p,q)}
).∎

We shall use also the following notion.

Definition 10.7

Let
\mathcal{P}
be a property defined on every topological vector space. Two separated vector topologies
\tau
and
\mathcal{T}
on a vector space
E
are called weakly
\mathcal{P}
-compatible if the spaces
(E,\tau)_{w}
and
(E,\mathcal{T})_{w}
have the same sets with
\mathcal{P}
.∎

Proposition 10.8

Let
1\leq p\leq q\leq\infty
, and let
H
be a dense subspace of a locally convex space
E
.

(i)

Assume that
\mathcal{P}
is the property of being a relatively compact subset, and let
\beta(E^{\prime},H)
and
\beta(E^{\prime},E)
be weakly
\mathcal{P}
-compatible. If
H
has the property
V_{(p,q)}

(
or
EV_{(p,q)}
)
, then
E
has the property
V_{(p,q)}

(
resp.,
EV_{(p,q)}
)
.

(ii)

Assume that
\mathcal{P}
is the property of being a relatively sequentially compact subset, and let
\beta(E^{\prime},H)
and
\beta(E^{\prime},E)
be weakly
\mathcal{P}
-compatible. If
H
has the property
sV_{(p,q)}

(
or
sEV_{(p,q)}
)
, then
E
has the property
sV_{(p,q)}

(
resp.,
sEV_{(p,q)}
)
.

(ii)

Assume that
\mathcal{P}
is the property of being a sequentially precompact subset, and let
\beta(E^{\prime},H)
and
\beta(E^{\prime},E)
be weakly
\mathcal{P}
-compatible. If
H
has the property
wsV_{(p,q)}

(
or
wsEV_{(p,q)}
)
, then
E
has the property
wsV_{(p,q)}

(
resp.,
wsEV_{(p,q)}
)
.

Proof 64

(i) Let
B
be a
(p,q)
-
(V)
(or
(p,q)
-
(EV)
) set in
E^{\prime}
. Then, by (ix) of Lemma
8.2
, the set
B
is also a
(p,q)
-
(V)
(or
(p,q)
-
(EV)
) set in
H^{\prime}=E^{\prime}
. Since
H
has the property
V_{(p,q)}
(or
EV_{(p,q)}
),
B
is relatively weakly compact in
H^{\prime}_{\beta}
. As
\beta(E^{\prime},H)
and
\beta(E^{\prime},E)
are weakly
\mathcal{P}
-compatible, it follows that
B
is relatively weakly compact in
E^{\prime}_{\beta}
and hence
E
has the property
V_{(p,q)}
(or
EV_{(p,q)}
).

(ii) and (iii) can be proved analogously.∎

As an immediate consequence of Propositions
10.8
and
2.4
we obtain the following assertion.

Corollary 10.9

Let
1\leq p\leq q\leq\infty
, and let
H
be a large subspace of a locally convex space
E
. If
H
has the property
V_{(p,q)}

(
resp.,
EV_{(p,q)}
,
sV_{(p,q)}
,
sEV_{(p,q)}
,
wsV_{(p,q)}
or
wsEV_{(p,q)}
)
, then also
E
has the same property.

To show that closed subspaces of spaces with the property
V_{(p,q)}
may not have the property
V_{(p,q)}
, we notice the following assertion.

Theorem 10.10

Let
1\leq p\leq q\leq\infty
, and let
\alpha
be a countable ordinal. Then for every Tychonoff space
X
, the function spaces
C_{p}(X)
and
B_{\alpha}(X)
have the properties
V_{(p,q)}
,
EV_{(p,q)}
,
sV_{(p,q)}
,
sEV_{(p,q)}
,
wsV_{(p,q)}
and
wsEV_{(p,q)}
.

Proof 65

By Theorem
9.4
, every
(p,q)
-
(V)
set (and hence also each
(p,q)
-
(EV)
set) in
E^{\prime}
is finite-dimensional and the assertion follows.∎

Corollary 10.11

Let
1\leq p\leq q\leq\infty
. For every locally convex space
E
there is a bounded-finite, zero-dimensional, paracompact Tychonoff space
X
such that
E_{w}
is topologically isomorphic to a closed subspace of the barrelled space
C_{p}(X)
with the properties
V_{(p,q)}
,
EV_{(p,q)}
,
sV_{(p,q)}
,
sEV_{(p,q)}
,
wsV_{(p,q)}
and
wsEV_{(p,q)}
.

Proof 66

The existence of an embedding formulated in the corollary was proved in Theorem 4.7 of
[
5
]
. Now Theorem
10.10
applies.∎

Remark 10.12

Corollary
10.11
shows that the properties
V_{(p,q)}
,
EV_{(p,q)}
,
sV_{(p,q)}
, and
sEV_{(p,q)}
are not (closely) hereditary. Indeed, take for example a Banach space
E
without the property
V
. Then, by (ii) and (v) of Lemma
10.2
,
E_{w}
also has no any of the properties
V_{(p,q)}
,
EV_{(p,q)}
,
sV_{(p,q)}
, and
sEV_{(p,q)}
. It remains to note that, by Corollary
10.11
,
E_{w}
is topologically isomorphic to a closed subspace of the barrelled space
C_{p}(X)
with all properties
V_{(p,q)}
,
EV_{(p,q)}
,
sV_{(p,q)}
, and
sEV_{(p,q)}
. ∎

Remark 10.13

Following Grothendieck
[
48
]
, a Banach space
X
has the reciprocal Dunford–Pettis property (
RDP
property) if any completely continuous (=Dunford–Pettis) operator
T:X\to Y
, were
Y
is an arbitrary Banach space, is weakly compact. It was shown by Leavelle
[
60
]
that a Banach space
X
has
RDP
property if and only if every
\infty
-
(V)
subset of
E^{\prime}_{\beta}
is relatively weakly compact, i.e., if and only if
X
has the property
V_{\infty}
. ∎

More generally, we have the following.

Remark 10.14

Let
p\in[1,\infty]
,
X
be a Banach space, and let
X^{\ast}=X^{\prime}_{\beta}
be the Banach dual of
X
. Following Ghenciu
[
42
]
, a subset
B
of
X^{\ast}
is called a weakly-
p
-
L
-set if
\big(\sup_{\chi\in B}|\langle\chi,x_{n}\rangle|\big)\in c_{0}
for every weakly
p
-summable sequence
\{x_{n}\}_{n\in\omega}
in
X
. Therefore, by definition, weakly-
p
-
L
-sets in
X^{\ast}
are exactly
p
-
(V)
subsets of
X^{\ast}
. Further, following
[
42
]
, the Banach space
X
has the reciprocal Dunford–Pettis property of order
p
or
RDP_{p}
(resp., the weak reciprocal Dunford–Pettis property of order
p
or
wRDP_{p}
) if each weakly-
p
-
L
-subset of
X^{\ast}
is relatively weakly sequentially compact (resp., weakly sequentially precompact). Therefore, by definition, for
1\leq p\leq\infty
, we obtain that a Banach space
X
has

\bullet

the
RDP_{p}
if and only if it has the property
sV_{p}
;

\bullet

the
wRDP_{p}
if and only if it has the property
wsV_{p}
.∎

11
V^{\ast}
type properties for locally convex spaces

Below we extend and generalize the property
V^{\ast}_{p}
for Banach spaces to locally convex spaces. Recall that, by Proposition
7.5
,
\mathsf{V}_{(p,q)}^{\ast}(E)=\mathsf{EV}_{(p,q)}^{\ast}(E)=\{0\}
for all
1\leq q<p\leq\infty
. Therefore we consider only the case
1\leq p\leq q\leq\infty
.

Definition 11.1

Let
1\leq p\leq q\leq\infty
. A locally convex space
E
is said to have

\bullet

a property
V^{\ast}_{(p,q)}
(a property
EV^{\ast}_{(p,q)}
) if every
(p,q)
-
(V^{\ast})
(resp.,
(p,q)
-
(EV^{\ast})
) set in
E
is relatively weakly compact;

\bullet

a property
sV^{\ast}_{(p,q)}
(a property
sEV^{\ast}_{(p,q)}
) if every
(p,q)
-
(V^{\ast})
(resp.,
(p,q)
-
(EV^{\ast})
) set in
E
is relatively weakly sequentially compact;

\bullet

a weak property
sV_{(p,q)}^{\ast}
or a property
wsV_{(p,q)}^{\ast}
(resp., a weak property
sEV_{(p,q)}^{\ast}
or a property
wsEV_{(p,q)}^{\ast}
) if each
(p,q)
-
(V^{\ast})
-subset (resp.,
(p,q)
-
(EV^{\ast})
-subset) of
E
is weakly sequentially precompact.

In the case when
q=\infty
we shall omit the subscript
q
and say that
E
has the property
V^{\ast}_{p}
etc., and in the case when
q=\infty
and
p=1
we shall say that
E
has the property
V^{\ast}
etc.∎

Note that for Banach spaces the the property
wsV^{\ast}
coincides with the property
weak
(V^{\ast})
introduced by Saab and Saab in
[
81
, p. 529]
.

Some relationships between the introduced notions are given in the following lemma.

Lemma 11.2

Let
1\leq p\leq q\leq\infty
, and let
(E,\tau)
be a locally convex space.

(i)

If
E
has the property
EV_{(p,q)}^{\ast}

(
resp.,
sEV_{(p,q)}^{\ast}
or
wsEV_{(p,q)}^{\ast}
)
, then
E
has the property
V_{(p,q)}^{\ast}

(
resp.,
sV_{(p,q)}^{\ast}
or
wsV_{(p,q)}^{\ast}
)
; the converse is true if
E
is
p
-quasibarrelled.

(ii)

If
p\leq p^{\prime}\leq q^{\prime}\leq q
and
E
has the property
V_{(p,q)}^{\ast}

(
resp.,
EV_{(p,q)}^{\ast}
,
sV_{(p,q)}^{\ast}
,
sEV_{(p,q)}^{\ast}
,
wsV_{(p,q)}^{\ast}
or
wsEV_{(p,q)}^{\ast}
)
, then
E
has the property
V_{(p^{\prime},q^{\prime})}^{\ast}

(
resp.,
EV_{(p^{\prime},q^{\prime})}^{\ast}
,
sV_{(p^{\prime},q^{\prime})}^{\ast}
,
sEV_{(p^{\prime},q^{\prime})}^{\ast}
or
wsV_{(p^{\prime},q^{\prime})}^{\ast}
)
.

(iii)

If
E
is weakly angelic
(
for example,
E
is a strict
(LF)
space
)
, then
E
has the property
V_{(p,q)}^{\ast}

(
resp.,
EV_{(p,q)}^{\ast}
)
if and only if it has the property
sV_{(p,q)}^{\ast}

(
resp.,
sEV_{(p,q)}^{\ast}
)
.

(iv)

If
\mathcal{T}
is a locally convex vector topology on
E
compatible with
\tau
, then
E
has the property
V_{(p,q)}^{\ast}

(
resp.,
sV_{(p,q)}^{\ast}
or
wsV_{(p,q)}^{\ast}
)
if and only if
(E,\mathcal{T})
has the same property.

(v)

If
E
has the property
sV_{(p,q)}^{\ast}
, then
E
has the property
wsV_{(p,q)}^{\ast}
; the converse is true if
E
is weakly sequentially complete.

Proof 67

(i), (ii) and (iv) follow from (i), (vi) and (vii) of Lemma
7.2
, respectively. The clause (iii) follows from the fact that in angelic spaces the classes of (relatively) sequentially compact sets and (relatively) compact sets coincide and Lemma
2.8
. Finally, the clause (v) follows from the corresponding definitions and Lemma
3.5
.∎

Proposition 11.3

Let
1\leq p\leq q\leq\infty
, and let
\{E_{i}\}_{i\in I}
be a non-empty family of locally convex spaces. Then:

(i)

E=\prod_{i\in I}E_{i}
has the property
V_{(p,q)}^{\ast}

(
resp.,
EV_{(p,q)}^{\ast}
)
if and only if all spaces
E_{i}
have the same property;

(ii)

E=\bigoplus_{i\in I}E_{i}
has the property
V_{(p,q)}^{\ast}

(
resp.,
EV_{(p,q)}^{\ast}
,
sV_{(p,q)}^{\ast}
,
sEV_{(p,q)}^{\ast}
,
wsV_{(p,q)}^{\ast}
or
wsEV_{(p,q)}^{\ast}
)
if and only if all spaces
E_{i}
have the same property;

(iii)

if
I=\omega
is countable, then
E=\prod_{i\in\omega}E_{i}
has the property
sV_{(p,q)}^{\ast}

(
resp.,
sEV_{(p,q)}^{\ast}
,
wsV_{(p,q)}^{\ast}
or
wsEV_{(p,q)}^{\ast}
)
if and only if all spaces
E_{i}
have the same property.

Proof 68

To prove the necessity, let
E
have the property
V_{(p,q)}^{\ast}
(resp., the property
EV_{(p,q)}^{\ast}
,
sV_{(p,q)}^{\ast}
,
sEV_{(p,q)}^{\ast}
,
wsV_{(p,q)}^{\ast}
or
wsEV_{(p,q)}^{\ast}
). Fix
j\in I
, and let
A_{j}
be a
(p,q)
-
(V^{\ast})
(resp.,
(p,q)
-
(EV^{\ast})
) set in
E_{j}
. Since
E_{j}
is a direct summand of
E
, to show that
A_{j}
is relatively weakly compact (resp., relatively weakly sequentially compact or weakly sequentially precompact) in
E_{j}
it suffices to show that the set
A:=A_{j}\times\prod_{i\in I{\setminus}\{j\}}\{0_{i}\}
is relatively weakly compact (resp. relatively weakly sequentially compact or weakly sequentially precompact) in
E
. In all cases (i)–(iii), by (iv) of Lemma
7.2
,
A
is a
(p,q)
-
(V^{\ast})
(resp.,
(p,q)
-
(EV^{\ast})
) set in
E
as the image of
A_{j}
under the canonical embedding of
E_{j}
into
E
. Since
E
has the property
V_{(p,q)}^{\ast}
(resp., the property
EV_{(p,q)}^{\ast}
,
sV_{(p,q)}^{\ast}
,
sEV_{(p,q)}^{\ast}
,
wsV_{(p,q)}^{\ast}
or
wsEV_{(p,q)}^{\ast}
) and
E_{j}
is weakly closed in
E
, it follows that
A
is relatively weakly compact (resp., relatively weakly sequentially compact or weakly sequentially precompact) in
E
, as desired.

To prove the sufficiency, assume that all spaces
E_{i}
have the property
V_{(p,q)}^{\ast}
(resp., the property
EV_{(p,q)}^{\ast}
,
sV_{(p,q)}^{\ast}
,
sEV_{(p,q)}^{\ast}
,
wsV_{(p,q)}^{\ast}
or
wsEV_{(p,q)}^{\ast}
), and let
A
be a
(p,q)
-
(V^{\ast})
(resp.,
(p,q)
-
(EV^{\ast})
) set in
E
.
Taking into account

1.

Lemmas
2.1
and
2.7
,

2.

for the cases (i) and (iii), the fact that the weak topology of a product is the product of weak topologies (see Theorem 8.8.5 of
[
52
]
),

3.

for the case (ii), the fact that the support of
A
is finite since
A
is bounded,

to show that
A
is relatively weakly compact (resp., relatively weakly sequentially compact or weakly sequentially precompact) it suffices to prove that for every
i\in I
, the projection
A_{i}
of
A
onto the
i
th coordinate is relatively weakly compact (resp., relatively weakly sequentially compact or weakly sequentially precompact) in
E_{i}
. But this condition is satisfied because, by (iv) of Lemma
7.2
, the projection
A_{i}
is a
(p,q)
-
(V^{\ast})
(resp.,
(p,q)
-
(EV^{\ast})
) set in
E_{i}
and hence it is relatively weakly compact (resp., relatively weakly sequentially compact or weakly sequentially precompact) in
E_{i}
by the corresponding property of
E_{i}
.∎

Proposition 11.4

Let
1\leq p\leq q\leq\infty
, and let
E
be a locally convex space.

(i)

If
E
is semi-reflexive, then
E
has the property
V_{(p,q)}^{\ast}
and the property
EV_{(p,q)}^{\ast}
. If in addition
E
is weakly angelic, then
E
has also the properties
sV_{(p,q)}^{\ast}
and
sEV_{(p,q)}^{\ast}
.

(ii)

If
E
has the property
V_{p}^{\ast}
and
E^{\prime}_{\beta}
has the
p
-Schur property, then
E
is semi-reflexive. If in addition
E
is quasibarrelled, then
E
is a reflexive space.

(iii)

If
E
is reflexive, then it is a
(
quasi
)
barrelled space with the properties
V_{(p,q)}^{\ast}
and
V_{(p,q)}
.

Proof 69

(i) Since
(p,q)
-
(V^{\ast})
sets and
(p,q)
-
(EV^{\ast})
sets are bounded (Lemma
7.2
), the assertion follows from Theorem 15.2.4 of
[
67
]
which states that an lcs is semi-reflexive if and only if every its closed bounded subset is weakly compact. If additionally
E
is weakly angelic, the space
E
has also the properties
sV_{(p,q)}^{\ast}
and
sEV_{(p,q)}^{\ast}
by (iii) of Lemma
11.2
.

(ii) By Theorem
7.8
, the
p
-Schur property of
E^{\prime}_{\beta}
implies the equality
\mathsf{Bo}(E)=\mathsf{V}_{p}^{\ast}(E)
. Therefore, by the property
V_{p}^{\ast}
of
E
, every bounded subset of
E
is relatively weakly compact. Thus, by Theorem 15.2.4 of
[
67
]
,
E
is semi-reflexive. The last assertion follows from the fact that a semi-reflexive quasibarrelled space is reflexive, see Proposition 11.4.2 of
[
52
]
.

(iii) Assume that
E
is reflexive. Then, by Proposition 11.4.2 of
[
52
]
,
E
is a (quasi)barrelled space whose closed disks are weakly compact. Taking into consideration that the class
\mathsf{V}_{(p,q)}^{\ast}(E)
is saturated (see (iii) of Lemma
7.2
) it follows that
E
has the property
V_{(p,q)}^{\ast}
.

Since, by Proposition 11.4.5 of
[
52
]
, the space
E^{\prime}_{\beta}
is also reflexive it follows that any closed disk in
E^{\prime}_{\beta}
is weakly compact. Taking also into account that any
(p,q)
-
(V)
set is strongly bounded (because
E
is semi-reflexive), it follows that each
(p,q)
-
(V)
set in
E^{\prime}_{\beta}
is relatively weakly compact. Thus
E
has the property
V_{(p,q)}
.∎

Remark 11.5

In (ii) of Proposition
11.4
the condition on
E^{\prime}_{\beta}
to have the
p
-Schur property is not necessary. Indeed, consider the reflexive Banach space
E=\ell_{p}
,
1<p<\infty
. Then, by (iii) of Proposition
11.4
, the space
E
has the property
V_{p}^{\ast}
. However, by Proposition
6.5
, the space
E^{\prime}_{\beta}=\ell_{p^{\ast}}
does not have the
p
-Schur property.∎

The clauses (i) and (ii) of Proposition
11.4
immediately imply

Corollary 11.6

Let
p\in[1,\infty]
, and let
E
be a locally convex space such that
E^{\prime}_{\beta}
has the
p
-Schur property. Then
E
is semi-reflexive if and only if it has the property
V_{p}^{\ast}
.

For a subset
A
of a topological space
X
, we denote by
[A]^{s}
the
sequential closure
of
A
, that is

[A]^{s}=\{x\in X:\mbox{ there is }\{a_{n}\}_{n\in\omega}\subseteq A\mbox{ such that }a_{n}\to x\}.

Clearly,
A\subseteq[A]^{s}\subseteq\mathrm{cl}(A)
and
A
is called
sequentially closed
if
A=[A]^{s}
. Evidently, every closed subset is sequentially closed. If the converse is true, i.e., if every sequentially closed subset of
X
is closed, then the space
X
is called a
sequential space
.

Generalizing Proposition 5 of
[
71
]
, it is shown in Corollary 2.15 of
[
61
]
that every closed subspace of a Banach space with the property
V_{p}^{\ast}
has the property
V_{p}^{\ast}
. The clause (i) of the following proposition generalizes this result.

Proposition 11.7

Let
1\leq p\leq q\leq\infty
, and let
L
be a subspace of a locally convex space
E
.

(i)

If
L
is closed in
E
and
E
has the property
V_{(p,q)}^{\ast}

(
resp.,
EV_{(p,q)}^{\ast}
)
, then also
L
has the same property.

(ii)

If
L_{w}
is sequentially closed in
E_{w}
and
E
has the property
sV_{(p,q)}^{\ast}

(
resp.,
sEV_{(p,q)}^{\ast}
)
, then also
L
has the same property.

(iii)

If
E
has the property
wsV_{(p,q)}^{\ast}

(
resp.,
wsEV_{(p,q)}^{\ast}
)
, then also
L
has the same property.

Proof 70

Let
A
be a
(p,q)
-
(V^{\ast})
(resp.,
(p,q)
-
(EV^{\ast})
) set in
L
. Then, by (viii) of Lemma
7.2
,
A
is a
(p,q)
-
(V^{\ast})
(resp.,
(p,q)
-
(EV^{\ast})
) set also in
E
.

(i) By the property
V_{(p,q)}^{\ast}
(resp.,
EV_{(p,q)}^{\ast}
) of
E
, the set
A
is relatively weakly compact in
E
. Since, by Corollary 8.7.3 of
[
52
]
,
L_{w}
is a closed subspace of
E_{w}
it follows that
A
is relatively weakly compact in
L
. Thus
L
has the property
V_{(p,q)}^{\ast}
(resp.,
EV_{(p,q)}^{\ast}
).

(ii) By the property
sV_{p}^{\ast}
(resp.,
sEV_{(p,q)}^{\ast}
) of
E
, the set
A
is relatively weakly sequentially compact in
E
. Since
L_{w}
is a sequentially closed subspace of
E_{w}
it follows that
A
is relatively weakly sequentially compact in
L
. Thus
L
has the property
sV_{(p,q)}^{\ast}
(resp.,
sEV_{p}^{\ast}
).

(iii) By the property
wsV_{(p,q)}^{\ast}
(resp.,
wsEV_{(p,q)}^{\ast}
) of
E
, the set
A
is weakly sequentially precompact in
E
. Let
\{a_{n}\}_{n\in\omega}
be a sequence in
A
. Take a subsequence
\{a_{n_{k}}\}_{k\in\omega}
of
\{a_{n}\}_{n\in\omega}
which is Cauchy in
E_{w}
. Since
L_{w}
is a subspace of
E_{w}
it follows that
\{a_{n_{k}}\}_{k\in\omega}
is Cauchy also in
L_{w}
. Therefore
A
is weakly sequentially precompact in
L
. Thus
L
has the property
wsV_{(p,q)}^{\ast}
(resp.,
wsEV_{p}^{\ast}
).∎

Corollary 11.8

Let
p\in[1,\infty]
, and let
E=\underrightarrow{\mbox{ s-$\mathsf{ind}$}}_{n}\,E_{n}
be the strict inductive limit of a sequence
\{(E_{n},\tau_{n})\}_{n\in\omega}
of locally convex spaces such that
E_{n}
is a closed proper subspace of
E_{n+1}
for every
n\in\omega
. If the space
E=\underrightarrow{\mbox{ s-$\mathsf{ind}$}}_{n}\,E_{n}
has the property
V_{(p,q)}^{\ast}

(
resp.,
EV_{(p,q)}^{\ast}
,
sV_{(p,q)}^{\ast}
,
sEV_{(p,q)}^{\ast}
,
wsV_{(p,q)}^{\ast}
or
wsEV_{(p,q)}^{\ast}
)
, then all spaces
E_{n}
have the same property.

We do not know whether the converse assertion in Corollary
11.8
holds true.

Corollary 11.9

If a locally convex space
E
contains a closed subspace which is topologically isomorphic to
c_{0}
, then
E
does not have the property
V_{p}^{\ast}
for every
p\in[1,\infty]
.

Proof 71

Since
(c_{0})^{\prime}_{\beta}=\ell_{1}
has the Schur property, Corollary
11.6
implies that the Banach space
c_{0}
does not have the property
V_{p}^{\ast}
for every
p\in[1,\infty]
. Now (i) of Proposition
11.7
applies.∎

Theorem 11.10

Let
1\leq p\leq q\leq\infty
,
X
be a Tychonoff space, and let
E
be a vector subspace of the product
\mathbb{F}^{X}
containing
C_{p}(X)
. Then
E
has the property
EV^{\ast}_{(p,q)}
if and only if it has the property
V^{\ast}_{(p,q)}
if and only if
E
is quasi-complete if and only if
E
is reflexive.

Proof 72

First we note that, by Corollary
7.11
, we have the following
\mathsf{V}_{(p,q)}^{\ast}(E)=\mathsf{EV}_{(p,q)}^{\ast}(E)=\mathsf{Bo}(E)
. Now it is clear that
E
has the property
EV^{\ast}_{(p,q)}
if and only if it has the property
V^{\ast}_{(p,q)}
.

Assume that
E
has the property
V^{\ast}_{(p,q)}
. As we noticed above,
\mathsf{V}_{(p,q)}^{\ast}(E)=\mathsf{Bo}(E)
. Then the property
V^{\ast}_{(p,q)}
of
E
implies that the closure of any bounded subset
A
of
E
is weakly compact and hence compact (recall that
E
carries its weak topology being a subspace of
\mathbb{F}^{X}
). Thus
E
is quasi-complete.

Assume that
E
is quasi-complete. Then the closure
\overline{A}
of every bounded subset
A
of
E
is complete. Since
E
carries its weak topology,
\overline{A}
is precompact. Being complete and precompact
\overline{A}
is compact, see Theorem 3.4.2 of
[
67
]
. Thus
E
has the property
V^{\ast}_{(p,q)}
.

Since
C_{p}(X)
is quasibarrelled, the space
E
is also quasibarrelled. Therefore, by Proposition 11.4.2 of
[
52
]
,
E
is quasi-complete if and only if it is reflexive.∎

Corollary 11.11

Let
1\leq p\leq q\leq\infty
, and let
X
be a Tychonoff space. Then
C_{p}(X)
has the property
EV^{\ast}_{(p,q)}
if and only if it has the property
V^{\ast}_{(p,q)}
if and only if
X
is discrete.

Proof 73

By Theorem
3.13
,
C_{p}(X)
is quasi-complete if and only if
X
is discrete. Now Theorem
11.10
applies.∎

Definition 11.12

Let
\mathcal{A}
be a saturated bornology of bounded subsets in a locally convex space
E
. The space
E
is called

\bullet

\mathcal{A}
-quasi-complete if every closed set in
\mathcal{A}
is complete;

\bullet

separably
\mathcal{A}
-quasi-complete if every closed separable set in
\mathcal{A}
is complete;

\bullet

\mathcal{A}
-von Neumann complete if every closed precompact set in
\mathcal{A}
is compact;

\bullet

separably
\mathcal{A}
-von Neumann complete if every closed separable precompact set in
\mathcal{A}
is compact;

\bullet

\mathcal{A}
-sequentially complete if every sequence in
\mathcal{A}
which is Cauchy converges;

\bullet

\mathcal{A}
-locally complete if the closed absolutely convex hull of a null sequence belonging to
\mathcal{A}
is complete;

\bullet

\mathcal{A}
-convex compactness property (
\mathcal{A}
-ccp for short) if the closed absolutely convex hull of a compact subset from
\mathcal{A}
is compact. ∎

In the case when
\mathcal{A}=\mathsf{Bo}(E)
we shall omit
\mathsf{Bo}(E)
and obtain the classical completeness type properties considered in Section
3
.

The next proposition generalizes Proposition 4 of
[
71
]
and Corollary 2.16 of
[
61
]
.

Proposition 11.13

Let
1\leq p\leq q\leq\infty
, and let
E
be a quasibarrelled locally convex space. Then:

(i)

if
E
has the property
V_{(p,q)}

(
resp.,
sV_{(p,q)}
)
, then
E^{\prime}_{\beta}
has the property
V_{(p,q)}^{\ast}

(
resp.,
sV_{(p,q)}^{\ast}
)
;

(ii)

if
E
is weakly
V_{(p,q)}^{\ast}
-quasi-complete and
E^{\prime}_{\beta}
is quasibarrelled with the property
V_{(p,q)}

(
resp.,
sV_{(p,q)}
)
, then
E
has the property
V_{(p,q)}^{\ast}

(
resp.,
sV_{(p,q)}^{\ast}
)
.

Proof 74

(i) Let
B
be a
(p,q)
-
(V^{\ast})
set in
E^{\prime}_{\beta}
. Then, by (x) of Lemma
8.2
,
B
is a
(p,q)
-
(V)
set. Hence, by the property
V_{(p,q)}
(resp.,
sV_{(p,q)}
) of
E
,
B
is relatively weakly compact (resp., relatively weakly sequentially compact) in
E^{\prime}_{\beta}
. Thus
E^{\prime}_{\beta}
has the property
V_{(p,q)}^{\ast}
(resp.,
sV_{(p,q)}^{\ast}
).

(ii) Let
A
be a
(p,q)
-
(V^{\ast})
in
E
. By Lemma
7.2
, without loss of generality we can assume that
A
is closed and absolutely convex. Since
E
is quasibarrelled, the canonical map
J_{E}:E\to E^{\prime\prime}=(E^{\prime}_{\beta})^{\prime}_{\beta}
is an embedding, and hence, by Lemma
7.2
, the set
J_{E}(A)
is a
(p,q)
-
(V^{\ast})
set in
E^{\prime\prime}
. By (i), the space
E^{\prime\prime}
has the property
V_{(p,q)}^{\ast}
(resp.,
sV_{(p,q)}^{\ast}
). Therefore the set
J_{E}(A)
is relatively weakly compact (resp., relatively weakly sequentially compact) in
E^{\prime\prime}
. Since
E
is weakly
V_{(p,q)}^{\ast}
-quasi-complete and
A
is closed and absolutely convex, we obtain that
A
is complete in
E_{w}
and hence
J_{E}(A)
is complete in
(E^{\prime\prime})_{w}
. So
J_{E}(A)
is weakly compact (resp., weakly sequentially compact) in
E^{\prime\prime}
. Since
J_{E}
is an embedding, the set
A
is weakly compact (resp., relatively weakly sequentially compact) in
E
. Thus
E
has the property
V_{(p,q)}^{\ast}
(resp.,
sV_{(p,q)}^{\ast}
).∎

The converse assertions to both items (i) and (ii) in Proposition
11.13
are not true in general even for Banach spaces, see
[
80
]
.

The following assertion generalizes Proposition 6 of
[
71
]
which states that a Banach space with the property
V^{\ast}
is weakly sequentially complete.

Proposition 11.14

Let
E
be a barrelled locally convex space. If
E
has the property
sV^{\ast}
, then
E
is weakly sequentially complete.

Proof 75

Fix an arbitrary weakly Cauchy sequence
S=\{x_{n}\}_{n\in\omega}
in
E
. We show that it weakly converges. To this end, fix a weakly
1
-summable sequence
\{\chi_{n}\}_{n\in\omega}
in
E^{\prime}_{\beta}
. For every
t=(t_{n})\in\ell_{\infty}
, consider the map
\eta_{t}:E\to\mathbb{F}
defined by
\langle\eta_{t},x\rangle:=\sum_{n\in\omega}t_{n}\cdot\langle\chi_{n},x\rangle
. By Corollary
4.20
,
\eta_{t}\in E^{\prime}
. Since
S
is weakly Cauchy, it follows that for every
t=(t_{n})\in\ell_{\infty}
there is the limit

\lim_{i\to\infty}\sum_{n\in\omega}t_{n}\cdot\langle\chi_{n},x_{i}\rangle=\lim_{i\to\infty}\langle\eta_{t},x_{i}\rangle.

(11.1)

As
\{\chi_{n}\}_{n\in\omega}
is weakly
1
-summable, the series
\sum_{n\in\omega}|\langle\chi_{n},x\rangle|
converges for every
x\in E
, that is
\big(\langle\chi_{n},x\rangle\big)_{n\in\omega}\in\ell_{1}
. Hence (
11.1
) means that the sequence
\big\{\big(\langle\chi_{n},x_{i}\rangle\big)_{n\in\omega}\big\}_{i\in\omega}
is weakly Cauchy in
\ell_{1}
. Since
\ell_{1}
is weakly sequentially complete, there is
z=(z_{n})\in\ell_{1}
such that
z-(\langle\chi_{n},x_{i}\rangle)
is weakly null in
\ell_{1}
. By the Schur property of
\ell_{1}
we have

\lim_{i\to\infty}\sum_{n\in\omega}\big|z_{n}-\langle\chi_{n},x_{i}\rangle\big|=0.

(11.2)

We claim that
\lim_{n\to\infty}\sup_{i\in\omega}|\langle\chi_{n},x_{i}\rangle|=0
. Indeed, otherwise, taking into account that
\{\chi_{n}\}_{n\in\omega}
is weak
∗
null, we could find strictly increasing sequences
(n_{k}),(i_{k})\subseteq\omega
and
\varepsilon>0
such that
|\langle\chi_{n_{k}},x_{i_{k}}\rangle|\geq 2\varepsilon
. Since
z_{n}\to 0
it follows that
\sum_{n\in\omega}\big|z_{n}-\langle\chi_{n},x_{i_{k}}\rangle\big|>\varepsilon
for each sufficiently large
k\in\omega
. But this contradicts (
11.2
). This proves the claim.

Since
\{\chi_{n}\}_{n\in\omega}
was arbitrary, the claim means that the sequence
S
is a
V^{\ast}
set in
E
. By the property
sV^{\ast}
of
E
we obtain that
S
has a weakly convergent subsequence. As
S
is weakly Cauchy it follows that
S
weakly converges to some element of
E
. Thus
E
is weakly sequentially complete.∎

The clause (i) of Proposition
11.13
and Proposition
11.14
immediately imply

Corollary 11.15

Let a quasibarrelled space
E
be such that
E^{\prime}_{\beta}
is barrelled. If
E
has the property
sV
, then
E^{\prime}_{\beta}
is weakly sequentially complete.

Remark 11.16

Let us recall that a subset
A
of a Banach space
X
is called a Dunford–Pettis set (a
DP
set) if each weakly null sequence
\{\chi_{n}\}_{n\in\omega}
in the Banach dual
X^{\ast}=X^{\prime}_{\beta}
tends to
0
uniformly on
A
, i.e.
\lim_{n\to\infty}\sup_{x\in A}|\langle\chi_{n},x\rangle|=0
. In other words,
DP
sets in
X
are exactly
\infty
-
(V^{\ast})
subsets of
X
. Following Bator, Lewis and Ochoa
[
7
]
, the space
X
has the
RDP^{\ast}
property if each
DP
subset of
X
is relatively weakly sequentially compact. Therefore
X
has the
RDP^{\ast}
property if and only if it has the
sV^{\ast}_{\infty}
property. The notion of the
RDP^{\ast}
property was generalized by Ghenciu
[
42
]
. If
p\in[1,\infty)
, a subset
A
of
X
is called weakly-
p
-Dunford–Pettis set if for every weakly
p
-summable sequence
\{\chi_{n}\}_{n\in\omega}
in
X^{\ast}
, it follows
\lim_{n\to\infty}\sup_{x\in A}|\langle\chi_{n},x\rangle|=0
. By definition, we obtain that weakly-
p
-Dunford–Pettis sets in
X
are exactly
p
-
(V^{\ast})
subsets of
X
. Following
[
42
]
, the space
X
has the
RDP^{\ast}_{p}
property if every weakly-
p
-Dunford–Pettis subset of
X
is relatively weakly sequentially compact. We summarize this discussion as follows: if
p\in[1,\infty]
, a Banach space
X
has the
RDP^{\ast}_{p}
property if and only if it has the property
sV^{\ast}_{p}
.∎

In Proposition 7 of
[
71
]
, Pełczyński showed that a Banach space
X
is reflexive if and only if it has both properties
V
and
V^{\ast}
.
The
p
-version of this remarkable result is not true in general. Chen, Chávez-Domínguez, and Li proved in
[
61
]
that the classical non-reflexive James space
J
has both property
V_{2}
and property
V^{\ast}_{2}
. These results motivates the next problem.

Problem 11.17

Let
E
be a (Baire, barrelled, quasibarrelled etc.) locally convex space. Is it true that
E
is reflexive if and only if it has the property
V
and the property
V^{\ast}
?

12
Property
p
-
(u)
for locally convex spaces

The following definition naturally generalizes Pełczyński’s property
(u)
.

Definition 12.1

Let
p\in[1,\infty)
. A locally convex space
E
is said to have a property
p
-
(u)
if for every weakly Cauchy sequence
\{y_{n}\}_{n\in\omega}\subseteq E
there exists a weakly
p
-summable sequence
\{x_{n}\}_{n\in\omega}
in
E
such that the sequence
\big\{y_{n}-\sum_{j\leq n}x_{j}\big\}
is weakly null. If
p=1
we say that
E
has the property
(u)
.∎

Remark 12.2

We do not consider the case
p=\infty
because the condition of Definition
12.1
holds for every lcs
E
. Indeed, let
\{y_{n}\}_{n\in\omega}\subseteq E
be a weakly Cauchy sequence. Set
x_{0}=y_{0}
, and for
n\geq 1
, we set
x_{n}=y_{n}-y_{n-1}
. As
\{y_{n}\}_{n\in\omega}
is weakly Cauchy, the sequence
\{x_{n}\}_{n\in\omega}
is weakly null (= weakly
\infty
-summable). It remains to note that
y_{n}=\sum_{j\leq n}x_{j}
for every
n\in\omega
.∎

Since every weakly
p
-summable sequence is weakly
q
-summable for
p\leq q
we obtain

Proposition 12.3

If
1\leq p<q<\infty
and a locally convex space
E
has the property
p
-
(u)
, then
E
has the property
q
-
(u)
.

Proposition 12.4

If a locally convex space
E
is weakly sequentially complete, then it has the property
p
-
(u)
for every
p\in[1,\infty)
.

Proof 76

Let
\{y_{n}\}_{n\in\omega}
be a weakly Cauchy sequence in
E
. Since
E_{w}
is sequentially complete, there is
y\in E
such that
y_{n}\to y
in the weak topology. Set
x_{0}=y
and
x_{n}=0
for every positive natural number
n
. It is clear that
\{x_{n}\}_{n\in\omega}
is weakly
p
-summable and
\big\{y_{n}-\sum_{j\leq n}x_{j}\big\}
is weakly null.∎

Propositions
11.14
and
12.4
imply the next result.

Corollary 12.5

Let
E
be a barrelled locally convex space. If
E
has the property
sV^{\ast}
, then it has the property
p
-
(u)
for every
p\in[1,\infty)
.

Remark 12.6

The quotient space of a space with the property
p
-
(u)
may not have the property
p
-
(u)
. Indeed, let
E
be a Banach space without the property
(u)
. It is well known that
E
is a quotient space of
\ell_{1}(\Gamma)
for some infinite set
\Gamma
. Since
\ell_{1}(\Gamma)
has the Schur property, it is weakly sequentially complete. Therefore, by Proposition
12.4
,
\ell_{1}(\Gamma)
has the property
p
-
(u)
.∎

Let
p\in[1,\infty]
, and let
E
be a locally convex space. We denote by
B_{1}^{p}(E)
the family of all
x^{\ast\ast}\in E^{\prime\prime}
which are weak
∗

p
-limits of weakly
p
-Cauchy sequences in
E
; if
p=\infty
we write simply
B_{1}(E)
. If
x^{\ast\ast}\in E^{\prime\prime}
and
\{x_{j}\}_{j\in\omega}
is a sequence in
E
such that the sequence
\big\{\sum_{j\leq n}x_{j}\big\}
weak
∗

p
-converges to
x^{\ast\ast}
, we write
x^{\ast\ast}=\sum^{\ast p}x_{j}
; if
p=\infty
we write simply
x^{\ast\ast}=\sum^{\ast}x_{j}
.

Proposition 12.7

Let
p\in[1,\infty)
, and let
E
be a locally convex space. Then
E
has the property
p
-
(u)
if and only if for every
x^{\ast\ast}\in B_{1}(E)
, there is a weakly
p
-summable sequence
\{x_{j}\}_{j\in\omega}
in
E
such that
x^{\ast\ast}=\sum^{\ast}x_{j}
.

Proof 77

Assume that
E
has the property
p
-
(u)
, and let
x^{\ast\ast}\in B_{1}(E)
. Take a weakly Cauchy sequence
\{y_{n}\}_{n\in\omega}\subseteq E
which weak
∗
converges to
x^{\ast\ast}
. By the property
p
-
(u)
, there is a weakly
p
-summable sequence
\{x_{j}\}_{j\in\omega}
in
E
such that the sequence
\big\{y_{n}-\sum_{j\leq n}x_{j}\big\}
is weakly null. Then

x^{\ast\ast}-\sum_{j\leq n}x_{j}=\big(x^{\ast\ast}-y_{n}\big)+\Big(y_{n}-\sum_{j\leq n}x_{j}\Big)\to 0\quad\mbox{ as }\;n\to\infty

in the weak
∗
topology on
E^{\prime\prime}
. Thus
x^{\ast\ast}=\sum^{\ast}x_{j}
.

Conversely, assume that for every
x^{\ast\ast}\in B_{1}(E)
, there is a weakly
p
-summable sequence
\{x_{j}\}_{j\in\omega}
in
E
such that
x^{\ast\ast}=\sum^{\ast}x_{j}
. Let
S=\{y_{n}\}_{n\in\omega}
be a weakly Cauchy sequence in
E
. Since
S
is a bounded subset of
E
, the polar
S^{\circ}
is a neighborhood of zero in
E^{\prime}_{\beta}
. By the Alaoglu theorem, the polar
S^{\circ\circ}
of
S^{\circ}
in
E^{\prime\prime}
is a weak
∗
compact set. Therefore
S
has a cluster point
x^{\ast\ast}\in S^{\circ\circ}
. Since
S
is weakly Cauchy,
x^{\ast\ast}
