is the weak
∗
limit of
S
. Hence
x^{\ast\ast}\in B_{1}(E)
and therefore
x^{\ast\ast}=\sum^{\ast}x_{j}
for some weakly
p
-summable sequence
\{x_{j}\}_{j\in\omega}
in
E
. Whence

y_{n}-\sum_{j\leq n}x_{j}=\big(y_{n}-x^{\ast\ast}\big)+\Big(x^{\ast\ast}-\sum_{j\leq n}x_{j}\Big)\to 0\quad\mbox{ as }\;n\to\infty

in the weak topology on
E
. Thus
E
has the property
p
-
(u)
.∎

Proposition 12.8

If
p\in[1,\infty)
and
\{E_{i}\}_{i\in I}
is a non-empty family of locally convex spaces, then

(i)

E=\prod_{i\in I}E_{i}
has the property
p
-
(u)
if and only if all spaces
E_{i}
have the property
p
-
(u)
;

(ii)

E=\bigoplus_{i\in I}E_{i}
has the property
p
-
(u)
if and only if all spaces
E_{i}
have the property
p
-
(u)
.

Proof 78

Assume that
E
has the property
p
-
(u)
. Fix
i\in I
, and let
\{y_{n}^{i}\}_{n\in\omega}\subseteq E_{i}
be a weakly Cauchy sequence. If
T_{i}:E_{i}\to E
is the identity inclusion, then
\{y_{n}=T_{i}(y_{n}^{i})\}_{n\in\omega}
is a weakly Cauchy sequence in
E
. By the property
p
-
(u)
of
E
, there exists a weakly
p
-summable sequence
S=\{x_{n}\}_{n\in\omega}
in
E
such that the sequence
\big\{y_{n}-\sum_{j\leq n}x_{j}\big\}
is weakly null. Then, by (iii) of Lemma
4.6
, the projection
\{x_{n}(i)\}_{n\in\omega}
of
S
onto
E_{i}
is a weakly
p
-summable sequence in
E_{i}
. Then for every
\chi\in(E_{i})^{\prime}
and considering
\chi
as an element of
E^{\prime}
, we obtain

\Big\langle\chi,y_{n}^{i}-\sum_{j\leq n}x_{j}(i)\Big\rangle=\Big\langle\chi,y_{n}-\sum_{j\leq n}x_{j}\Big\rangle\to 0\quad\mbox{ as }\;n\to\infty.

Hence the sequence
\big\{y_{n}^{i}-\sum_{j\leq n}x_{j}(i)\big\}
is weakly null. Thus
E_{i}
has the property
p
-
(u)
.

Conversely, assume that all spaces
E_{i}
have the property
p
-
(u)
. Let
S=\{y_{n}\}_{n\in\omega}\subseteq E
be a weakly Cauchy sequence. In the case (ii), let
F
be the finite support of
S
. Then for every
i\in I
, the projection
\{P_{i}(y_{n})\}_{n\in\omega}
of
S
onto
E_{i}
is weakly Cauchy in
E_{i}
. Since
E_{i}
has the property
p
-
(u)
, there exists a weakly
p
-summable sequence
\{x_{n}^{i}\}_{n\in\omega}
in
E_{i}
such that the sequence
\big\{P_{i}(y_{n})-\sum_{j\leq n}x_{j}^{i}\big\}
is weakly null. For every
n\in\omega
, define
x_{n}:=(x_{n}^{i})_{i\in I}
and observe that the obtained sequence
\{x_{n}\}_{n\in\omega}
is weakly
p
-summable in
E
by Lemma
4.25
(in the case (ii), the support of
\{x_{n}\}_{n\in\omega}
is also
F
). If
\chi=(\chi_{i})\in E^{\prime}
and, in the case (i),
G
is the finite support of
\chi
, then

\Big\langle\chi,y_{n}-\sum_{j\leq n}x_{j}\Big\rangle=\sum_{i\in F\cup G}\Big\langle\chi_{i},P_{i}(y_{n})-\sum_{j\leq n}x_{j}^{i}\Big\rangle\to 0\quad\mbox{ as }\;n\to\infty.

Hence the sequence
\big\{y_{n}-\sum_{j\leq n}x_{j}\big\}
is weakly null. Thus
E
has the property
p
-
(u)
. ∎

Proposition 12.9

Let
p\in[1,\infty)
, and let
(E,\tau)
be a locally convex space. If
\mathcal{T}
is a locally convex topology on
E
compatible with
\tau
, then
(E,\tau)
has the property
p
-
(u)
if and only if
(E,\mathcal{T})
has the property
p
-
(u)
.

Proof 79

The assertion immediately follows from the fact that the notions of being weakly null, weakly Cauchy and weakly
p
-summable depend only on the duality
(E,E^{\prime})
.∎

We finish this section with the several open problems.

Problem 12.10

For every distinct pairs
(p,q)
and
(p^{\prime},q^{\prime})
with
p\leq q
and
p^{\prime}\leq q^{\prime}
, find (Banach, Fréchet, barrelled, etc.) locally convex spaces
E
with the property
V_{(p,q)}
but without the property
V_{(p^{\prime},q^{\prime})}^{\prime}

(
analogously, for the properties
EV_{(p,q)}
,
V_{(p,q)}^{\ast}
,
EV_{(p,q)}^{\ast}
and
p
-
(u)
)
.

Problem 12.11

Characterize Tychonoff spaces
X
for which the space
C_{k}(X)
has one of the property
V_{(p,q)}

(
EV_{(p,q)}
,
V_{(p,q)}^{\ast}
,
EV_{(p,q)}^{\ast}
or
p
-
(u)
)
.

Problem 12.12

Characterize Tychonoff spaces
X
for which the free lcs
L(X)
over
X
has one of the property
V_{(p,q)}

(
EV_{(p,q)}
,
V_{(p,q)}^{\ast}
,
EV_{(p,q)}^{\ast}
or
p
-
(u)
)
.

Problem 12.13

Characterize Tychonoff spaces
X
for which the space
C_{p}(X)
has the property
p
-
(u)
.

13
Weakly sequentially
p
-(pre)compact and
p
-convergent operators

In what follows we shall use intensively the following
p
-versions of weakly compact-type properties, which extend the corresponding notions in the case of Banach spaces defined in
[
12
]
and
[
42
]
.

Definition 13.1

Let
p\in[1,\infty]
. A subset
A
of a separated tvs
E
is called

\bullet

(relatively) weakly sequentially
p
-compact if every sequence in
A
has a weakly
p
-convergent subsequence with limit in
A
(resp., in
E
);

\bullet

weakly sequentially
p
-precompact if every sequence from
A
has a weakly
p
-Cauchy subsequence.∎

Note that if
E
is a Banach space, weakly sequentially
p
-precompact sets of
E
are known as
conditionally weakly
p
-compact sets
, see Definition 2.5 of
[
16
]
.

Lemma 13.2

Let
p\in[1,\infty]
,
E
and
L
be locally convex spaces, and let
T:E\to L
be a weak-weak continuous linear map. If
A
is a
(
relatively
)
weakly sequentially
p
-compact set
(
resp., a weakly sequentially
p
-precompact set
)
in
E
, then the image
T(A)
is a
(
relatively
)
weakly sequentially
p
-compact
(
resp., weakly sequentially
p
-precompact
)
set in
L
.

Proof 80

We consider only the case when
A
is a (relatively) weakly sequentially
p
-compact set, the case when
A
is weakly sequentially
p
-precompact can be considered analogously. Let
\{T(a_{n})\big\}_{n\in\omega}
be a sequence in
T(A)
. Since
A
is (relatively) weakly sequentially
p
-compact, the sequence
\{a_{n}\}
has a subsequence
\big\{a_{n_{k}}\big\}_{k\in\omega}
which weakly
p
-converges to some
a\in A
(resp.,
a\in E
). Let now
\chi\in L^{\prime}
. Since
T
is weakly continuous, Theorem 8.10.3 of
[
67
]
implies that
T^{\ast}(\chi)\in E^{\prime}
and we have

\big(\langle\chi,T(a_{n_{k}})-T(a)\rangle\big)_{k\in\omega}=\big(\langle T^{\ast}(\chi),a_{n_{k}}-a\rangle\big)_{k\in\omega}\in\ell_{p}\;(\mbox{or $\in c_{0}$ if $p=\infty$}).

Therefore
T(a_{n_{k}})
weakly
p
-converges to
T(a)
. Thus
T(A)
is a
(
relatively
)
weakly sequentially
p
-compact set in
L
. ∎

Below we extend the notion of
p
-convergent operators between Banach spaces defined by Castillo and Sánchez
[
12
]
to the general case of all separated topological vector spaces. Weakly
p
-precompact operators between Banach spaces were defined by Ghenciu
[
42
]
.

Definition 13.3

Let
p\in[1,\infty]
. A linear map
T:E\to L
between separated topological vector spaces
E
and
L
is said to be

\bullet

p
-convergent if it sends weakly
p
-summable sequences in
E
into null-sequences in
L
;

\bullet

weakly sequentially
p
-compact if for some
U\in\mathcal{N}_{0}(E)
, the set
T(U)
is a relatively weakly sequentially
p
-compact subset of
L
;

\bullet

weakly sequentially
p
-precompact if for some
U\in\mathcal{N}_{0}(E)
, the set
T(U)
is weakly sequentially
p
-precompact in
L
;

\bullet

unconditionall converging if
T
sends
wuC
series into unconditionally convergent series. ∎

To characterize
p
-convergent operators we need the next lemma.

Lemma 13.4

Let
p\in[1,\infty]
,
E
and
L
be locally convex spaces, and let
T:E\to L
be a
p
-convergent linear map. If
\{x_{n}\}_{n\in\omega}\subseteq E
is weakly
p
-Cauchy, then the sequence
\big\{T(x_{n})\big\}_{n\in\omega}
is Cauchy in
L
. Consequently, if
L
is sequentially complete, then
\big\{T(x_{n})\big\}_{n\in\omega}
converges in
L
.

Proof 81

Take a pair of strictly increasing sequences
(k_{n}),(j_{n})
in
\omega
. Then
\{x_{k_{n}}-x_{j_{n}}\}
is weakly
p
-summable. Since
T
is
p
-convergent, we obtain
T(x_{k_{n}})-T(x_{j_{n}})\to 0
in
L
, and hence
\big\{T(x_{n})\big\}_{n\in\omega}
is Cauchy in
L
.∎

For
1\leq p<\infty
and operators between Banach spaces the next proposition is Theorem 2.6 of
[
16
]
.

Proposition 13.5

Let
p\in[1,\infty]
, and let
T
be a weak-weak sequentially continuous linear map from a locally convex space
E
to a sequentially complete locally convex space
L
. Then the following assertions are equivalent:

(i)

T
is
p
-convergent;

(ii)

T(A)
is relatively sequentially compact in
L
for each weakly sequentially
p
-precompact subset
A
of
E
;

(iii)

T(A)
is sequentially precompact in
L
for any weakly sequentially
p
-precompact subset
A
of
E
.

Proof 82

(i)
\Rightarrow
(ii) Let
A
be a weakly sequentially
p
-precompact subset of
E
, and let
S=\{a_{n}\}_{n\in\omega}
be a sequence in
A
. Then
S
has a weakly
p
-Cauchy subsequence
\{a_{n_{k}}\}_{k\in\omega}
. Then, by Lemma
13.4
, the sequence
\big\{T(a_{n_{k}})\big\}_{k\in\omega}
converges to some element
x\in L
. Thus
T(A)
is relatively sequentially compact in
L
.

(ii)
\Rightarrow
(iii) is trivial.

(iii)
\Rightarrow
(i) Let
\{x_{n}\}_{n\in\omega}
be a weakly
p
-summable sequence in
E
. Then
\{x_{n}\}_{n\in\omega}
is weakly
p
-Cauchy in
E
, and hence
\{T(x_{n})\}_{n\in\omega}
is sequentially precompact in
L
. Since
L
is sequentially complete, Lemma
3.5
implies that
\{T(x_{n})\}_{n\in\omega}
is relatively sequentially compact in
L
. Therefore, any subsequence
S
of
\{T(x_{n})\}_{n\in\omega}
has a subsequence
\{T(x_{n_{k}})\}_{k\in\omega}
which converges to some element
z\in L
. On the other hand, since
T
is weak-weak sequentially continuous and
x_{n}\to 0
in the weak topology, we obtain that
T(x_{n})\to 0
in the weak topology of
L
. Therefore
z=0
. Since
S
was arbitrary, it follows that
T(x_{n})\to 0
in the space
L
. Thus
T
is
p
-convergent.∎

The problem to find pairs
(E,L)
of locally convex spaces for which
\mathcal{L}(E,L)
contains only
p
-convergent operators is of independent interest.
Below we consider partial cases when this problem has a positive solution.

Proposition 13.6

Let
E
and
L
be locally convex spaces.

(i)

If
p\in[1,\infty]
and
E
or
L
has the
p
-Schur property, then each operator
T\in\mathcal{L}(E,L)
is
p
-convergent.

(ii)

Assume that
L
has the Schur property
(
for example,
L
carries its weak topology
)
. Then for every
p\in[1,\infty]
, each operator
T\in\mathcal{L}(E,L)
is
p
-convergent.

Proof 83

(i) If
E
has the
p
-Schur property, the assertion is trivial. If
L
has the
p
-Schur property, the assertion follows from (iii) of Lemma
4.6
which states that continuous images of weakly
p
-summable sequences are weakly
p
-summable.

(ii) If
L
has the Schur property, it has the
p
-Schur property for every
p\in[1,\infty]
. Now (i) applies.∎

A sufficient condition of being a
p
-convergent operator is given in the next assertion.

Proposition 13.7

Let
p\in[1,\infty)
,
E
and
L
be locally convex spaces, and let
T:E\to L
be a weak-weak sequentially continuous linear map. Assume that one of the following conditions holds:

(i)

for any operator
S:\ell_{p^{\ast}}^{0}\to E

(
or
S:c_{0}^{0}\to E
if
p=1
)
, the linear map
T\circ S
is sequentially precompact;

(ii)

E
is sequentially complete and for any operator
S:\ell_{p^{\ast}}\to E

(
or
S:c_{0}\to E
if
p=1
)
, the linear map
T\circ S
is sequentially precompact.

Then
T
is
p
-convergent.

Proof 84

Let
T\circ S
be sequentially precompact for any operator
S
from (i) or (ii). To show that
T
is
p
-convergent, fix an arbitrary weakly
p
-summable sequence
\{x_{n}\}_{n\in\omega}
in
E
. We have to prove that
T(x_{n})\to 0
in
L
. Suppose for a contradiction that
T(x_{n})\not\to 0
in
L
. Then we can find a neighborhood
U
of zero in
L
and a subsequence
\{x_{n_{i}}\}_{i\in\omega}
of
\{x_{n}\}
such that
T(x_{n_{i}})\not\in U
for every
i\in\omega
. Since
\{x_{n_{i}}\}_{i\in\omega}
is also weakly
p
-summable, without loss of generality we can assume that
x_{n}\not\in U
for every
n\in\omega
.

By Proposition
4.14
, there is
S\in\mathcal{L}(\ell_{p^{\ast}}^{0},E)
(resp.,
S\in\mathcal{L}(\ell_{p^{\ast}},E)
or
S\in\mathcal{L}(c_{0}^{0},E)
,
S\in\mathcal{L}(c_{0},E)
if
p=1
) such that
S(e_{n}^{\ast})=x_{n}
for every
n\in\omega
. Since
T\circ S
is sequentially precompact from a normed space and
\{e_{n}^{\ast}\}_{n\in\omega}
is bounded, the sequence
\{TS(e_{n}^{\ast})\}_{n\in\omega}
has a Cauchy subsequence
\{TS(e_{n_{k}}^{\ast})\}_{k\in\omega}
. Denote by
D
the co-extension of
TS
into the completion
\overline{L}
of the space
L
. Since the sequence
\{TS(e_{n_{k}}^{\ast})\}_{k\in\omega}
is also Cauchy in the complete space
\overline{L}
, it converges to some point
\overline{x}\in\overline{L}
. Observe that
\{e_{n}^{\ast}\}_{n\in\omega}
is a weakly null-sequence in
\ell_{p^{\ast}}
(or in
c_{0}
if
p=1
), and hence the weak-weak sequential continuity of
D
implies that
D(e_{n}^{\ast})=TS(e_{n}^{\ast})\to 0
in the weak topology of
\overline{L}
. Since also
TS(e_{n_{k}}^{\ast})\to\overline{x}
in the weak topology on
\overline{L}
, we obtain that
\overline{x}=0
, and hence
T(x_{n_{k}})=TS(e_{n_{k}}^{\ast})\to 0
in the original topology of
L
. But this contradicts the choice of
\{x_{n}\}
. ∎

For
p\in[1,\infty]
, the
p
-version of the weak sequential completeness of Banach spaces is introduced in Definition 2.17 of
[
61
]
. Below we extend this notion to the class of all locally convex spaces.

Definition 13.8

Let
p\in[1,\infty]
. A locally convex space
E
is called weakly sequentially
p
-complete if every weakly
p
-Cauchy sequence is weakly
p
-convergent.∎

So weakly sequentially
\infty
-complete spaces are exactly weakly sequentially complete. In particular,
\ell_{1}
is weakly sequentially
\infty
-complete. Even a more general assertion holds true.

Proposition 13.9

If a Banach space
E
has the Schur property, then
E
is weakly sequentially
p
-complete for every
p\in[1,\infty]
.

Proof 85

Let
S=\{x_{n}\}_{n\in\omega}
be a weakly
p
-Cauchy sequence in
E
. Then
S
is weakly Cauchy and hence, by the Schur property,
S
is Cauchy in
E
. Since
E
is complete, there is
x\in E
such that
\|x_{n}-x\|\to 0
. Choose a strictly increasing sequence
(n_{k})
in
\omega
such that

\|x_{n_{k}}-x\|<\tfrac{1}{2^{k}}\;\;\mbox{ for every }\;k\in\omega.

Then
\{x_{n_{k}}\}_{k\in\omega}
is weakly
p
-converges to
x
since (we consider the case
p<\infty
)

\sum_{k\in\omega}|\langle\chi,x_{n_{k}}-x\rangle|^{p}\leq\sum_{k\in\omega}\|\chi\|\cdot\tfrac{1}{2^{k}}<\infty

for every
\chi\in E^{\prime}
. Thus, by Lemma
4.5
,
S
weakly
p
-converges to
x
.∎

In Corollary
13.13
below we shall show that for every
1<p<\infty
, the space
\ell_{p}
is weakly sequentially
p^{\ast}
-complete.

The next assertion will play a crucial role to characterize
p
-convergent operators.

Proposition 13.10

Let
p\in[1,\infty)
, and let
E
be a locally convex space.

(i)

Each operator
S:\ell_{p^{\ast}}^{0}\to E

(
or
S:c_{0}^{0}\to E
if
p=1
)
is weakly sequentially
p
-precompact.

(ii)

If
p>1
, then for each operator
S:\ell_{p^{\ast}}\to E
, the set
S(B_{\ell_{p^{\ast}}})
is weakly sequentially
p
-compact in
E
; in particular,
S
is weakly sequentially
p
-compact.

(iii)

If
p=1
, then every operator
S:c_{0}\to E
is weakly sequentially
1
-precompact. If additionally
E
is weakly sequentially
1
-complete, then for each operator
S:c_{0}\to E
, the set
S(B_{c_{0}})
is a weakly sequentially
1
-compact subset of
E
; in particular,
S
is weakly sequentially
p
-compact.

Proof 86

To avoid an unnecessary complication with notations and taking into account that
p<\infty
, below we set
\ell_{1^{\ast}}^{0}:=c_{0}^{0}
and
\ell_{1^{\ast}}:=c_{0}
. We show that the set
S(B_{\ell_{p^{\ast}}^{0}})
(resp.,
S(B_{\ell_{p^{\ast}}})
) is weakly sequentially
p
-precompact (resp., weakly sequentially
p
-compact) in
E
. To this end, let
\{x_{n}\}_{n\in\omega}
be an arbitrary sequence in
B_{\ell_{p^{\ast}}^{0}}
(resp., in
B_{\ell_{p^{\ast}}}
). To prove the proposition we have to find a weakly
p
-Cauchy (resp., weakly
p
-convergent in
S(B_{\ell_{p^{\ast}}})
) subsequence of
\{S(x_{n})\}_{n\in\omega}
. We shall consider
\ell_{p^{\ast}}^{0}
as a dense subspace of
\ell_{p^{\ast}}
. We distinguish between the next three possible cases.

Case 1. The sequence
\{x_{n}\}_{n\in\omega}
contains a subsequence
\{x_{n_{k}}\}_{k\in\omega}
which is Cauchy in the norm topology. Passing to a subsequence if needed, we can assume that
x_{n}
norm converges to some element
x\in B_{\ell_{p^{\ast}}}
and moreover, that
\|x_{n}-x\|_{\ell_{p^{\ast}}}<\tfrac{1}{2^{n}}
for every
n\in\omega
. Then for every
n\in\omega
and each
\chi\in E^{\prime}
, we have

\big|\langle\chi,S(x_{n})-S(x)\rangle\big|=\big|\langle S^{\ast}(\chi),x_{n}-x\rangle\big|\leq\|S^{\ast}(\chi)\|\cdot\tfrac{1}{2^{n}}.

Let
(k_{n}),(j_{n})\subseteq\omega
be two strictly increasing sequences. Then for every
\chi\in E^{\prime}
, we obtain

\big|\langle\chi,S(x_{k_{n}})-S(x_{j_{n}})\rangle\big|\leq\big|\langle\chi,S(x_{k_{n}})-S(x)\rangle\big|+\big|\langle\chi,S(x_{j_{n}})-S(x)\rangle\big|\leq\|S^{\ast}(\chi)\|\cdot(\tfrac{1}{2^{k_{n}}}+\tfrac{1}{2^{j_{n}}})

and hence
\{S(x_{n})\}_{n\in\omega}
is weakly
p
-Cauchy (resp.,
S(x_{n})
weakly
p
-converges to
S(x)\in S(B_{\ell_{p^{\ast}}})
) and we are done.

Case 2. The sequence
\{x_{n}\}_{n\in\omega}
contains a subsequence
\{x_{n_{k}}\}_{k\in\omega}
weakly convergent in
B_{\ell_{p^{\ast}}}
which does not contain a norm-Cauchy subsequence.

In this case, passing to a subsequence if needed, without loss of generality we can assume that
\{x_{n}\}_{n\in\omega}
weakly converges to some element
x\in B_{\ell_{p^{\ast}}}
. Since it does not contain a norm-Cauchy subsequence, there is
\varepsilon>0
such that
\|x_{n}-x\|_{\ell_{p^{\ast}}}\geq\varepsilon>0
for every
n\in\omega
.
Then, by Proposition 2.1.3 of
[
1
]
(see also Theorem 1.3.2 of
[
1
]
), there are a basic subsequence
\{x_{n_{k}}-x\}_{k\in\omega}\subseteq\ell_{p^{\ast}}
of
\{x_{n}-x\}_{n\in\omega}
and a linear topological isomorphism
R:\overline{\mathsf{span}}\big(\{x_{n_{k}}-x\}_{k\in\omega}\big)\to\ell_{p^{\ast}}
such that (recall that
(e_{k}^{\ast})
is the standard unit basis of
\ell_{p^{\ast}}
)

R(x_{n_{k}}-x)=a_{k}e^{\ast}_{k}\quad\mbox{ for every $k\in\omega$, where }\;a_{k}=\|x_{n_{k}}-x\|_{\ell_{p^{\ast}}},

(13.1)

and such that
V:=\overline{\mathsf{span}}\big(\{x_{n_{k}}-x\}_{k\in\omega}\big)
is complemented in
\ell_{p^{\ast}}
. Note that

\varepsilon\leq a_{k}=\|x_{n_{k}}-x\|_{\ell_{p^{\ast}}}\leq 2\quad\mbox{ for every $k\in\omega$},

and therefore the map
Q:\ell_{p^{\ast}}\to\ell_{p^{\ast}}
defined by
Q(\xi_{k}):=(a_{k}\xi_{k})
is a topological linear isomorphism. Hence, replacing
R
by
Q^{-1}\circ R
, without loss of generality we can assume that
a_{k}=1
for every
k\in\omega
.

Subcase 2.1. Let
S:\ell_{p^{\ast}}^{0}\to E
be an operator.

Since the sequence
\{x_{n}\}
was arbitrary, to show that
S
is weakly sequentially
p
-precompact it is sufficient to prove that the subsequence
\{S(x_{n_{k}})\}_{k\in\omega}
is weakly
p
-Cauchy in
E
. To this end, it suffices to show that
\big\{S\big(x_{n_{k_{j}}}-x_{n_{k_{j+1}}}\big)\big\}_{k\in\omega}
is weakly
p
-summable for every strictly increasing sequence
(k_{j})
in
\omega
.

Let
H_{(k_{j})}:=\mathsf{span}\big\{x_{n_{k_{j}}}-x_{n_{k_{j+1}}}\big\}_{j\in\omega}\subseteq V
,
R_{(k_{j})}
be the restriction of
R
onto
H_{(k_{j})}
, and let
X_{(k_{j})}:=R_{(k_{j})}\big(H_{(k_{j})}\big)\subseteq\mathsf{span}\{e^{\ast}_{k_{j}}\}_{j\in\omega}
. Then
R_{(k_{j})}
is a linear topological isomorphism. Observe also that
H_{(k_{j})}\subseteq\ell_{p^{\ast}}^{0}
. Let
\mathsf{id}_{(k_{j})}:H_{(k_{j})}\to\ell_{p^{\ast}}^{0}
be the identity embedding. Then we obtain the following two sequences

\textstyle{X_{(k_{j})}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{R_{(k_{j})}^{-1}}
\textstyle{H_{(k_{j})}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathsf{id}_{(k_{j})}}
\textstyle{\ell_{p^{\ast}}^{0}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{S}
\textstyle{E}

and

\textstyle{E^{\prime}_{\beta}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{S^{\ast}}
\textstyle{\ell_{p}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathsf{id}_{(k_{j})}^{\ast}}
\textstyle{\hskip 5.69054pt\big(H_{(k_{j})}\big)^{\prime}_{\beta}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\big(R_{(k_{j})}^{-1}\big)^{\ast}}
\textstyle{\big(X_{(k_{j})}\big)^{\prime}_{\beta}.}

Fix now an arbitrary
\chi\in\ E^{\prime}
and set
\eta:=\big(R_{(k_{j})}^{-1}\big)^{\ast}\circ\mathsf{id}_{(k_{j})}^{\ast}\circ S^{\ast}(\chi)\in\big(X_{(k_{j})}\big)^{\prime}_{\beta}
. Since
X_{(k_{j})}
is a subspace of
W:=\mathsf{span}\{e^{\ast}_{k_{j}}\}_{j\in\omega}
, we can consider
\eta
as an element
(b_{j})
of
\ell_{p}=W^{\prime}_{\beta}
. By (
13.1
), we have
R_{(k_{j})}\big(x_{n_{k_{j}}}-x_{n_{k_{j+1}}}\big)=e^{\ast}_{k_{j}}-e^{\ast}_{k_{j+1}}
and hence

\displaystyle\langle\chi,S\big(x_{n_{k_{j}}}-x_{n_{k_{j+1}}}\big)\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle S^{\ast}(\chi),\mathsf{id}_{(k_{j})}\big(x_{n_{k_{j}}}-x_{n_{k_{j+1}}}\big)\rangle=\Big\langle\big(R_{(k_{j})}^{-1}\big)^{\ast}\circ\mathsf{id}_{(k_{j})}^{\ast}\circ S^{\ast}(\chi),e^{\ast}_{k_{j}}-e^{\ast}_{k_{j+1}}\Big\rangle

\displaystyle=\big\langle(b_{j}),e^{\ast}_{k_{j}}-e^{\ast}_{k_{j+1}}\big\rangle=b_{j}-b_{j+1},

and hence

\sum_{j\in\omega}\big|\langle\chi,S\big(x_{n_{k_{j}}}-x_{n_{k_{j+1}}}\big)\rangle\big|^{p}=\sum_{j\in\omega}\big|b_{j}-b_{j+1}\big|^{p}<\infty.

Thus the sequence
\big\{S\big(x_{n_{k_{j}}}-x_{n_{k_{j+1}}}\big)\big\}_{k\in\omega}\subseteq E
is weakly
p
-summable, as desired.

Subcase 2.2. Let
S:\ell_{p^{\ast}}\to E
be an operator.

Since the sequence
\{x_{n}\}
was arbitrary, to show that
S\big(B_{\ell_{p^{\ast}}}\big)
is weakly sequentially
p
-compact it is sufficient to prove that the sequence
\{S(x_{n_{k}}-x)\}_{k\in\omega}\subseteq E
is weakly
p
-summable, and hence
S(x_{n_{k}})
weakly
p
-converges to
S(x)
. To this end, we denote by
\mathsf{id}_{V}:V\to\ell_{p^{\ast}}
the identity embedding. Then we obtain the following sequences

\textstyle{\ell_{p^{\ast}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{R^{-1}}
\textstyle{V\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathsf{id}_{V}}
\textstyle{\ell_{p^{\ast}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{S}
\textstyle{E}

and

\textstyle{E^{\prime}_{\beta}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{S^{\ast}}
\textstyle{\ell_{p}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathsf{id}_{V}^{\ast}}
\textstyle{V^{\prime}_{\beta}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(R^{-1})^{\ast}}
\textstyle{\ell_{p}.}

Take an arbitrary
\chi\in E^{\prime}
. Set
(b_{k}):=(R^{-1})^{\ast}\circ\mathsf{id}_{V}^{\ast}\circ S^{\ast}(\chi)\in(\ell_{p^{\ast}})^{\prime}=\ell_{p}
. Then (
13.1
) implies

\sum_{k\in\omega}\big|\langle\chi,S(x_{n_{k}}-x)\rangle\big|^{p}=\sum_{k\in\omega}\Big|\big\langle S^{\ast}(\chi),\mathsf{id}_{V}\circ R^{-1}(e^{\ast}_{k})\big\rangle\Big|^{p}=\sum_{k\in\omega}\Big|\Big\langle(b_{k}),e^{\ast}_{k}\Big\rangle\Big|^{p}<\infty.

Thus the sequence
\{S(x_{n_{k}}-x)\}_{k\in\omega}\subseteq E
is weakly
p
-summable.

Case 3. The sequence
\{x_{n}\}_{n\in\omega}
does not contain a weakly convergent subsequence.

Observe first that this case is possible only if
p=1
. (Indeed, if
1<p<\infty
, then the space
\ell_{p^{\ast}}
is separable and reflexive, and hence
B_{\ell_{p^{\ast}}}
is a metric compact space in the weak topology. Thus
\{x_{n}\}_{n\in\omega}
contains weakly convergent (in
\ell_{p^{\ast}}
) subsequences.) So, let
S:c_{0}^{0}\to E
(or
S:c_{0}\to E
) be an operator.

By assumption, the norm closure
\overline{\{x_{n}\}_{n\in\omega}}^{\,\|\cdot\|}
does not contain
0
. Therefore, by the Kadets–Pełczyński Theorem 1.5.6 of
[
1
]
, either (1) the weak closure
K
of
\{x_{n}\}_{n\in\omega}
is weakly compact in
c_{0}
and fails to contain
0
, or (2)
\{x_{n}\}_{n\in\omega}
contains a basic subsequence.
However, the case (1) is impossible. Indeed, since
c_{0}
is a separable metrizable spaces, by
[
66
]
,
K
is cosmic and hence metrizable. Therefore, the sequence
\{x_{n}\}_{n\in\omega}
must contain a weakly convergent subsequence which is impossible by the assumption of Case 3.
Hence passing to a subsequence we can assume that
\{x_{n}\}_{n\in\omega}
is a basic sequence in
c_{0}
.

Considering
c_{0}
as a subspace of
\mathbb{F}^{\omega}
, the bounded sequence
\{x_{n}\}_{n\in\omega}
has a convergent subsequence. Therefore, without loss of generality we can and will assume that
\{x_{n}\}_{n\in\omega}
pointwise converges to a point
z\in\mathbb{F}^{\omega}
.
Let
n_{0}=0
. Since
x_{n_{0}}\in c_{0}
, there is
N_{0}\in\omega
such that
x_{n_{0}}(i)<1
for every
i>N_{0}
. As
x_{n}\to z
in
\mathbb{F}^{\omega}
, there is
n_{1}>n_{0}
such that
|x_{n_{1}}(i)-z(i)|<\tfrac{1}{4}
for every
i\leq N_{0}
. Since
x_{n_{1}}\in c_{0}
, there is
N_{1}>N_{0}
such that
|x_{n_{1}}(i)|<\tfrac{1}{4}
for every
i>N_{1}
. Continuing this process we can find a subsequence
\{x_{n_{k}}\}_{n\in\omega}
of
\{x_{n}\}_{n\in\omega}
which satisfies the following property: there is a strictly increasing sequence
(N_{k})\subseteq\omega
such that for every
k\in\omega
, the following inequalities hold

\displaystyle\big|x_{n_{k+1}}(i)-z(i)\big|<\tfrac{1}{4^{k+1}}\quad\mbox{ for every }\;i\leq N_{k},

(13.2)

\displaystyle\big|x_{n_{k+1}}(i)\big|<\tfrac{1}{4^{k+1}}\quad\mbox{ for every }\;i>N_{k+1}.

Then for every
s>k>0
and each
\eta\in\ell_{1}=(c_{0}^{0})^{\prime}
, (
13.2
) implies (recall that all
x_{n}\in B_{c_{0}}
)

\displaystyle\big|\langle\eta,x_{n_{k}}-x_{n_{s}}\rangle\big|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i\leq N_{k-1}}|\eta(i)|\cdot\tfrac{2}{4^{k}}+\sum_{i=N_{k-1}+1}^{N_{s+1}}|\eta(i)|\cdot 2+\sum_{i>N_{s+1}}|\eta(i)|\cdot\tfrac{2}{4^{k}}

(13.3)

\displaystyle\leq\|\eta\|_{\ell_{1}}\cdot\tfrac{2}{4^{k}}+2\sum_{i=N_{k-1}+1}^{N_{s+1}}|\eta(i)|.

We claim that that the sequence
\{S(x_{n_{k}})\}_{n\in\omega}
is weakly
1
-Cauchy. To this end, fix an arbitrary strictly increasing sequence
(k_{j})\subseteq\omega
. For every
\chi\in E^{\prime}
, let
\eta:=S^{\ast}(\chi)\in\ell_{1}
. Taking into account that
k_{j+1}+1\leq k_{j+3}-1
and hence
N_{k_{j+1}+1}<N_{k_{j+3}-1}+1
, (
13.3
) implies

\sum_{j\in\omega}\big|\big\langle\chi,S\big(x_{n_{k_{j}}}-x_{n_{k_{j+1}}}\big)\big\rangle\big|\leq\sum_{j\in\omega}\tfrac{2}{4^{k_{j}}}\cdot\|\eta\|_{\ell_{1}}+\sum_{j\in\omega}2\sum_{i=N_{k_{j}-1}+1}^{N_{k_{j+1}+1}}|\eta(i)|<\|\eta\|_{\ell_{1}}\cdot\Big(6+\sum_{j\in\omega}\tfrac{2}{4^{k_{j}}}\Big)<\infty

Therefore the sequence
\{S(x_{n_{k}})\}_{n\in\omega}
is weakly
1
-Cauchy. Thus
S\big(B_{c_{0}^{0}}\big)
(or
S\big(B_{c_{0}}\big)
) is weakly sequentially
1
-precompact.

If additionally
E
is weakly sequentially
1
-complete and
S:c_{0}\to E
, the sequence
\{S(x_{n_{k}})\}_{n\in\omega}
weakly
1
-converges to some point of
E
. Thus
S\big(B_{c_{0}}\big)
is weakly sequentially
1
-compact.∎

Corollary 13.11

Let
p\in[1,\infty)
, and let
\Gamma
be an infinite set. Then:

(i)

the identity operators
\mathsf{id}_{\ell_{p^{\ast}}^{0}}:\ell_{p^{\ast}}^{0}(\Gamma)\to\ell_{p^{\ast}}^{0}(\Gamma)
and
\mathsf{id}_{\ell_{p^{\ast}}}:\ell_{p^{\ast}}(\Gamma)\to\ell_{p^{\ast}}(\Gamma)

(
or
\mathsf{id}_{c_{0}^{0}}:c_{0}^{0}(\Gamma)\to c_{0}^{0}(\Gamma)
and
\mathsf{id}_{c_{0}}:c_{0}(\Gamma)\to c_{0}(\Gamma)
if
p=1
)
are weakly sequentially
p
-precompact;

(ii)

(Proposition 1.4 of
[
12
]
)
if
p>1
, then the identity operator
\mathsf{id}_{\ell_{p^{\ast}}}:\ell_{p^{\ast}}(\Gamma)\to\ell_{p^{\ast}}(\Gamma)
is weakly sequentially
p
-compact.

Proof 87

(i) follows from Proposition
13.10
in which
E=\ell_{p^{\ast}}^{0}
,
c_{0}^{0}
,
\ell_{p^{\ast}}^{0}
or
c_{0}
, respectively, and the fact that any sequence sits in a direct summand which is isomorphic to
E
.

(ii) follows from (ii) of Proposition
13.10
and the fact from (i).∎

Remark 13.12

The condition
p\not=1
in (ii) of Corollary
13.11
is essential. Indeed, for
p=1
the sequence
\{e_{0}+\cdots+e_{n}\}_{n\in\omega}\subseteq B_{c_{0}}
(which has no weakly convergent subsequences) does not have weakly
1
-convergent subsequences. Therefore
\mathsf{id}_{c_{0}}
is not weakly sequentially
1
-compact.∎

Corollary 13.13

For every
1<p<\infty
, the space
\ell_{p}
is weakly sequentially
p^{\ast}
-complete.

Proof 88

By (ii) of Proposition
13.10
applied to the identity operator
\mathsf{id}_{\ell_{p}}:\ell_{p}\to\ell_{p}
, we obtain that
B_{\ell_{p}}
is a weakly sequentially
p^{\ast}
-compact subset of
\ell_{p}
. Now, let
S=\{x_{n}\}_{n\in\omega}
be a weakly
p^{\ast}
-Cauchy sequence in
\ell_{p}
. Since
S
is bounded, without loss of generality we can assume that
S\subseteq B_{\ell_{p}}
. Then
S
has a subsequence
\{x_{n_{k}}\}_{k\in\omega}
which weakly
p^{\ast}
-converges to some point
x\in B_{\ell_{p}}
. Since
S
is weakly
p^{\ast}
-Cauchy, Lemma
4.5
implies that also
S
weakly
p^{\ast}
-converges to
x
. Thus
\ell_{p}
is weakly sequentially
p^{\ast}
-complete.∎

Recall (see Definition 2.1 of
[
13
]
) that a Banach space
X
belongs to the class
W_{p}
,
p\in[1,\infty]
, if any bounded sequence admits a weakly
p
-convergent subsequence. We extend this notion as follows.

Definition 13.14

Let
p\in[1,\infty]
. A locally convex space
E
is said to belong to the class
W_{p}
if any bounded subset of
E
is relatively weakly sequentially
p
-compact.∎

The next assertion immediately follows from Definitions
13.1
and
13.14
.

Proposition 13.15

Let
p\in[1,\infty]
, and let a locally convex space
E
belong to the class
W_{p}
. If
E
has the
p
-Schur property, then every bounded subset of
E
is relatively sequentially compact.

Corollary 13.16

Let
1\leq p<\infty
. Then
\ell_{p}\in W_{p}
if and only if
p\geq 2
.

Proof 89

Assume that
1<p<2
. Then
p<p^{\ast}
, and hence, by Proposition
6.5
,
\ell_{p}
has the
p
-Schur property. Recall also that
\ell_{1}
has the Schur property. Since
B_{\ell_{p}}
is not compact,
\ell_{p}
does not belong to
W_{p}
for every
1\leq p<2
.

Assume that
2\leq p<\infty
. By (ii) of Corollary
13.11
,
B_{\ell_{p}}
is weakly sequentially
p^{\ast}
-compact. Since
1<p^{\ast}\leq 2
, we have
p^{\ast}\leq p
. Therefore any weakly
p^{\ast}
-convergent sequence is also weakly
p
-convergent. Thus
B_{\ell_{p}}
is weakly sequentially
p
-compact and hence
\ell_{p}\in W_{p}
.∎

The following theorem generalizes and extends the corresponding assertion for Banach spaces, see
[
12
, p. 45]
and Proposition 13 of
[
42
]
.

Theorem 13.17

Let
p\in[1,\infty)
. For any locally convex spaces
E
and
L
and each weak-weak sequentially continuous linear map
T:E\to L
, the following assertions are equivalent:

(i)

T
is
p
-convergent;

(ii)

T\circ S
is a sequentially precompact operator for any operator
S:\ell_{p^{\ast}}^{0}\to E

(
or
S:c_{0}^{0}\to E
if
p=1
)
; moreover the set
T\circ S(B_{\ell_{p^{\ast}}^{0}})

(
or
T\circ S(B_{c_{0}^{0}})
)
is sequentially precompact in
E
.

If in addition
E
is sequentially complete, then
(i)
and
(ii)
are equivalent to the following

(iii)

T\circ S
is a sequentially precompact operator for any operator
S:\ell_{p^{\ast}}\to E

(
or
S:c_{0}\to E
if
p=1
)
; moreover, the set
T\circ S(B_{\ell_{p^{\ast}}})

(
or
T\circ S(B_{c_{0}})
)
is sequentially precompact in
E
.

If
1<p<\infty
and
E
is sequentially complete, then
(i)
–
(iii)
are equivalent to the following

(iv)

T\circ S
is a sequentially compact operator for any operator
S:\ell_{p^{\ast}}\to E
; moreover, the set
T\circ S(B_{\ell_{p^{\ast}}})
is sequentially compact in
E
.

If
p=1
,
E
and
L
are sequentially complete, and
E
is weakly sequentially
1
-complete, then
(i)
–
(iii)
are equivalent to

(v)

T\circ S
is a sequentially compact operator for any operator
S:c_{0}\to E
; moreover, the set
T\circ S(B_{c_{0}})
is sequentially compact in
E
.

Proof 90

(i)
\Rightarrow
(ii) and (i)
\Rightarrow
(iii): Let
\{x_{n}\}_{n\in\omega}
be an arbitrary sequence in
B_{\ell_{p^{\ast}}^{0}}
(resp.,
B_{c_{0}^{0}}
,
B_{\ell_{p^{\ast}}}
or
B_{c_{0}}
). By Proposition
13.10
, any operator
S:\ell_{p^{\ast}}^{0}\to E
(resp.,
S:c_{0}^{0}\to E
,
S:\ell_{p^{\ast}}\to E
or
S:c_{0}\to E
) is weakly sequentially
p
-precompact. Therefore there is a subsequence
\{x_{n_{k}}\}_{k\in\omega}
of
\{x_{n}\}_{n\in\omega}
such that for every increasing sequence
(k_{j})
in
\omega
, the sequence
\big\{S\big(x_{n_{k_{j}}}-x_{n_{k_{j+1}}}\big)\big\}_{k\in\omega}\subseteq E
is weakly
p
-summable. Since
T
is
p
-convergent it follows that
TS\big(x_{n_{k_{j}}}-x_{n_{k_{j+1}}}\big)\to 0
in the space
L
. As the sequence
(k_{j})
was arbitrary, we obtain that the sequence
\{TS(x_{n_{k}})\}
is Cauchy in
L
. Therefore
TS
is sequentially precompact. Since any sequentially precompact subset is bounded, it follows that the linear map
TS
is bounded and hence continuous.

(ii)
\Rightarrow
(i) and (iii)
\Rightarrow
(i) follow from Proposition
13.7
.

Assume that
1<p<\infty
and
E
is sequentially complete.

(i)
\Rightarrow
(iv) Let
\{x_{n}\}_{n\in\omega}
be an arbitrary sequence in
B_{\ell_{p^{\ast}}}
. Then, by (ii) of Proposition
13.10
, there is a subsequence
\{x_{n_{k}}\}_{k\in\omega}
of
\{x_{n}\}_{n\in\omega}
and
x\in B_{\ell_{p^{\ast}}}
such that the sequence
\big\{S\big(x_{n_{k}}-x\big)\big\}_{k\in\omega}\subseteq E
is weakly
p
-summable. Since
T
is
p
-convergent it follows that
TS\big(x_{n_{k}}-x\big)\to 0
in
E
, and hence
TS\big(x_{n_{k}}\big)\to TS(x)\in TS(B_{\ell_{p^{\ast}}})
. Therefore
TS(B_{\ell_{p^{\ast}}})
is a sequentially compact subset of
L
. As above since any sequentially compact subset is bounded, we obtain that the linear map
TS
is bounded and hence continuous. In particular,
TS
is a sequentially compact operator.

(iv)
\Rightarrow
(iii) is trivial.

Assume that
p=1
,
E
and
L
are sequentially complete, and
E
is weakly sequentially
1
-complete.

(i)
\Rightarrow
(v) Let
\{x_{n}\}_{n\in\omega}
be an arbitrary sequence in
B_{c_{0}}
. Then, by (iii) of Proposition
13.10
, there are a subsequence
\{x_{n_{k}}\}_{k\in\omega}
of
\{x_{n}\}_{n\in\omega}
and
x\in B_{\ell_{p^{\ast}}}
such that
S(x_{n_{k}})
weakly
1
-converges to
S(x)
. Then the
1
-convergence of
T
implies
TS\big(x_{n_{k}}\big)\to TS(x)\in TS(B_{\ell_{p^{\ast}}})
. Therefore the set
T\circ S(B_{c_{0}})
is sequentially compact in
E
, and hence, as above,
TS
is a sequentially compact operator.

(v)
\Rightarrow
(iii) is trivial. ∎

Since in angelic spaces the class of relatively sequentially compact sets coincides with the class of relatively compact sets, Theorem
13.18
implies the following assertion.

Corollary 13.18

Let
p\in[1,\infty)
,
E
be a sequentially complete locally convex space which is weakly
1
-complete if
p=1
, and let
L
be an angelic sequentially complete locally convex space
(
for example
L
is a strict
(LF)
-space
)
. Then for each weak-weak sequentially continuous linear map
T:E\to L
, the following assertions are equivalent:

(i)

T
is
p
-convergent;

(ii)

T\circ S
is sequentially precompact for any operator
S:\ell_{p^{\ast}}^{0}\to E

(
or
S:c_{0}^{0}\to E
if
p=1
)
.

(iii)

T\circ S
is sequentially precompact for any operator
S:\ell_{p^{\ast}}\to E

(
or
S:c_{0}\to E
if
p=1
)
.

(iv)

T\circ S
is sequentially compact for any operator
S:\ell_{p^{\ast}}\to E

(
or
S:c_{0}\to E
if
p=1
)
.

(v)

T\circ S
is compact for any operator
S:\ell_{p^{\ast}}\to E

(
or
S:c_{0}\to E
if
p=1
)
.

The conclusion of Theorem
13.17
is not true in general for the case
p=\infty
as the following example shows.

Example 13.19

Let
p=\infty
,
E=L=\ell_{1}
, and let
T:E\to L
and
S:\ell_{1}^{0}\to E
be the identity maps. Then
T
is
\infty
-convergent, but
T\circ S
is not even weakly sequentially precompact.

Proof 91

Every weakly
\infty
-summable sequence is weakly null, and hence the Schur property of
E
implies that
T
is
\infty
-convergent. The Schur property implies also that the sequence
\{TS(e_{n})\}_{n\in\omega}
does not have a weakly Cauchy subsequence. Thus
T\circ S
is not weakly sequentially precompact.∎

The condition of being sequentially complete in Theorem
13.17
is also essential.
We denote by
\mathbf{s}=\{0\}\cup\{\tfrac{1}{n}:n\in\mathbb{N}\}
the convergent sequence with the topology induced from
\mathbb{R}
.

Example 13.20

Let
p=1
,
E=L=C_{p}(\mathbf{s})
,
T:E\to L
be the identity map, and let
S:c_{0}\to E
be the identity inclusion defined by
S(\mathbf{a}):=f_{\mathbf{a}}
, where
\mathbf{a}=(a_{n})_{n\in\omega}\in c_{0}
and

f_{\mathbf{a}}(0)=0\;\mbox{ and }\;f_{\mathbf{a}}\big(\tfrac{1}{n}\big):=a_{n-1}\;\mbox{ for }\;n\geq 1.

Then
E
is not sequentially complete,
T
is
1
-convergent, but
T\circ S
is not sequentially compact.

Proof 92

The space
E=C_{p}(\mathbf{s})
is not sequentially complete by Theorem
3.15
. The operator
T
is
1
-convergent by Proposition
13.6
. To show that
TS
is not sequentially compact, consider the sequence
\{\mathbf{a}_{n}\}_{n\in\omega}
in
B_{c_{0}}
where
\mathbf{a}_{n}:=(\underbrace{1,\dots,1}_{n},0,\dots)
. Then
TS(\mathbf{a}_{n})=f_{\mathbf{a}_{n}}
pointwise converges to the function which is discontinuous at
0
. Therefore the sequence
\big\{TS(\mathbf{a}_{n})\big\}_{n}
does not have a convergent subsequence. Thus
TS(B_{c_{0}})
is not a relatively sequentially compact subset of
L
.∎

Below we show that in general one cannot replace
\ell^{0}_{p^{\ast}}
in (ii) of Theorem
13.17
by
\ell_{p^{\ast}}
without the condition on
E
being sequentially complete.

Example 13.21

Let
E=L=\ell_{2}^{0}
,
T:E\to L
be the identity map. Then
E
is not sequentially complete,
T
is not
2
-convergent, but
TS
is finite-dimensional for every
S\in\mathcal{L}(\ell_{2},E)
.

Proof 93

The normed space
\ell_{p}^{0}
is not even locally complete because it is not complete, see Corollary 5.1.9 of
[
72
]
. To show that
T
is not
2
-convergent, observe that the canonical basis
\{e_{n}\}_{n\in\omega}
of
E
is weakly
2
-summable because for every
\chi\in E^{\prime}=\ell_{2}
we have
(\langle\chi,e_{n}\rangle)_{n\in\omega}=\chi\in\ell_{2}
. However,
T(e_{n})=e_{n}\not\to 0
in
L
. Thus
T
is not
2
-convergent. The last assertion follows from the next claim.

Claim 1: For every
p\in[1,\infty)
, each operator
S\in\mathcal{L}(\ell_{p},\ell_{p}^{0})
has finite rank.

Indeed, since
\ell_{p}^{0}=\bigcup_{n\in\omega}\mathsf{span}(e_{0},\dots,e_{n})
, we obtain
\ell_{p}=\bigcup_{n\in\omega}S^{-1}\big(\mathsf{span}(e_{0},\dots,e_{n})\big)
. By the Baire property of
\ell_{p}
, there is
m\in\omega
such that the closed subspace
S^{-1}\big(\mathsf{span}(e_{0},\dots,e_{m})\big)
of
\ell_{p}
has a non-empty interior. Thus
\ell_{p}=S^{-1}\big(\mathsf{span}(e_{0},\dots,e_{m})\big)
, and hence
S(\ell_{p})
is finite-dimensional.∎

Now we characterize locally convex spaces with the
p
-Schur property.

Proposition 13.22

Let
p\in[1,\infty]
, and let
E
be a locally convex space. Consider the following assertions:

(i)

the identity map
\mathsf{id}_{E}:E\to E
is
p
-convergent, i.e.,
E
has the
p
-Schur property;

(ii)

every
(
relatively
)
weakly sequentially
p
-compact subset
A
of
E
is
(
resp., relatively
)
sequentially compact in
E
;

(iii)

every weakly sequentially
p
-precompact subset
A
of
E
is sequentially precompact in
E
.

Then
(i)
\Leftrightarrow
(ii)
\Rightarrow
(iii)
. If in addition
E
is sequentially complete, then the implication
(iii)
\Rightarrow
(i)
is also satisfied.

Proof 94

(i)
\Rightarrow
(ii) Assume that
E
has the
p
-Schur property, and let
A
be a (relatively) weakly sequentially
p
-compact subset of
E
. Fix an arbitrary sequence
\{a_{n}\}_{n\in\omega}
in
A
. Then it has a subsequence
\{a_{n_{k}}\}_{k\in\omega}
weakly
p
-converging to an element
a\in A
(resp.,
a\in E
). Since
E
has the
p
-Schur property, we obtain that
a_{n_{k}}\to a
in
E
. Thus
A
is (resp., relatively) sequentially compact in
E
.

(ii)
\Rightarrow
(i) Assume that every (relatively) weakly sequentially
p
-compact subset
A
of
E
is (resp., relatively) sequentially compact in
E
. Take an arbitrary weakly
p
-summable sequence
S=\{x_{n}\}_{n\in\omega}
in
E
. Then
S\cup\{0\}
is a weakly sequentially
p
-compact set. By assumption,
S
is (resp., relatively) sequentially compact in
E
. Now suppose for a contradiction that
x_{n}\not\to 0
in
E
. Passing to a subsequence if needed, without loss of generality we can assume that there is
U\in\mathcal{N}_{0}(E)
such that
x_{n}\not\in U
for every
n\in\omega
. Since
S
is relatively sequentially compact in
E
, there is a subsequence
\{x_{n_{k}}\}_{k\in\omega}
of
\{x_{n}\}_{n\in\omega}
which converges (in
E
) to some element
x
. Clearly,
x\not=0
and
x_{n_{k}}\to x
weakly. On the other hand, since
S
is weakly null,
\{x_{n_{k}}\}_{k\in\omega}
is also weakly null and hence it can converge only to zero. Whence
x=0
, a contradiction. Therefore
S
is a null-sequence. Thus
E
has the
p
-Schur property.

(i)
\Rightarrow
(iii) Assume that
E
has the
p
-Schur property, and let
A
be a weakly sequentially
p
-precompact subset of
E
. Let
\{a_{n}\}_{n\in\omega}
be a sequence in
A
. Then it has a weakly
p
-Cauchy subsequence
\{a_{n_{k}}\}_{k\in\omega}
. Therefore for every sequence
(k_{j})
in
\omega
, the sequence
\{a_{n_{k_{j}}}-a_{n_{k_{j+1}}}\}_{k\in\omega}
is weakly
p
-summable. By the
p
-Schur property,
a_{n_{k_{j}}}-a_{n_{k_{j+1}}}\to 0
in
E
. But this means that
\{a_{n_{k}}\}_{k\in\omega}
is a Cauchy sequence in
E
. Thus
A
is sequentially precompact.

(iii)
\Rightarrow
(i) Assume that
E
is sequentially complete and every weakly sequentially
p
-precompact subset
A
of
E
is sequentially precompact in
E
. Take an arbitrary weakly
p
-summable sequence
S=\{x_{n}\}_{n\in\omega}
in
E
. Then
S
is weakly sequentially
p
-precompact and hence sequentially precompact in
E
. Suppose for a contradiction that
x_{n}\not\to 0
in
E
. Then, passing to a subsequence if needed, we assume that there is an open
U\in\mathcal{N}_{0}(E)
such that
x_{n}\not\in U
for every
n\in\omega
. Since
S
is sequentially precompact, there is a Cauchy subsequence
\{x_{n_{k}}\}_{k\in\omega}
of
\{x_{n}\}_{n\in\omega}
. As
E
is sequentially complete, there is
x\in E{\setminus}U
such that
x_{n_{k}}\to x
. Since
S
is weakly null,
\{x_{n_{k}}\}_{k\in\omega}
is also weakly null and hence it can converge only to zero. As
x\not=0
, we obtain a contradiction. Thus
x_{n}\to 0
in
E
, and hence
E
has the
p
-Schur property.∎

Theorem
13.17
applied to the identity map
T=\mathsf{id}_{E}:E\to E
immediately implies the following operator characterization of the
p
-Schur property.

Corollary 13.23

Let
p\in[1,\infty)
, and let
E
be a locally convex space. Then the following assertions are equivalent:

(i)

the identity operator
\mathsf{id}_{E}:E\to E
is
p
-convergent, i.e.,
E
has the
p
-Schur property;

(ii)

any operator
S:\ell_{p^{\ast}}^{0}\to E

(
or
S:c_{0}^{0}\to E
if
p=1
)
is sequentially precompact.

If
E
is sequentially complete, then
(i)
and
(ii)
are equivalent to the following

(iii)

any operator
S:\ell_{p^{\ast}}\to E

(
or
S:c_{0}\to E
if
p=1
)
is sequentially precompact.

If
1<p<\infty
and
E
is sequentially complete, then
(i)
–
(iii)
are equivalent to the following

(iv)

any operator
S:\ell_{p^{\ast}}\to E
is a sequentially compact.

If
p=1
and
E
is sequentially complete and weakly sequentially
1
-complete, then
(i)
–
(iii)
are equivalent to

(v)

any operator
S:c_{0}\to E
is sequentially compact.

Remark 13.24

It is natural to ask whether
T^{\ast\ast}
of a
p
-convergent operator is also
p
-convergent. A negative answer to this question is given in Theorem 2.7 of
[
16
]
.∎

14
Pełczyński’s type sets and
p
-convergent operators

Let
E
and
L
be locally convex spaces, and let
T:E\to L
be an operator.
According to Lemma
7.2
, if
A
is a
(p,q)
-
(V^{\ast})
set then so is its image
T(A)
, and, by Lemmas
8.2
and
8.6
, if
B
is a
(p,q)
-
(V)
set or a weak
∗

(p,q)
-
(V)
set in
E^{\prime}
, then so is
T^{\ast}(B)
. Taking into account that
A
is a bounded subset of
E
and
B
is a weak
∗
bounded subset of
E^{\prime}
, these results motivate the following problems:

(1)

Characterize those operators
T
which map
all
bounded sets into
(p,q)
-
(V^{\ast})
sets.

(2)

Characterize those operators
T
such that
T^{\ast}
maps
all
weak
∗
bounded sets or strongly bounded sets into
(p,q)
-
(V)
sets or weak
∗

(p,q)
-
(V)
sets.

The purpose of this section is to give complete answers to problems (1) and (2) for the
(p,\infty)
-case in the general case when
E
and
L
are locally convex spaces. We are interested in this special case also because it is dually connected with
p
-convergent operators, see in particular Theorems
14.1
and
14.6
.

If
1\leq p<\infty
, a characterization of operators
T
between Banach spaces for which
T^{\ast}
is
p
-convergent was obtained by Ghenciu
[
42
]
. Below we generalize this result.

Theorem 14.1

Let
p\in[1,\infty]
, and let
T:E\to L
be an operator between locally convex spaces
E
and
L
. Then the following assertions are equivalent:

(i)

for every
B\in\mathsf{Bo}(E)
, the image
T(B)
is a
p
-
(V^{\ast})
set in
L
;

(ii)

T^{\ast}:L^{\prime}_{\beta}\to E^{\prime}_{\beta}
is
p
-convergent.

If
1\leq p<\infty
, then (i) and (ii) are equivalent to

(iii)

T^{\ast}\circ S
is sequentially precompact for any operator
S:\ell_{p^{\ast}}^{0}\to L^{\prime}_{\beta}

(
or
S:c_{0}^{0}\to L^{\prime}_{\beta}
if
p=1
)
.

If
1<p<\infty
and
L^{\prime}_{\beta}
is sequentially complete, then
(i)-(iii)
are equivalent to the following

(iv)

T^{\ast}\circ S
is sequentially compact for any operator
S:\ell_{p^{\ast}}\to L^{\prime}_{\beta}
;

(v)

T^{\ast}\circ S
is sequentially precompact for any operator
S:\ell_{p^{\ast}}\to L^{\prime}_{\beta}
.

If
p=1
,
E^{\prime}_{\beta}
and
L^{\prime}_{\beta}
are sequentially complete and
L^{\prime}_{\beta}
is weakly sequentially
1
-complete, then
(i)-(iii)
are equivalent to the following

(vi)

T^{\ast}\circ S
is sequentially compact for any operator
S:c_{0}\to L^{\prime}_{\beta}
;

(vii)

T^{\ast}\circ S
is sequentially precompact for any operator
S:c_{0}\to L^{\prime}_{\beta}
.

Proof 95

(i)
\Rightarrow
(ii) Let
\{\chi_{n}\}_{n\in\omega}
be a weakly
p
-summable sequence in
L^{\prime}_{\beta}
. We show that
T^{\ast}(\chi_{n})\to 0
in
E^{\prime}_{\beta}
. To this end, fix an arbitrary
B\in\mathsf{Bo}(E)
. Since
T(B)
is a
p
-
(V^{\ast})
set in
L
we have

\sup_{b\in B}\big|\langle T^{\ast}(\chi_{n}),b\rangle\big|=\sup_{b\in B}\big|\langle\chi_{n},T(b)\rangle\big|\to 0\;\mbox{ as }\;n\to\infty,

and hence
T^{\ast}(\chi_{n})\in B^{\circ}
for all sufficiently large
n\in\omega
. Since
B
was arbitrary this means that
T^{\ast}(\chi_{n})\to 0
in
E^{\prime}_{\beta}
, as desired.

(ii)
\Rightarrow
(i) Let
B\in\mathsf{Bo}(E)
. To show that
T(B)
is a
p
-
(V^{\ast})
set in
L
, take any weakly
p
-summable sequence
\{\chi_{n}\}_{n\in\omega}
in
L^{\prime}_{\beta}
. For every
\varepsilon>0
, the polar
\varepsilon B^{\circ}=\big(\tfrac{1}{\varepsilon}B\big)^{\circ}
is a neighborhood of zero in
E^{\prime}_{\beta}
. Since
T^{\ast}
is
p
-convergent, we have
T^{\ast}(\chi_{n})\to 0
in
E^{\prime}_{\beta}
and hence there is
N_{\varepsilon}\in\omega
such that
T^{\ast}(\chi_{n})\in\varepsilon B^{\circ}
for all
n\geq N_{\varepsilon}
. Therefore

\sup_{b\in B}\big|\langle\chi_{n},T(b)\rangle\big|=\sup_{b\in B}\big|\langle T^{\ast}(\chi_{n}),b\rangle\big|\leq\varepsilon\;\mbox{ for all }\;n\geq N_{\varepsilon}.

As
\varepsilon
was arbitrary it follows that
\sup_{b\in B}\big|\langle\chi_{n},T(b)\rangle\big|\to 0
. Thus
T(B)
is a
p
-
(V^{\ast})
set.

All other assertions and equivalences follow from Theorem
13.17
applied to
E_{1}=L^{\prime}_{\beta}
,
L_{1}=E^{\prime}_{\beta}
and
T_{1}=T^{\ast}
.∎

Theorem
14.1
applied to the identity map
T=\mathsf{id}_{E}:E\to E
immediately implies the next corollary in which the equivalence (i)
\Leftrightarrow
(ii) is proved in Theorem
7.8
.

Corollary 14.2

Let
p\in[1,\infty]
, and let
E
be a locally convex space. Then the following conditions are equivalent

(i)

every bounded subset of
E
is a
p
-
(V^{\ast})
set
\big(
i.e.,
\mathsf{Bo}(E)=\mathsf{V}^{\ast}_{p}(E)
\big)
;

(ii)

the identity map
\mathsf{id}_{E^{\prime}}:E^{\prime}_{\beta}\to E^{\prime}_{\beta}
is
p
-convergent, i.e.,
E^{\prime}_{\beta}
has the
p
-Schur property.

If
1\leq p<\infty
, then (i) and (ii) are equivalent to

(iii)

any operator
S:\ell_{p^{\ast}}^{0}\to E^{\prime}_{\beta}

(
or
S:c_{0}^{0}\to E^{\prime}_{\beta}
if
p=1
)
is sequentially precompact.

If
1<p<\infty
and
E^{\prime}_{\beta}
is sequentially complete, then
(i)-(iii)
are equivalent to the following

(iv)

any operator
S:\ell_{p^{\ast}}\to E^{\prime}_{\beta}
is sequentially compact;

(v)

any operator
S:\ell_{p^{\ast}}\to E^{\prime}_{\beta}
is sequentially precompact.

If
p=1
,
E^{\prime}_{\beta}
is sequentially complete and weakly sequentially
1
-complete, then
(i)-(iii)
are equivalent to the following

(vi)

any operator
S:c_{0}\to E^{\prime}_{\beta}
is sequentially compact;

(vii)

any operator
S:c_{0}\to E^{\prime}_{\beta}
is sequentially precompact.

Let
1\leq p\leq q\leq\infty
, and let
E
be a locally convex space. According to Lemma
7.2
the family
\mathsf{V}^{\ast}_{(p,q)}(E)
of all
(p,q)
-
(V^{\ast})
sets in
E
is a saturated bornology containing all finite subsets of
E
. Therefore one can naturally define:

Definition 14.3

Let
1\leq p\leq q\leq\infty
, and let
E
be a locally convex space. Denote by
V^{\ast}_{(p,q)}(E^{\prime},E)
the polar topology on
E^{\prime}
of uniform convergence on
(p,q)
-
(V^{\ast})
sets. Set
V^{\ast}_{p}(E^{\prime},E)=V^{\ast}_{(p,\infty)}(E^{\prime},E)
and
V^{\ast}(E^{\prime},E)=V^{\ast}_{(\infty,\infty)}(E^{\prime},E)
.∎

Since the family
\mathsf{V}^{\ast}_{(p,q)}(E)
depends only on the duality, the topology
V^{\ast}_{(p,q)}(E^{\prime},E)
is a dual topology. For further references we select the next simple lemma.

Proposition 14.4

Let
1\leq p\leq q\leq\infty
, and let
E
be a locally convex space. Then:

(i)

\sigma(E^{\prime},E)\subseteq V^{\ast}_{(p,q)}(E^{\prime},E)\subseteq\beta(E^{\prime},E)
,

(ii)

V^{\ast}_{(p,q)}(E^{\prime},E)\subseteq\mu(E^{\prime},E)
if and only if every
(p,q)
-
(V^{\ast})
set
A
in
E
is relatively weakly compact. Consequently,
E
has the property
V^{\ast}_{(p,q)}
if and only if the topology
V^{\ast}_{(p,q)}(E^{\prime},E)
is compatible with the duality
(E,E^{\prime})
.

Proof 96

(i) is clear, and
(ii) follows from the Mackey–Arens theorem and the fact that
\mathsf{V}^{\ast}_{(p,q)}(E)
is a saturated bornology. ∎

The next result naturally complements Theorem
14.1
.

Proposition 14.5

Let
1\leq p\leq q\leq\infty
, and let
T:E\to L
be an operator between locally convex spaces
E
and
L
. Then:

(i)

the adjoint map
T^{\ast}:\big(L^{\prime},V^{\ast}_{(p,q)}(L^{\prime},L)\big)\to\big(E^{\prime},V^{\ast}_{(p,q)}(E^{\prime},E)\big)
is continuous;

(ii)

the adjoint map
T^{\ast}:L^{\prime}_{\beta}\to\big(E^{\prime},V^{\ast}_{p}(E^{\prime},E)\big)
is
p
-convergent.

Proof 97

(i) To show that
T^{\ast}
is continuous, let
A^{\circ}
be a standard
V^{\ast}_{(p,q)}(E^{\prime},E)
-neighborhood of zero, where
A
is a
(p,q)
-
(V^{\ast})
set in
E
. By Lemma
7.2
,
T(A)
is a
(p,q)
-
(V^{\ast})
set in
L
. Then for every
\eta\in T(A)^{\circ}
and each
a\in A
, we have
|\langle T^{\ast}(\eta),a\rangle|=|\langle\eta,T(a)\rangle|\leq 1
and hence
T^{\ast}\big(T(A)^{\circ}\big)\subseteq A^{\circ}
. Thus
T^{\ast}
is continuous.

(ii) Let
\{\chi_{n}\}_{n\in\omega}
be a weakly
p
-summable sequence in
L^{\prime}_{\beta}
. To show that
T^{\ast}(\chi_{n})\to 0
in
\big(E^{\prime},V^{\ast}_{p}(E^{\prime},E)\big)
, fix an arbitrary
B\in\mathsf{V}^{\ast}_{p}(E)
. By Lemma
7.2
,
T(B)
is a
p
-
(V^{\ast})
set in
L
and hence

\lim_{n\to\infty}\sup_{b\in B}\big|\langle T^{\ast}(\chi_{n}),b\rangle\big|=\lim_{n\to\infty}\sup_{b\in B}\big|\langle\chi_{n},T(b)\rangle\big|=0.

Therefore
T^{\ast}(\chi_{n})\in B^{\circ}
for all sufficiently large
n\in\omega
. Since
B
was arbitrary this means that
T^{\ast}(\chi_{n})\to 0
in
V^{\ast}_{p}(E^{\prime},E)
, as desired.∎

Below we give sufficient and necessary conditions on operators to be
p
-convergent.

Theorem 14.6

Let
p\in[1,\infty]
,
E
and
L
be locally convex spaces, and let
T\in\mathcal{L}(E,L)
.

(i)

If
T^{\ast}(B)
is a
p
-
(V)
set in
E^{\prime}
for every
B\in\mathsf{Bo}(L^{\prime}_{\beta})
, then
T
is
p
-convergent. The converse assertion is true if
L
is quasibarrelled.

(ii)

If
T^{\ast}(B)
is a
p
-
(V)
set in
E^{\prime}
for every
B\in\mathsf{Bo}(L^{\prime}_{w^{\ast}})
, then
T
is
p
-convergent. The converse assertion is true if
L
is barrelled.

Proof 98

Assume that
T^{\ast}(B)
is a
p
-
(V)
set in
E^{\prime}
for every
B\in\mathsf{Bo}(L^{\prime}_{\beta})
(resp.,
B\in\mathsf{Bo}(L^{\prime}_{w^{\ast}})
). Fix an arbitrary weakly
p
-summable sequence
\{x_{n}\}_{n\in\omega}
in
E
. We have to show that
T(x_{n})\to 0
in
L
. To this end, fix
U\in\mathcal{N}_{0}^{c}(L)
. Then the polar
B:=U^{\circ}
is strongly (hence weak
∗
) bounded by Theorem 11.3.5 of
[
67
]
, and hence
T^{\ast}(B)
is a
p
-
(V)
set in
E^{\prime}
. Consequently, by the definition of
p
-
(V)
sets, we have

\lim_{n\to\infty}\sup_{\chi\in B}|\langle\chi,T(x_{n})\rangle|=\lim_{n\to\infty}\sup_{\chi\in B}|\langle T^{\ast}(\chi),x_{n}\rangle|=0.

In particular, for all sufficiently large
n\in\omega
, it follows that
|\langle\chi,T(x_{n})\rangle|\leq 1
for every
\chi\in B=U^{\circ}
. This means that
T(x_{n})\in U^{\circ\circ}=U
for all sufficiently large
n\in\omega
. Thus
T(x_{n})\to 0
in
L
and hence
T
is
p
-convergent.

Conversely, assume additionally that
L
is quasibarrelled (resp., barrelled) and let
T
be
p
-convergent. Let
B\in\mathsf{Bo}(L^{\prime}_{\beta})
(resp.,
B\in\mathsf{Bo}(L^{\prime}_{w^{\ast}})
). We have to show that

\lim_{n\to\infty}\sup_{\chi\in B}|\langle T^{\ast}(\chi),x_{n}\rangle|=\lim_{n\to\infty}\sup_{\chi\in B}|\langle\chi,T(x_{n})\rangle|=0

for every weakly
p
-summable sequence
\{x_{n}\}_{n\in\omega}
in
E
. To this end, fix
\varepsilon>0
. Since
T
is
p
-convergent,
T(x_{n})\to 0
in
L
. As
L
is quasibarrelled (resp., barrelled) the set
B
is equicontinuous. Take
U\in\mathcal{N}_{0}(L)
such that
B\subseteq U^{\circ}
. Choose
N_{\varepsilon}\in\omega
such that
T(x_{n})\in\varepsilon U
for every
n\geq N_{\varepsilon}
. Then for every
n\geq N_{\varepsilon}
, we obtain

\sup_{\chi\in B}|\langle\chi,T(x_{n})\rangle|\leq\sup\big\{\sup_{\chi\in B}|\langle\chi,y\rangle|:y\in\varepsilon U\big\}\leq\varepsilon.

Thus
\sup_{\chi\in B}|\langle T^{\ast}(\chi),x_{n}\rangle|\to 0
, as desired.∎

Corollary 14.7

Let
p\in[1,\infty]
,
E
be a locally convex space with the property
V_{p}

(
resp., the property
sV_{p}
)
, and let
L
be a quasibarrelled space. If an operator
T:E\to L
is
p
-convergent, then the adjoint
T^{\ast}:L^{\prime}_{\beta}\to E^{\prime}_{\beta}
maps bounded sets of
L^{\prime}_{\beta}
into relatively weakly
(
resp., sequentially
)
compact sets of
E^{\prime}_{\beta}
. Consequently, if
L
is a normed space and
T
is
p
-convergent, then
T^{\ast}
is weakly compact
(
resp., weakly sequentially compact
)
.

Now we summarize characterizations of the
p
-Schur property of an lcs
E
and its strong dual
E^{\prime}_{\beta}
.

Corollary 14.8

Let
p\in[1,\infty]
, and let
E
be a locally convex space. Then:

(i)

if
E
is quasibarrelled, then
\mathsf{Bo}(E^{\prime}_{\beta})\subseteq\mathsf{V}_{p}(E^{\prime})
if and only if
E
has the
p
-Schur property;

(ii)

if
E
is barrelled, then
\mathsf{Bo}(E^{\prime}_{w^{\ast}})=\mathsf{V}_{p}(E^{\prime})
if and only
E
has the
p
-Schur property;

(iii)

\mathsf{Bo}(E)=\mathsf{V}_{p}^{\ast}(E)
if and only if
E^{\prime}_{\beta}
has the
p
-Schur property.

Proof 99

(i) follows from Theorem
14.6
applied to the identity map
\mathsf{id}_{E}:E\to E
.

(ii) By Theorem
14.6
applied to the identity map
\mathsf{id}_{E}:E\to E
, we obtain that
\mathsf{Bo}(E^{\prime}_{w^{\ast}})\subseteq\mathsf{V}_{p}(E^{\prime})
if and only
E
has the
p
-Schur property. It remains to note that
\mathsf{V}_{p}(E^{\prime})\subseteq\mathsf{Bo}(E^{\prime}_{w^{\ast}})
always by (ii) of Lemma
8.2
.

(iii) is Theorem
7.8
.∎

Below we give a useful construction of
p
-convergent operators from
\ell_{1}^{0}
into locally convex spaces.

Proposition 14.9

Let
p\in[1,\infty]
,
\{x_{n}\}_{n\in\omega}
be a bounded sequence in a locally convex space
(E,\tau)
, and let
T:\ell_{1}^{0}\to E
be a linear map defined by

T(a_{0}e_{0}+\cdots+a_{n}e_{n}):=a_{0}x_{0}+\cdots+a_{n}x_{n}\quad(n\in\omega,\;a_{0},\dots,a_{n}\in\mathbb{F}),

where
\{e_{n}\}
is the canonical unit basis of
\ell_{1}^{0}
. Then
T
is continuous. Moreover, if
E
is locally complete, then
T
can be extended to a continuous operator from
\ell_{1}
to
E
. In any case, if
\{x_{n}\}_{n\in\omega}
is a
p
-
(V^{\ast})
set, then
T^{\ast}:E^{\prime}_{\beta}\to\ell_{\infty}
is
p
-convergent.

Proof 100

Denote by
A
the closed absolutely convex hull of
\{x_{n}\}_{n\in\omega}
. Then
A
is a bounded subset of
E
(in the cases when
\{x_{n}\}_{n\in\omega}
is a
p
-
(V^{\ast})
set, Lemma
7.2
implies that
A
is also a
p
-
(V^{\ast})
set). Consider the normed space
(E_{A},\|\cdot\|)
and observe that
\|x_{n}\|\leq 1
for every
n\in\omega
. Therefore, if
|a_{0}|+\cdots+|a_{n}|\leq 1
we obtain

\big\|T(a_{0}e_{0}+\cdots+a_{n}e_{n})\big\|=\|a_{0}x_{0}+\cdots+a_{n}x_{n}\|\leq 1

and hence
T:\ell_{1}^{0}\to(E_{A},\|\cdot\|)
is continuous. In the case when
E
is locally complete, by Proposition
3.6
,
(E_{A},\|\cdot\|)
is a Banach space and hence
T
can be extended to a continuous operator from
\ell_{1}
to
E_{A}
. Since, by Proposition
3.6
, the norm topology on
E_{A}
is finer than the original topology
\tau{\restriction}_{E_{A}}
it follows that
T:\ell_{1}^{0}\to E
(or
T:\ell_{1}\to E
if
E
is locally complete) is continuous.

Assume now that
\{x_{n}\}_{n\in\omega}
is a
p
-
(V^{\ast})
set.
To show that the adjoint operator
T^{\ast}
is
p
-convergent, let
\{\chi_{n}\}_{n\in\omega}
be a weakly
p
-summable sequence in
E^{\prime}_{\beta}
. Since
A
is a
p
-
(V^{\ast})
set, by definition, we have
\lim_{n\to\infty}\sup_{x\in A}|\langle\chi_{n},x\rangle|=0
. Therefore

\|T^{\ast}(\chi_{n})\|_{\ell_{\infty}}=\sup_{k\in\omega}\big|\langle T^{\ast}(\chi_{n}),e_{k}\rangle\big|=\sup_{k\in\omega}\big|\langle\chi_{n},x_{k}\rangle\big|\leq\sup_{x\in A}\big|\langle\chi_{n},x\rangle\big|\to 0\;\;\mbox{ as }\;n\to\infty.

Thus
\big\{T^{\ast}(\chi_{n})\big\}_{n\in\omega}
is a null sequence in
\ell_{\infty}
, and hence
T^{\ast}
is
p
-convergent.∎

We know that
p
-
(V^{\ast})
sets are bounded. Below we give an operator characterization of those spaces
E
in which the
p
-
(V^{\ast})
sets have a stronger topological property than just being bounded as, for example, being weakly sequentially (pre)compact. The following theorem extends Theorem 15 of
[
42
]
.

Theorem 14.10

Let
p\in[1,\infty]
, and let
(E,\tau)
be a locally convex space. Then the following assertions are equivalent:

(i)

for every normed space
Y
, if
T\in\mathcal{L}(Y,E)
is such that the adjoint operator
T^{\ast}:E^{\prime}_{\beta}\to Y^{\prime}_{\beta}
is
p
-convergent, then
T
is weakly sequentially precompact
(
resp., weakly sequentially compact, sequentially precompact or sequentially compact
)
;

(ii)

the same as
(i)
with
Y=\ell_{1}^{0}
;

(iii)

every
p
-
(V^{\ast})
subset of
E
is weakly sequentially precompact
(
resp., relatively weakly sequentially compact, sequentially precompact or relatively sequentially compact
)
.

If additionally
E
is locally complete, then (i)-(iii) are equivalent to

(iv)

the same as
(i)
with
Y=\ell_{1}
.

Proof 101

(i)
\Rightarrow
(ii) is clear.

(ii)
\Rightarrow
(iii) Let
A
be a
p
-
(V^{\ast})
subset of
E
. To show that
A
is weakly sequentially precompact (resp., relatively weakly sequentially compact, sequentially precompact or relatively sequentially compact), take an arbitrary sequence
\{x_{n}\}_{n\in\omega}
in
A
and define
T:\ell_{1}^{0}\to E
by

T(a_{0}e_{0}+\cdots+a_{n}e_{n}):=a_{0}x_{0}+\cdots+a_{n}x_{n}\quad(n\in\omega,\;a_{0},\dots,a_{n}\in\mathbb{F}).

Then, by Proposition
14.9
,
T
is continuous and such that
T^{\ast}:E^{\prime}_{\beta}\to\ell_{\infty}
is
p
-convergent.

By the assumption of (ii), we obtain that
T
is weakly sequentially precompact (resp., weakly sequentially compact, sequentially precompact or sequentially compact). By definition this means that the sequence
\{x_{n}\}=\big\{T(e_{n})\big\}
has a subsequence which is weakly Cauchy (resp., weakly convergent in
E
, Cauchy in
E
or convergent in
E
). Thus
A
is a weakly sequentially precompact (resp., relatively weakly sequentially compact, sequentially precompact or relatively sequentially compact) subset of
L
, as desired.

(iii)
\Rightarrow
(i) To prove this implication it suffices to show that
T(B_{Y})
is weakly sequentially precompact (resp., relatively weakly sequentially compact, sequentially precompact or relatively sequentially compact) in
E
under the assumption that
T^{\ast}
is
p
-convergent. To this end, we note that, by the equivalence (i)
\Leftrightarrow
(ii) of Theorem
14.1
, the
p
-convergence of
T^{\ast}
and the boundedness of
B_{Y}
imply that the set
T(B_{Y})
is a
p
-
(V^{\ast})
set, and hence, by (iii),
T(B_{Y})
is weakly sequentially precompact (resp., relatively weakly sequentially compact, sequentially precompact or relatively sequentially compact), as desired.

Assume now that
E
is locally complete. Then the implication (i)
\Rightarrow
(iv) is evident. To prove the implication (iv)
\Rightarrow
(ii), let
T\in\mathcal{L}(\ell_{1}^{0},E)
be such that
T^{\ast}:E^{\prime}_{\beta}\to\ell_{\infty}
is
p
-convergent. Then
\{T(e_{n})\}_{n\in\omega}
is a bounded subset of
E
, and hence, by Proposition
3.6
, the space
E_{B}
is Banach, where
B
is the closed absolutely convex hull of
\{T(e_{n})\}_{n\in\omega}
. Since
T\big(B_{\ell_{1}^{0}}\big)\subseteq B_{E_{B}}
the operator
T:\ell_{1}^{0}\to E_{B}
is continuous. As
E_{B}
is complete,
T
can be extended to an operator
\bar{T}
from
\ell_{1}
to
E_{B}
. Since, by Proposition
3.6
, the topology of
E_{B}
is stronger than the original topology on
E_{B}
it follows that
\bar{T}
belongs to
\mathcal{L}(\ell_{1},E)
. Observe that
(\bar{T})^{\ast}=T^{\ast}
because
\ell_{1}^{0}
is dense in
\ell_{1}
, and hence
(\bar{T})^{\ast}
is
p
-convergent. Therefore
\bar{T}
is weakly sequentially precompact (resp., weakly sequentially compact, sequentially precompact or sequentially compact), and hence so is the operator
T
. ∎

For the weakly sequentially precompact case and the weakly sequentially compact case Theorem
14.10
can be reformulated as an operator characterization of the property
wsV^{\ast}_{p}
and the property
sV^{\ast}_{p}
, respectively.

Theorem 14.11

Let
p\in[1,\infty]
, and let
(E,\tau)
be a locally convex space. Then the following assertions are equivalent:

(i)

for every normed space
Y
, if
T\in\mathcal{L}(Y,E)
is such that the adjoint operator
T^{\ast}:E^{\prime}_{\beta}\to Y^{\prime}_{\beta}
is
p
-convergent, then
T
is weakly sequentially precompact
(
resp., weakly sequentially compact
)
;

(ii)

the same as
(i)
with
Y=\ell_{1}^{0}
;

(iii)

the space
E
has the property
wsV^{\ast}_{p}

(
resp., the property
sV^{\ast}_{p}
)
.

If additionally
E
is locally complete, then (i)-(iii) are equivalent to

(iv)

the same as
(i)
with
Y=\ell_{1}
.

The following proposition complements Theorem
14.10
.

Proposition 14.12

Let
p\in[1,\infty]
, and let
E
be a locally convex space. Then the following assertions are equivalent:

(i)

each relatively weakly sequentially
p
-compact set in
E
is a
p
-
(V^{\ast})
set;

(ii)

if
\{x_{n}\}_{n\in\omega}\subseteq E
is weakly
p
-summable and
\{\chi_{n}\}_{n\in\omega}
is weakly
p
-summable in
E^{\prime}_{\beta}
, then

\lim_{n\to\infty}\langle\chi_{n},x_{n}\rangle=0.

Proof 102

(i)
\Rightarrow
(ii) The sequence
\{x_{n}\}_{n\in\omega}
is trivially weakly sequentially
p
-compact. Therefore, by (i),
\{x_{n}\}_{n\in\omega}
is a
p
-
(V^{\ast})
set. Therefore

\lim_{n\to\infty}|\langle\chi_{n},x_{n}\rangle|\leq\lim_{n\to\infty}\sup_{k\in\omega}|\langle\chi_{n},x_{k}\rangle|=0.

(ii)
\Rightarrow
(i) Let
A
be a relatively weakly sequentially
p
-compact set in
E
, and suppose for a contradiction that
A
is not a
p
-
(V^{\ast})
set. Then there is a weakly
p
-summable sequence
\{\chi_{n}\}_{n\in\omega}
in
E^{\prime}_{\beta}
such that
\lim_{n\to\infty}\sup_{x\in A}|\langle\chi_{n},x\rangle|\not=0
. Passing to a subsequence if needed, we can assume that there are
a>0
and a sequence
\{x_{n}\}_{n\in\omega}
in
A
such that

|\langle\chi_{n},x_{n}\rangle|\geq a\quad\mbox{ for every }n\in\omega.

Since
A
is relatively weakly sequentially
p
-compact and once more passing to a subsequence, we can assume that
\{x_{n}\}_{n\in\omega}
weakly
p
-converges to some point
x\in E
. Therefore
\{x_{n}-x\}_{n\in\omega}
is weakly
p
-summable. As
\{\chi_{n}\}_{n\in\omega}
is weakly
p
-summable, it follows
\lim_{n\in\omega}\langle\chi_{n},x\rangle=0
. Therefore, by (ii), we obtain

a\leq|\langle\chi_{n},x_{n}\rangle|\leq|\langle\chi_{n},x_{n}-x\rangle|+|\langle\chi_{n},x\rangle|\to 0,

a contradiction. ∎

Let
E
and
Y
be locally convex spaces, and let
T:Y\to E
be an operator. If
B
is a
p
-
(V)
set in
E^{\prime}
, then by (iv) of Lemma
8.2
, its image
T^{\ast}(B)
is a
p
-
(V)
set in
Y^{\prime}
. Analogously to Theorems
14.1
and
14.6
it is natural to describe some classes of operators
T
for which the image
T^{\ast}(B)
has some additional (topological) properties as, for example, being a relatively (weakly) sequentially [pre]compact set. Below we obtain some necessary and sufficient conditions.

We start from the following construction of
p
-convergent operators into
\ell_{\infty}
or
c_{0}
.

Lemma 14.13

Let
E
be a locally convex space,
\{\chi_{n}\}_{n\in\omega}\subseteq E^{\prime}
be a weak
∗
bounded
(
resp., weak
∗
null
)
sequence, and let
S:E\to\ell_{\infty}

(
resp.,
S:E\to c_{0}
)
be a linear map defined by

S(x):=\big(\langle\chi_{n},x\rangle\big)_{n\in\omega}\quad(x\in E).

Then:

(i)

S
is continuous if and only if
\{\chi_{n}\}_{n\in\omega}
is equicontinuous.

(ii)

if
p\in[1,\infty]
, then
S
is
p
-convergent if and only if
\{\chi_{n}\}_{n\in\omega}
is a
p
-
(V)
set.

Proof 103

(i) Assume that
S
is continuous. Then for every
\varepsilon>0
, there is
V\in\mathcal{N}_{0}(E)
such that
S(V)\subseteq\varepsilon B_{\ell_{\infty}}
(resp.,
S(V)\subseteq\varepsilon B_{c_{0}}
). This means that
|\langle\chi_{n},x\rangle|\leq\varepsilon
for every
n\in\omega
and each
x\in V
. Thus
\{\chi_{n}\}_{n\in\omega}
is equicontinuous. Conversely, assume that
\{\chi_{n}\}_{n\in\omega}
is equicontinuous. Then there is
U\in\mathcal{N}_{0}(E)
such that
\{\chi_{n}\}_{n\in\omega}\subseteq U^{\circ}
. Then
S(U)\subseteq B_{\ell_{\infty}}
(resp.,
S(U)\subseteq B_{c_{0}}
), and hence
S
is continuous.

(ii) Assume that
S
is
p
-convergent. To show that the sequence
\{\chi_{n}\}_{n\in\omega}
is a
p
-
(V)
set, fix a weakly
p
-summable sequence
\{x_{n}\}_{n\in\omega}
in
E
. Since
S
is
p
-convergent,
S(x_{n})\to 0
in
\ell_{\infty}
. Therefore

\sup_{n\in\omega}\big|\langle\chi_{n},x_{k}\rangle\big|=\big\|S(x_{k})\big\|_{\ell_{\infty}}\to 0\quad\mbox{ as }\;\;k\to\infty,

and hence
\{\chi_{n}\}_{n\in\omega}
is a
p
-
(V)
set. Conversely, assume that
\{\chi_{n}\}_{n\in\omega}
is a
p
-
(V)
set. To show that
S
is
p
-convergent, let
\{x_{n}\}_{n\in\omega}
be a weakly
p
-summable sequence in
E
. Then, by the definition of
p
-
(V)
sets, we have

\lim_{k\to\infty}\big\|S(x_{k})\big\|_{\ell_{\infty}}=\lim_{k\to\infty}\sup_{n\in\omega}\big|\langle\chi_{n},x_{k}\rangle\big|=0

and hence
S
is
p
-convergent.∎

Now we consider an important case when
Y
is a normed space.

Proposition 14.14

Let
p\in[1,\infty]
,
E
be an
\ell_{\infty}
-
V_{p}
-barrelled space, and let
T:Y\to E
be a weakly sequentially
p
-precompact operator from a normed space
Y
to
E
. Then for every
p
-
(V)
set
B
in
E^{\prime}
, its image
T^{\ast}(B)
is relatively
(
sequentially
)
compact in the Banach space
Y^{\prime}_{\beta}
.

Proof 104

To prove that
T^{\ast}(B)
is relatively compact in the Banach space
Y^{\prime}_{\beta}
, we have to show that for every sequence
\{\chi_{n}\}_{n\in\omega}
in
B
,
\{T^{\ast}(\chi_{n})\}_{n\in\omega}
has a convergent (in
Y^{\prime}_{\beta}
) subsequence. Since
E
is
\ell_{\infty}
-
V_{p}
-barrelled and
\{\chi_{n}\}_{n\in\omega}
is a
p
-
(V)
set, the sequence
\{\chi_{n}\}_{n\in\omega}
is equicontinuous. Therefore, by Lemma
14.13
, the linear map
S:E\to\ell_{\infty}
defined by
S(x):=\big(\langle\chi_{n},x\rangle\big)_{n\in\infty}
is a
p
-convergent operator.

We show that the operator
ST:Y\to\ell_{\infty}
is compact. Indeed, let
\{x_{n}\}_{n\in\omega}\subseteq B_{Y}
. Since
T
is weakly sequentially
p
-precompact, the sequence
\big\{T(x_{n})\big\}
contains a weakly
p
-Cauchy subsequence
\big\{T(x_{n_{k}})\big\}_{k}
. As
S
is
p
-convergent, Lemma
13.4
implies that
ST(x_{n_{k}})
converges to some element of
\ell_{\infty}
. Thus
ST(B_{Y})
has compact closure in
\ell_{\infty}
, and hence
ST
is compact.

By the Schauder theorem (for its formulation, see Theorem
15.4
below), the adjoint map
T^{\ast}S^{\ast}
is also compact. Now, if
\{e^{\ast}_{n}\}
is the standard unit basis of
\ell_{1}\subseteq(\ell_{\infty})^{\prime}
, then

\langle S^{\ast}(e_{n}^{\ast}),x\rangle=\langle e_{n}^{\ast},S(x)\rangle=\langle\chi_{n},x\rangle\quad\mbox{ for every }\;x\in E,

and hence
S^{\ast}(e_{n}^{\ast})=\chi_{n}
. Therefore the sequence
\big\{T^{\ast}(\chi_{n})\big\}_{n\in\omega}=\big\{T^{\ast}S^{\ast}(e^{\ast}_{n})\big\}_{n\in\omega}
has a convergent subsequence, as desired. ∎

Corollary 14.15

Let
p\in[1,\infty]
,
E
be a locally convex space such that
E^{\prime}_{\beta}
is an
\ell_{\infty}
-
V_{p}
-barrelled space, and let
T:E\to Y
be an operator into a Banach space
Y
whose adjoint operator
T^{\ast}:Y^{\prime}_{\beta}\to E^{\prime}_{\beta}
is weakly sequentially
p
-precompact. Then for every
p
-
(V^{\ast})
set
A\subseteq E
, its image
T(A)
is relatively compact in
Y
.

Proof 105

Since
A
is a
p
-
(V^{\ast})
set, the set
J_{E}(A)
is a
p
-
(V)
set in
E^{\prime\prime}
(recall that
J_{E}:E\to E^{\prime\prime}
denotes the canonical inclusion). Therefore, by Proposition
14.14
,
T^{\ast\ast}\big(J_{E}(A)\big)
is relatively compact in the Banach space
Y^{\prime\prime}
. Since
J_{Y}
is an embedding of
Y
onto the closed subspace
J_{Y}(Y)
of
Y^{\prime\prime}
and
T^{\ast\ast}\big(J_{E}(A)\big)=J_{Y}\big(T(A)\big)\subseteq J_{Y}(Y)
, we obtain that
J_{Y}\big(T(A)\big)
is relatively compact in
J_{Y}(Y)
. Thus
T(A)
is relatively compact in
Y
.∎

Below we apply the obtained results to give necessary and sufficient conditions for subsets of
E
and
E^{\prime}
to be a
p
-
(V^{\ast})
set or a
p
-
(V)
set, respectively.
The next result generalizes Corollary 20 of
[
42
]
.

Theorem 14.16

Let
1<p<\infty
, and let
E
be a quasibarrelled space such that
E^{\prime}_{\beta}
is an
\ell_{\infty}
-
V_{p}
-barrelled space. Then for a bounded subset
A
of
E
the following assertions are equivalent:

(i)

A
is a
p
-
(V^{\ast})
set;

(ii)

T(A)
is relatively compact whenever
Y
is a Banach space and
T:E\to Y
is an operator whose adjoint operator
T^{\ast}:Y^{\prime}_{\beta}\to E^{\prime}_{\beta}
is weakly sequentially
p
-precompact;

(iii)

T(A)
is relatively compact in
\ell_{p}
for every operator
T:E\to\ell_{p}
.

Proof 106

(i)
\Rightarrow
(ii) follows from Corollary
14.15
.

(ii)
\Rightarrow
(iii) Let
T\in\mathcal{L}(E,\ell_{p})
. Then
T^{\ast}\in\mathcal{L}(\ell_{p^{\ast}},E^{\prime}_{\beta})
. By (ii) of Corollary
13.11
, the identity operator
\mathsf{id}_{\ell_{p^{\ast}}}
of
\ell_{p^{\ast}}
is weakly sequentially
p
-compact. Hence the operator
T^{\ast}=T^{\ast}\circ\mathsf{id}_{\ell_{p^{\ast}}}
is weakly sequentially
p
-precompact. Therefore, by (ii),
T(A)
is relatively compact in
\ell_{p}
.

(iii)
\Rightarrow
(i) Let
\{\chi_{n}\}_{n\in\omega}
be a weakly
p
-summable sequence in
E^{\prime}_{\beta}
. Since
E^{\prime}_{\beta}
is quasi-complete by Proposition 11.2.3 of
[
52
]
, we apply Proposition
4.14
to find an operator
S\in\mathcal{L}(\ell_{p^{\ast}},E^{\prime}_{\beta})
such that
S(e_{n}^{\ast})=\chi_{n}
for every
n\in\omega
. Set
T:=S^{\ast}\circ J_{E}
, where
J_{E}:E\to E^{\prime\prime}
is the canonical map. Since
E
is quasibarrelled,
J_{E}
is continuous, and hence
T
is an operator from
E
into
\ell_{p}
. By assumption of (iii), we obtain that
T(A)
is relatively compact in
\ell_{p}
. In particular,
by Proposition
2.10
, we have

\sup_{a\in A}|\langle\chi_{n},a\rangle|=\sup_{a\in A}|\langle J_{E}(a),S(e_{n}^{\ast})\rangle|=\sup_{a\in A}|\langle T(a),e_{n}^{\ast}\rangle|\to 0\;\mbox{ as }n\to\infty.

Thus
A
is a
p
-
(V^{\ast})
set.∎

The case
p=1
was considered by Emmanuele in
[
25
]
. For Banach spaces the next theorem was proved in Theorem 1.1 of
[
25
]
.

Theorem 14.17

Let
E
be a barrelled space. Then a bounded subset
A
of
E
is a
(V^{\ast})
set if and only if
T(A)
is relatively compact in
\ell_{1}
for every operator
T:E\to\ell_{1}
.

Proof 107

Assume that
A
is a
(V^{\ast})
set. Then, by (iv) of Lemma
7.2
,
T(A)
is a
(V^{\ast})
subset of
\ell_{1}
. Since, by Proposition 9 of
[
71
]
,
\ell_{1}
has the property
V^{\ast}
it follows that
T(A)
is relatively weakly compact. Therefore, by the Schur property,
T(A)
is a relatively compact subset of
\ell_{1}
. Conversely, assume that
T(A)
is relatively compact in
\ell_{1}
for every operator
T:E\to\ell_{1}
. Let
\{\chi_{n}\}_{n\in\omega}
be an arbitrary weakly
1
-summable sequence in
E^{\prime}_{\beta}
. Then, by Proposition
4.19
, the linear map
T:E\to\ell_{p}
defined by
T(x):=(\langle\chi_{n},x\rangle)
is continuous. By assumption,
T(A)
is relatively compact. Therefore, by Proposition
2.10
, we obtain

\lim_{n\to\infty}\sup_{a\in A}|\langle\chi_{n},x\rangle|=0.

Thus
A
is a
(V^{\ast})
set.∎

In Corollary 1.3 of
[
8
]
Bombal proved that every relatively weakly sequentially compact subset of a Banach space is a
(V^{\ast})
set. Below we generalize this result.

Corollary 14.18

Let
E
be a barrelled space. Then every weakly sequentially precompact subset
A
of
E
is a
(V^{\ast})
set.

Proof 108

Let
T:E\to\ell_{1}
be an operator. Then
T(A)
is weakly sequentially precompact in
\ell_{1}
. Since
\ell_{1}
is weakly sequentially complete and has the Schur property,
T(A)
is relatively compact in
\ell_{1}
. Thus, by Theorem
14.17
,
A
is a
(V^{\ast})
set.∎

Following
[
35
]
, we shall say that a sequence
A=\{a_{n}\}_{n\in\omega}
of an lcs
E
is
equivalent to the unit basis
\{e_{n}:n\in\omega\}
of
\ell_{1}
or is an
\ell_{1}
-sequence
if there exists a linear topological isomorphism
R
from the closure
\overline{\mathsf{span}}(A)
of
\mathsf{span}(A)
onto a subspace of
\ell_{1}
such that
R(a_{n})=e_{n}
for every
n\in\omega
(we do not assume that
\overline{\mathsf{span}}(A)
is complete). The following corollary generalizes Theorem 1.2 of
[
25
]
.

Corollary 14.19

Let
E
be a barrelled space satisfying the following condition:

(\dagger)

if a bounded subset
A
of
E
is not relatively weakly sequentially compact, then there is an
\ell_{1}
-sequence
\{a_{n}\}_{n\in\omega}\subseteq A
such that
\overline{\mathsf{span}}\big(\{a_{n}\}_{n\in\omega}\big)
is complemented in
E
.

Then
E
has the property
sV^{\ast}
.

Proof 109

Suppose for a contradiction that there is a
(V^{\ast})
subset
A
of
E
which is not relatively weakly sequentially compact. Then, by
(\dagger)
, there are a sequence
\{a_{n}\}_{n\in\omega}\subseteq A
and a linear topological isomorphism
R
from
\overline{\mathsf{span}}(A)
onto a subspace of
\ell_{1}
such that
R(a_{n})=e_{n}
for every
n\in\omega
and the subspace
\overline{\mathsf{span}}\big(\{a_{n}\}_{n\in\omega}\big)
is complemented in
E
. Let
P
be a projection from
E
onto
\overline{\mathsf{span}}\big(\{a_{n}\}_{n\in\omega}\big)
. Then
R\circ P:E\to\ell_{1}
is an operator such that
R\circ P(a_{n})=e_{n}
for every
n\in\omega
. By Theorem
14.17
, the canonical unit basis
\{e_{n}\}_{n\in\omega}
of
\ell_{1}
is relatively compact, a contradiction.∎

Corollary 14.20

Let
p\geq 1
and a locally convex space
E
satisfy one of the following conditions

(i)

1<p<2
and
E
is a quasibarrelled space such that
E^{\prime}_{\beta}
is an
\ell_{\infty}
-
V_{p}
-barrelled space,

(ii)

p=1
and
E
is barrelled.

Then every relatively weakly sequentially
p
-compact subset of
E
is a
p
-
(V^{\ast})
set.

Proof 110

Let
A
be a relatively weakly sequentially
p
-compact subset of
E
. Then, by Lemma
13.2
,
T(A)
is a relatively weakly sequentially
p
-compact subset of
\ell_{p}
for every operator
T:E\to\ell_{p}
. If
1<p<2
, we obtain
p<p^{\ast}
and hence, by Proposition
6.5
,
\ell_{p}
has the
p
-Schur property. Recall that
\ell_{1}
has the Schur property. Therefore in both cases (i) and (ii), the set
T(A)
is relatively sequentially compact in
\ell_{p}
. Thus, by Theorems
14.16
and
14.17
,
T(A)
is a
p
-
(V^{\ast})
subset of
E
.∎

Remark 14.21

The condition
1\leq p<2
in Corollary
14.20
is essential even for Banach spaces. Indeed, let
p=2
and
E=\ell_{2}
. Then the standard unit basis
S=\{e_{n}\}_{n\in\omega}
of
E
is weakly
2
-summable (indeed, if
\chi=(a_{n})\in E^{\prime}=E
, then
\sum_{n\in\omega}|\langle\chi,e_{n}\rangle|^{2}=\sum_{n\in\omega}a_{n}^{2}<\infty
). Therefore
S
is a relatively weakly sequentially
2
-compact subset of
E
. However, for
T=\mathsf{id}_{E}
, the set
T(S)=S
is not relatively compact in
E
. Thus, by Theorem
14.16
,
S
is not a
2
-
(V^{\ast})
set.∎

For the case when
Y=\ell_{p^{\ast}}^{0}
and
p\not=\infty
we can reverse Proposition
14.14
as follows.

Proposition 14.22

Let
p\in[1,\infty)
,
E
be a locally convex space, and let
B
be a subset of
E^{\prime}
. Assume that one of the following conditions holds:

(i)

T^{\ast}(B)
is relatively compact in
\ell_{p}
for every
T\in\mathcal{L}(\ell_{p^{\ast}}^{0},E)

(
or
T\in\mathcal{L}(c_{0}^{0},E)
if
p=1
)
;

(ii)

E
is sequentially complete and
T^{\ast}(B)
is relatively compact in
\ell_{p}
for every
T\in\mathcal{L}(\ell_{p^{\ast}},E)

(
or
T\in\mathcal{L}(c_{0},E)
if
p=1
)
.

Then
B
is a
p
-
(V)
set.

Proof 111

Let
\{x_{n}\}_{n\in\omega}
be a weakly
p
-summable sequence in
E
. Then, by Proposition
4.14
, there is
T\in\mathcal{L}(\ell_{p^{\ast}}^{0},E)
or
T\in\mathcal{L}(c_{0}^{0},E)
(resp.,
T\in\mathcal{L}(\ell_{p^{\ast}},E)
or
T\in\mathcal{L}(c_{0},E)
if
E
is sequentially complete) such that
T(e_{n}^{\ast})=x_{n}
for every
n\in\omega
. Since
T^{\ast}(B)
is relatively compact in
\ell_{p}
, Proposition
2.10
implies

\sup_{\chi\in B}|\langle\chi,x_{n}\rangle|=\sup_{\chi\in B}|\langle T^{\ast}(\chi),e_{n}^{\ast}\rangle|\to 0,

which means that
B
is a
p
-
(V)
set in
E^{\prime}
, as desired.∎

The next theorem generalizes Theorem 19 of
[
42
]
, for the case
1<p<\infty
, Theorem 3.10 of
[
16
]
and, for the case
p=1
, Proposition 5 of
[
19
]
.

Theorem 14.23

Let
1\leq p<\infty
, and let
E
be an
\ell_{\infty}
-
V_{p}
-barrelled space. Then for a weak
∗
bounded subset
B
of
E^{\prime}
the following assertions are equivalent:

(i)

B
is a
p
-
(V)
set;

(ii)

for every normed space
Y
and each weakly sequentially
p
-precompact operator
T:Y\to E
, the set
T^{\ast}(B)
is relatively compact in the Banach space
Y^{\prime}_{\beta}
;

(iii)

T^{\ast}(B)
is relatively compact in
\ell_{p}
for every
T\in\mathcal{L}(\ell_{p^{\ast}}^{0},E)

(
or
T\in\mathcal{L}(c_{0}^{0},E)
if
p=1
)
.

If additionally
E
is sequentially complete, then (i)-(iii) are equivalent to

(iv)

T^{\ast}(B)
is relatively compact in
\ell_{p}
for every
T\in\mathcal{L}(\ell_{p^{\ast}},E)

(
or
T\in\mathcal{L}(c_{0},E)
if
p=1
)
.

Proof 112

(i)
\Rightarrow
(ii) follows from Proposition
14.14
.

(ii)
\Rightarrow
(iii) Let
T\in\mathcal{L}(\ell_{p^{\ast}}^{0},E)
(or
T\in\mathcal{L}(c_{0}^{0},E)
if
p=1
). By (i) of Corollary
13.11
, the identity operator
\mathsf{id}_{\ell_{p^{\ast}}^{0}}
(or
\mathsf{id}_{c_{0}^{0}}
if
p=1
) is weakly sequentially
p
-precompact, and hence, by Lemma
13.2
, also
T=T\circ\mathsf{id}_{\ell_{p^{\ast}}^{0}}
(or
T=T\circ\mathsf{id}_{c^{0}_{0}}
if
p=1
) is weakly sequentially
p
-precompact. By (ii),
T^{\ast}(B)
is relatively compact in
\ell_{p}
.

(iii)
\Rightarrow
(i) follows from (i) of Proposition
14.22
.

Assume now that
E
is additionally sequentially complete.

(iii)
\Rightarrow
(iv) Let
T\in\mathcal{L}(\ell_{p^{\ast}},E)
(or
T\in\mathcal{L}(c_{0},E)
if
p=1
). Denote by
S
the restriction of
T
onto
\ell_{p^{\ast}}^{0}
(or onto
c_{0}^{0}
if
p=1
). Since
\ell_{p^{\ast}}^{0}
(resp.,
c_{0}^{0}
) is dense in
\ell_{p^{\ast}}
(resp.,
c_{0}
) it follows that
T^{\ast}=S^{\ast}
. Therefore, by (iii),
T^{\ast}(B)=S^{\ast}(B)
