is injective, the operator
i_{L}\circ{\hat{T}}:H_{p}\to\ell_{\infty}(B_{L^{\prime}})
has an extension
{\tilde{T}}:L_{p}(\mu)\to\ell_{\infty}(B_{L^{\prime}})
. Clearly,
{\tilde{T}}
is the operator we are looking for.

(iii)
\Rightarrow
(iv) Denote by
J^{\infty}
the identity mapping from
C(K)
to
L_{\infty}(\mu)
. Set
I_{U}^{\infty}:=J^{\infty}\circ I_{U}
. Now it is clear from the diagram of (iii) that the diagram in (iv) is commutative.

(iv)
\Rightarrow
(i) By Example 2.9(d) of
[
24
]
, the operator
J^{\infty}_{p}
is
B_{L_{\infty}(\mu)}
-
p
-summing. Therefore, by Theorem
17.10
and the commutativity of the diagram in (iv), the operator
i_{L}\circ T
is
V
-
p
-summing for
V:=\big((I_{U}^{\infty})^{\ast}\big(B^{\circ}_{L_{\infty}(\mu)}\big)\big)^{\circ}
. Since
i_{L}
is an isometry, Theorem
17.12
implies that also
T
is
V
-
p
-summing. To show that
V
is a neighborhood of zero, observe that
B^{\circ}_{L_{\infty}(\mu)}
is equicontinuous and hence so is
(I_{U}^{\infty})^{\ast}\big(B^{\circ}_{L_{\infty}(\mu)}\big)\subseteq E^{\prime}
. Thus
V\in\mathcal{N}_{0}^{c}(E)
, as desired. ∎

It is well known that every
p
-summing operator between Banach spaces is weakly compact and completely continuous, see Theorem 2.17 of
[
24
]
. The next theorem extends this result.

Theorem 17.16

Let
1\leq p<\infty
, and let
T:E\to L
be an operator from a locally convex space
E
to a Banach space
L
. If
T
is
U
-
p
-summing for some
U\in\mathcal{N}_{0}^{c}(E)
, then it is weakly
(
sequentially
)
compact and completely continuous.

Proof 140

By Theorem
17.11
applied to
p_{1}=q_{1}=p
and
p_{2}=q_{2}=q
, we can assume that
p>1
. By (iii) of the Pietsch Factorization Theorem
17.15
, we have
i_{L}\circ T={\tilde{T}}\circ J_{p}\circ I_{U}
. Since
L_{p}(\mu)
is reflexive, all bounded subsets of
L_{p}(\mu)
are relatively weakly (sequentially) compact. Therefore the operator
J_{p}
and hence also
i_{L}\circ T={\tilde{T}}\circ J_{p}\circ I_{U}
are weakly (sequentially) compact. It is well known that the canonical map
J_{p}:C(K)\to L_{p}(\mu)
is completely continuous, and hence so is
i_{L}\circ T={\tilde{T}}\circ J_{p}\circ I_{U}
. Taking into account that
i_{L}
is an isometric embedding, we obtain that
T
is weakly (sequentially) compact and completely continuous.∎

Since, by Proposition
4.10
,
E
is a direct summand of
\ell_{p}[E]
it follows that the barrelledness of
\ell_{p}[E]
implies that
E
is a barrelled space as well. As the space
E_{w}
is not barrelled if
E\not=E_{w}
, the next proposition shows that the barrelledness of
\ell_{p}[E]
in (ii) of Proposition
17.5
is not a necessary condition.

Proposition 17.17

Let
1\leq p\leq q<\infty
,
E
be a locally convex space, and let
L
be a Banach space. Then every operator
T:E_{w}\to L
is
U
-
(q,p)
-summing for some
U\in\mathcal{N}_{0}^{c}(E_{w})
.

To prove Proposition
17.17
we need the next simple lemma.

Lemma 17.18

Let
E
be a locally convex space such that
E=E_{w}
, and let
L
be a normed space. Then every
T\in\mathcal{L}(E,L)
is finite-dimensional.

Proof 141

Observe that
T
can be extended to an operator
{\bar{T}}
from a completion
{\bar{E}}
of
E
to a completion
{\bar{L}}
of
L
. As
E
carries its weak topology, we obtain
{\bar{E}}=\mathbb{F}^{\kappa}
for some cardinal
\kappa
. Since
{\bar{T}}
is continuous, there is a finite subset
\lambda
of
\kappa
such that
{\bar{T}}\big(\{0\}^{\lambda}\times\mathbb{F}^{\kappa{\setminus}\lambda}\big)
is contained in the unit ball
B_{{\bar{L}}}
of
{\bar{L}}
. Taking into account that
B_{{\bar{L}}}
contains no non-trivial linear subspaces we obtain that
\{0\}^{\lambda}\times\mathbb{F}^{\kappa{\setminus}\lambda}
is contained in the kernel
\ker({\bar{T}})
of
{\bar{T}}
. Therefore
{\bar{T}}[\mathbb{F}^{\kappa}]={\bar{T}}[\mathbb{F}^{\lambda}]
is finite-dimensional. Thus also
T
is finite-dimensional.∎

Proof 142
(Proof of Proposition
17.17
)

By Lemma
17.18
,
T
is finite-dimensional. Therefore there are
\chi_{0},\dots,\chi_{m}\in E^{\prime}
and
y_{0},\dots,y_{m}\in L
such that
T(x)=\sum_{j=0}^{m}\langle\chi_{j},x\rangle y_{j}
. Set
U:=\{\chi_{0},\dots,\chi_{m}\}^{\circ}
, so
U\in\mathcal{N}_{0}^{c}(E_{w})
. We show that
T
is
U
-
(q,p)
-summing. To this end, by Proposition
17.8
(i), it suffices to prove that each operator
S_{j}:E\to L
defined by
S_{j}(x)=\langle\chi_{j},x\rangle y_{j}
is
U
-
(q,p)
-summing. Set
C:=\max\{\|y_{0}\|,\dots,\|y_{m}\|\}
. Then for every
n\in\omega
and each each
x_{0},\dots,x_{n}\in U
, we obtain

\Big(\sum_{i=0}^{n}\|S_{j}(x_{i})\|^{q}\Big)^{1/q}\leq\Big(\sum_{i=0}^{n}\|S_{j}(x_{i})\|^{p}\Big)^{1/p}\leq C\cdot\Big(\sum_{i=0}^{n}|\langle\chi_{j},x_{i}\rangle|^{p}\Big)^{1/p}\leq C\cdot\sup_{\chi\in U^{\circ}}\Big(\sum_{i=0}^{n}|\langle\chi,x_{i}\rangle|^{p}\Big)^{1/p}

which means that
S_{j}
is
U
-
(q,p)
-summing.∎

As an application of the obtained results we shall show that each
(p,p)
-
(V^{\ast})
subset of a Banach space is relatively weakly compact.

Theorem 17.19

If
1\leq p<\infty
, then every Banach space
E
has the property
V^{\ast}_{(p,p)}
.

Proof 143

Let
A
be a
(p,p)
-
(V^{\ast})
subset of
E
. Then, by Theorem
16.11
, the adjoint operator
T_{A}^{\ast}:E^{\prime}_{\beta}\to\ell_{\infty}(A)
is
(p,p)
-convergent. Therefore, by Corollary
17.7
,
T_{A}^{\ast}
is
p
-summing. Now we apply Theorem
17.16
to get that
T_{A}^{\ast}
is weakly compact. Then, by the Gantmacher Theorem
15.5
, the operator
T_{A}:\ell_{1}(A)\to E
is also weakly compact. Therefore
A=T_{A}(A)
is a relatively weakly compact subset of
E
. Thus
E
has the property
V^{\ast}_{(p,p)}
.∎

Remark 17.20

The condition
p<\infty
in Theorem
17.19
is essential. Indeed, let
E=c_{0}
. We show that
B_{c_{0}}
is an
(\infty,\infty)
-
(V^{\ast})
set. Fix a weakly
\infty
-summable sequence
\{\chi_{n}\}_{n\in\omega}\in(c_{0})^{\prime}=\ell_{1}
. By the Schur property, we have
\|\chi_{n}\|\to 0
. Therefore
\sup\{|\langle\chi_{n},x\rangle|:x\in B_{c_{0}}\}=\|\chi_{n}\|\to 0
, and hence
B_{c_{0}}
is an
(\infty,\infty)
-
(V^{\ast})
set. On the other hand, since the sequence
\{e_{0}+\cdots+e_{n}\}_{n\in\omega}\subseteq B_{c_{0}}
has no cluster points in the weak topology,
B_{c_{0}}
is not relatively weakly compact. Thus
c_{0}
does not have the property
V^{\ast}_{(\infty,\infty)}
.∎

Below we give another application of the obtained results, cf. Proposition 2.1 of
[
23
]
.

Theorem 17.21

Let
1\leq p\leq q<\infty
, and let
E
be a locally convex space such that
\ell_{p}[E^{\prime}_{\beta}]
is barrelled. Then every
(p,p)
-
(V^{\ast})
subset of
E
is also a
(q,q)
-
(V^{\ast})
set.

Proof 144

Let
A
be a
(p,p)
-
(V^{\ast})
subset of
E
. Recall that Theorem
16.11
states that a bounded subset
A
of
E
is a
(p,p)
-
(V^{\ast})
set if and only if the adjoint operator
T_{A}^{\ast}:E^{\prime}_{\beta}\to\ell_{\infty}(A)
is
(p,p)
-convergent. Since
\ell_{p}[E^{\prime}_{\beta}]
is barrelled, Corollary
17.7
implies that
T_{A}^{\ast}
is
U
-
(p,p)
-summing for some
U\in\mathcal{N}_{0}^{c}(E)
. By the inclusion property Theorem
17.11
, the operator
T_{A}^{\ast}
is also
U
-
(q,q)
-summing, and hence, by Proposition
17.5
,
T_{A}^{\ast}
is
(q,q)
-convergent. Once more applying Theorem
16.11
we obtain that
A
is a
(q,q)
-
(V^{\ast})
set.∎
